# Joey64

Standalone Android N64 emulator built on the **mupen64plus-next** libretro core,
focused on handheld devices (Retroid, Odin, AYN, Anbernic).

## Status
Runs games on real handhelds (Retroid Pocket 3.5 Flip, Odin 2 Portal, Retroid Pocket Duo, Odin 3)
with parallel-RDP on Vulkan (bundled Turnip on Adreno) or GLideN64. No menus yet: games are started
over adb (see CLAUDE.md).

## Layout
```
app/src/main/java/.../MainActivity.kt   Surface + lifecycle, picks core/ROM
app/src/main/java/.../NativeBridge.kt   JNI bindings
app/src/main/cpp/libretro_host.cpp      dlopen core, environment callbacks, run loop
app/src/main/cpp/egl_context.cpp/.h     EGL/GLES3 context + FBO for the core
app/src/main/cpp/audio_out.cpp/.h       AAudio output
app/src/main/cpp/libretro.h             Download from libretro/libretro-common (see below)
```

## Getting the core
Build our core, Joey64 (the private repo JoeysRetroHandhelds/joey64-core, a fork of
https://github.com/libretro/mupen64plus-libretro-nx), then run `devtools/bundle-core.sh`: it copies it to
`app/src/main/jniLibs/arm64-v8a/libjoey64_libretro_android.so` and records the core commit in `core-version.txt`.

Copy `libretro.h` from https://github.com/libretro/libretro-common/blob/master/include/libretro.h
into `app/src/main/cpp/`.

## Licensing
Joey64 is GPL-3.0-or-later (`LICENSE`, decided 2026-11-05). The core's own code is GPLv2+, but the core we build also compiles in GNU lightning (LGPLv3+, the parallel-RSP JIT) and GLideN64's xBRZ (GPLv3), and the launcher's Compose/AndroidX libraries are Apache-2.0, so the app as shipped can only be GPLv3. GPLv3 code (gopher64, RMG, FZ, hakuX) may be copied; GPLv2-only code (Project64) may not, ideas only. Keep ares-derived code under its ISC notice. angrylion (old MAME licence, non-commercial; Joey64 never used it) is out of the core since a8ab829 (2026-11-07, Joey's go): its source is deleted and HAVE_THR_AL is never set; SM64 checked on the Thor on both renderers at 60 fps. The app lists every other part and its licence text in Settings › About › Open-source licences (2026-11-07). Found while building that list, for Joey to decide: GLideN64's LICENSE says "the GNU General Public License version 2" with no "or later", and its source files carry no licence notices, so it may be GPLv2-only, which the GPLv3 reasoning above assumes it isn't (the core's own files do say "or any later version"). Ways out: ask GLideN64's author, or treat GLideN64 like angrylion (out of the build) before a public release. Not changed yet. Joey 2026-11-07: leave it for now; decide before the first public release.

## Roadmap
Focus now: performance, compatibility and latency; menus come after.

- [x] M0 – Host core, load ROM, GLES video, AAudio, run loop
- [x] M1 – Input: positional gamepad mapping, analog sticks, fast-forward; unbatched stick input on Android 11 too (2026-11-06)
- [x] M2 – Vulkan HW context for parallel-RDP, Turnip/system driver pick per GPU
- [ ] M3 – Per-game / per-GPU profile DB (ROM hash + GPU vendor/driver → renderer + core options); a few rules are hard-coded so far. Everything known per game (settings, open problems per device, fixes) is in `gamedb/games.json` (rules in `gamedb/README.md`), started 2026-11-05 and kept current with every game change
- [~] M4 – Frame pacing: big-core pinning, audio sync, low-latency audio, timed pacing on 120 Hz (opt-in) done; speed cap when audio doesn't pace done too (app 0985fb0: New Tetris 60 fps on 120 Hz, was 121); thermal fallback open
- [ ] M5 – Handheld frontend: game list, settings, controller profiles, controller-only UI
- [ ] M6 – Test harness: compatibility runs across a ROM library per device (routine compat set, Joey 2026-11-09 "Yes": in 40 full 82-game runs every build ended 82/82 running except an unkept dynarec-thread experiment, and blackcheck over all of them flags one game twice (Tony Hawk 3, those experimental Flip builds); only about 15-18 games have ever had an issue, most found by screenshot reviews or targeted checks. So routine compat runs 34 games: every game that has ever had an issue, the 14 bench games, and a 2D game, a Transfer Pak game and the review finds (scratchpad compat/compat-set.txt, one line per game with why; SET=compat-set.txt sh compat64c.sh <device> <tag> -). The full 82 stays for big changes: renderer or driver switches, core merges, state/save paths; a game that shows a new issue joins the set.) (scripted, first pass done), n64-systemtest results, Pokémon Snap photo check. n64-systemtest crashes the core first (dynarec: "Compile at bogus memory address b0001040" on code run from cart ROM; the cached interpreter crashes elsewhere): fix that before using its results
- [ ] Paks: Controller Pak works (player 1, saved in the .srm). Rumble Pak works too (app 0985fb0; 33 ms pulses in Star Fox 64), still to do: pick memory or rumble pak per game, Transfer Pak (Game Boy ROM + save for Pokémon Stadium 1/2; the core supports it through libretro's Transfer Pak subsystem, which the app doesn't load yet, and has upstream's bank-mirroring and MBC3 clock fixes since core c052db1/f4e3041, 2026-11-06), paks for players 2-4
- [ ] Save states, then run-ahead (removes games' built-in input lag). Save states are built (launcher Phase 3, 2026-11-06: slots, auto, recovery, RetroArch's format; audit 2026-11-07); run-ahead and the lag test below are what's left. Started: dev-only loading (`N64_STATE=<raw file>`, 30 frames after boot, for benchmarks); no saving, slots or UI yet. RetroArch states import cleanly once unpacked: RZIP (zlib chunks) around a RASTATE container whose MEM chunk is the core's `M64+SAVE` data; Joey's states from RetroArch's Mupen64Plus-Next load in our core (OoT and SM64 checked so far; the benchmark logs each load). So the app can read RetroArch .state files directly Then the lag test from docs/latency-plan.md (from a state, run K frames with and without a press; the first frame that differs is the game's own lag) to set run-ahead per game in the profile DB.
- [x] Performance on weak devices: parallel-RDP command-ring syscalls, NEON RSP vector path (core 9bad896, b504751, 502d881)

### Current focus: performance, compatibility, latency (revisit as they come up)
- [ ] Crash recovery: a core crash (e.g. Elise's freeze or a dynarec crash; Last Impact's ERET crash was fixed 2026-11-03, core 22aa47a) shouldn't take the app down; keep saves safe, show an error
- [ ] Core emulation fixes for what M6 finds (Banjo-Tooie music, OoT pause freezes; Last Impact's ERET error fixed 2026-11-03 by the PI DMA bit-0 rule, core 22aa47a); decide how to track upstream (the `gliden64-update` merge into `handheld` is done). Checked 2026-11-04: nothing watches the projects the core is built from. libretro core: we have everything up to 2026-10-22. GLideN64: we merged gonetz master on 2026-11-02, up to date. parallel-RSP: up to date (9ab776a). mupen64plus-core: the six upstream fixes libretro's copy lacked are now in our core (01d92a9, 09c9871: PIF/netplay/savestate/64DD bounds, Game Boy RAM files keep trailing data). parallel-RDP: our copy is from Dec 2022, and parallel-rdp-standalone has 18 updates since then (last one Nov 2024) that no libretro build has. Daily upstream check: `devtools/check-upstreams.py` + `.github/workflows/upstream-updates.yml` (sources in devtools/upstreams.json, with libretro parallel-n64's parallel-RDP folder first, as Joey asked): one open issue per source, a comment per day with only the new commits; runs once pushed. parallel-RDP pull: merged on core branch `prdp-update` (e35678f, worktree C:\GithubProjects\joey64-core-prdp; one glue fix), builds and runs; the first launch after it recompiles every shader (Flip OoT 109 fps cold vs 139 warm, same as the old copy once warm); Flip 14 games, warm round only (round 1 and 2 agree within a few %): +7.9% geomean; Smash Bros. 97 -> 133 (+37%), Yoshi's Story +22%, GoldenEye +19%, Mario Kart 64 +14%, Paper Mario +14%, Banjo-Kazooie +11%, Perfect Dark +10%, SM64 +8%, Snap +3%, OoT +2%; losses Conker -7%, Cruis'n USA -5%, DK64 -4%, Majora's Mask -4%. On the Duo and Odin 3 every game crashed at start: the new Granite asks for host query reset on our Vulkan 1.1 instance but never enabled the extension, so vkResetQueryPool was null (the Flip's driver hands it over anyway). Fixed in core bf44e45 (enable VK_EXT_host_query_reset, and never call a null entry point); OoT runs on the Duo. Odin 3 with the fix, 14 games x 2: +0.3% geomean, no real change (Paper Mario +3.2%, Snap +2.9%, OoT/Yoshi/Smash +1.7-1.8%; DK64 -6.0% comes from one slow round, 524 vs 593, Perfect Dark -2.2% likewise; the Odin 3 is not GPU-bound at 450-1500 fps); so the update helps the Flip and costs nothing on the Odin 3. Duo full compat run running; the losses get a look after (likely one of the 18 upstream changes). After that, port parallel-n64's parallel-RDP speed changes (list in emulator-review.md: page-diff RDRAM upload, in-place primitive streams, VI image pool, plain-store readback, pipeline pre-build), one at a time with a bench each. Merged into gpu2 2026-11-05 09:1x (Joey: 'Merge it'; core 023dd9e: merge 5cc1c9d plus one fix, the N64_RDP_WAVE switch reads vk13_features/vk13_props in the new Granite); app-gpu8.apk = app 25db539 + this core; Portal smoke test SM64 60 fps, picture right. gpu2 now has parallel-RDP at upstream's latest (1cecd042, 2024-11-09; upstream has not changed since). Queued on the Flip after its NEON run (`chain41_flip_gpu8.sh`): quick set old vs new (`flip-gpu8`) and a full compat run on it (`flip-gpu8c`). Flip result (2026-11-05, uncapped, 14 games x 2, both rounds within 1% except Conker): the new parallel-RDP is +9.6% (geomean), the biggest gain since repeat frames: Smash +32.4% (132 vs 100 fps), Yoshi's Story +24.9% (200 vs 160), GoldenEye +19.7% (137 vs 115), Paper Mario +15.1%, Perfect Dark and SM64 +11.3%, Banjo +8.8%, Snap +8.2%; Cruis'n USA, Majora's Mask and OoT flat (-1.3% to -0.3%), none worse. The Mali sits idle less with it (88% vs 84% GPU busy). Slowest frames about the same (MK64's compile burst and Conker/Perfect Dark's 100-140 ms boot frames in both). Compat on the new core (`flip-gpu8c`, 82 games): 82/82 by the script's checks (pictures not looked at by hand). Correction 2026-11-05: by brightness and drawn frames, 2 games end black on the Flip that draw on the Odin 3 and Duo: Spider-Man (50) and Yoshi's Story (79); the script's checks pass a game that goes black after its first screenshots (Flip black screens item below). Duo result (2026-11-05, `duo-gpu8`, 14 games x 2, uncapped): no change. Runs at the same Duo speed level match within ~1% in every game (Banjo-Kazooie 260/260 vs 261/262, Majora's Mask 262/261 vs 261/262, Mario Kart 64 375/379 vs 379/378, Conker 151/153 vs 153/153); the large gaps in the table are the Duo's two speed levels, not the core: one new-core run each in GoldenEye (329 vs 261), Smash (368 vs 239) and Pokemon Snap (720 vs 479) landed in the fast level, and one old-core run each in Paper Mario (330 vs 419) and Yoshi's Story (389 vs 544) in the slow one; Cruis'n USA has one slow-level run on each core (475/360 vs 480/360). So the new parallel-RDP costs nothing on the Adreno 722 and gains +9.6% on the Flip; Duo compat on it (`duo-gpu8c`) done 2026-11-05 16:1x: 82/82 ok by the script's checks, and `blackcheck.py` against duo-prdp and duo-faux-lazy finds no game that ends black. Screenshots against duo-prdp (`results/duo-gpu8c-vs-prdp.png`, the 12 biggest differences looked at): all are the same game reached at a different moment (WWF War Zone and Mario Tennis on other attract/menu screens or mid-fade, Conker's first shot one moment before the Rare logo, Smash Remix with its PRESS START blink); nothing drawn wrong. So the merged core passes on the Duo too, the Adreno 722's first full run on it. Build note: ndk-build's make crashes (segfault, no output) when the tree sits under the long scratchpad path; worktrees go in C:\GithubProjects
- [ ] 50 Hz (PAL) games on 120 Hz panels (PAL-only games and RetroAchievements sets that only support PAL): no 50/100 Hz or VRR modes on our devices, so use timed pacing to show frames in a fixed 2,2,3,2,3 refresh pattern (5 frames per 12 refreshes, 17/25 ms). Optional frame blending later. Not 60 Hz mode (17/33 ms) or running at 60 fps
- [ ] Never lose progress when Android kills the app (audit 2026-11-07: save states, the auto save on quit with Continue and the recovery state are built in the launcher; still open: a state on going to the background, the "tap to resume" notification, holding the last frame over the resume): savestates first (retro_serialize on the emu thread; Lemuroid's notes: restore only after the first frame is rendered, retry a failed unserialize, detect states from another core version), then a temporary state on background/process death that resumes on relaunch (Dolphin), auto-save on exit + quick resume (flycast, Lemuroid, with a thumbnail), and a foreground service with a "tap to resume" notification while a game runs (Eden) Also (research audit 2026-11-04): hold the last frame on screen over the resume handoff so there is no black flash (GammaOS); keep states loadable across our own core updates with a versioned state format (hakuX).
- [ ] Performance hints (ADPF, Android 13+, from PPSSPP): report each frame's CPU time so the scheduler stops parking the emu thread on slow cores/clocks between frames; experiment `N64_PERF_HINT=1` built; Odin 3: same medians, worst frame in Peach's Fury 9.2 -> 8.4 ms (both rounds), Duo, clean 3-round A/B (2026-11-04): no gain (GoldenEye run 9.3 base / 8.1 hint / 9.1 turbo ms, worst frames 11.6 / 11.5 / 11.8; Peach's Fury 11.4 / 13.1 / 12.0, rounds vary by +-1.5 ms on their own); not possible on the Flip (Android 11, ADPF needs 13). Decide: remove, unless a Portal check shows a gain
- [x] Force max GPU clocks on Adreno (Dolphin, libadrenotools `adrenotools_set_turbo`): experiment `N64_GPU_TURBO=1` built (Odin 3: GoldenEye 3.0 -> 2.8 ms/frame, Peach's Fury no change, both already at 60 fps); Duo: no gain either (same A/B as the perf hint); weigh against heat and battery. Decide: remove, or keep as a per-game "performance" option Removed 2026-11-05 (Joey: "remove"): mode 1 (`N64_GPU_TURBO=1`, adrenotools max clocks) is gone from the app. The KGSL clock pin (`N64_GPU_TURBO=2`, gpu_clock_pin.cpp) stays as a dev switch, because the Portal slow mode (GPU stuck at 220 MHz, see the Portal GPU clock item) ended during a pin run and that still needs checking
- [x] Turnip GPU priority above the compositor (hakuX): hook the driver's KGSL draw-context creation and set priority 7, so SurfaceFlinger's GPU composition stops preempting our frames (hakuX: GPU hangs 4-9 per 6 min -> 0, 2 late frames); write our own hook (their patch is GPL-3) and measure late frames with HW overlays off Done 2026-11-07: priority 1 on the Odin 3 only, see T4.
- [x] HLE audio with LLE graphics (core, now on by default; `N64_HLE_AUDIO=0` turns it off): Joey listened on the Odin 3 (2026-11-04) to OoT, Majora's Mask, SM64, Banjo-Kazooie and Conker, no difference heard. Speed, uncapped: Flip Majora's Mask +14-19%, Paper Mario +2%, others 0 (GPU-bound); Flip 14 games +3.2% geomean (Majora's Mask 94 -> 115, Mario Kart 64 +13%, DK64 +7%, Banjo-Kazooie and Conker +4%, others about 0); Portal +2-6% (measured without lazy waking). Odin 3 compat run with it (`odin3-hlea`): 82/82, so it is on by default since core bd3cd23 (2026-11-04). The app's bundled core does not have it yet: rebundle (open item below) once the parallel-RDP update, inline RSP JIT and lazy waking are decided
- [ ] Rebundle the app's core (devtools/bundle-core.sh) with HLE audio on and whatever of the parallel-RDP update / ports / inline RSP JIT / lazy waking is kept; install on all devices
- [ ] Volume differs between games (Joey: Majora's Mask seemed louder than SM64): no normalization, each game mixes at its own level as on hardware. Check HLE vs LLE output level on the same save spot; maybe a per-game volume setting
- [ ] Fast-forward stutters in heavy games (Conker on the Odin 3: 110-240 fps instead of a steady 240; OoT smooth). Pace fast-forward evenly at a speed the device holds (e.g. drop to 3x/2x) instead of running flat out. Fast-forward is fixed at 4x today: add a speed setting (2x/3x/4x/unlimited, as most emulators have), and step down automatically when a game cannot hold it
- [ ] Audio: exclusive AAudio stream is on by default (Duo 14-16 -> 10 ms, Odin 3 14 -> 9 ms device latency; the Flip refuses it and stays shared); the game usage tag (PPSSPP) made no difference and was removed. Still open: pause on audio focus loss, and lighter settings in battery saver (PPSSPP)
- [x] Present thread (Azahar's async presentation) so the emu thread never blocks in acquire/present; measure on the Flip, where poll->display is highest. Already built and measured 2026-11-03: `N64_PRESENTER=1-4` (app e177ae5): Flip dip ~55 fps instead of 41-50 but +8-13 ms lag; the lockstep variants lag less but drop to 42-53 fps; no change on the Duo or Portal. Kept as a dev switch. Rechecked 2026-11-05 on the current build (`flip-dip22`, Mario Tennis): the dip without it is now 51.7-56.3 fps in one window and the present thread gives the same 55.3-55.6 for ~20 ms more poll->display, so it buys nothing now until this item is decided Dropped 2026-11-05 (Joey: "drop"): `N64_PRESENTER` and its staging images and thread are removed from vk_context (app, 262 lines)
- [ ] Automatic controller mapping (Joey, 2026-11-04: "do this work properly"). Odin 3 problems found: N64 A/B on the wrong buttons, and forward on the stick only walked. Causes (getevent): the Odin 3 has Nintendo labels and sends Android codes by label (A right = BUTTON_A), while the app assumed Xbox positions; its stick reaches 77% forward, 93% back. Done (`Controllers.kt`): profiles by USB vendor:product (Odin 3 = 2020:0111, Nintendo labels), mapping by position (bottom = N64 A, left = N64 B, right = C-down, top = C-up), stick calibration that learns each direction's reach per device (starts at the profile's guess, saved per InputDevice descriptor) and clamps to a circle. Joey on the Odin 3, OoT: "seems perfect". Left: profiles for the Duo, Portal, Flip and Thor (check each with getevent: label vs position codes, stick reach), a press-to-bind remap screen per controller (Lemuroid/PPSSPP/Dolphin style), RetroArch's Android autoconfig profiles for other pads (check the licence), an optional "rotate the stick" calibration step and a range setting (see the stick item below)
- [ ] Analog stick range and calibration per device (real N64 sticks don't reach as far as modern sticks, so movement feels too sensitive unless scaled)
- [ ] Battery and heat: measure power use, thermal fallback (M4)
- [ ] Timing accuracy: no emulator matches N64 timing exactly (ares is closest), so scenes that slowed down on hardware run faster and a few games built around that slowdown misbehave. Compare game speed against ares on known cases, tune per game (CountPerOp) in the profile DB
- [ ] Games that need the accurate path: custom microcodes (Factor 5: Rogue Squadron, Battle for Naboo, Indiana Jones) and framebuffer effects (Snap photos, blur/motion effects) break on GLideN64; the profile DB should keep them on parallel-RDP + LLE RSP, or warn on weak devices that fall back

### Open items (found, not yet done or not yet checked; audit 2026-11-04)
- [x] Audit 2026-11-04 fixes: (1) parallel-RDP at 1x everywhere is by design: the default is always native, upscaling is the user's choice; (2) libjoey64host now builds at -O2: on the Flip its share of all CPU fell from 5-9% to 1-1.7% (OoT, GoldenEye, MK64, 2 rounds), emulation-thread frame time unchanged, so the gain is CPU/heat, not fps; (3) fast-forward keeps sync for RE2, New Tetris, Cruis'n USA and Yoshi's Story (`g_ffKeepSync`): Odin 3 check, all four run 240 fps in fast-forward, draw correctly and return to 60 fps after (the old build also drew these menu scenes fine, so this shows the fix is safe, not that the menus needed it). Six stale items closed
- [ ] Head-to-head vs RetroArch (stock upstream core, local bench build `com.retroarch.bench`) and mupen64plus-ae: same ROMs, keys and settings, measured the same way (process/thread CPU via run-as, displayed fps from SurfaceFlinger). First Duo try, GoldenEye GLideN64 in-game: both 60 fps; ours 332 ms/s CPU, RetroArch 270. The gap was our UI thread polling the Rumble Pak every vsync (120 Hz on the Duo, slow in a debug build): now a thread waits for motor changes instead, ours 290/276 ms/s vs RetroArch 266 (rumble checked in Star Fox 64: 33 ms pulses reach the vibrator). Our emulation thread alone is lighter than RetroArch's single thread (218-227 vs 238-241 ms/s). The rest was Qualcomm's frame pacing library (`libpenguin_impl.so`, `vendor.perf.framepacing.enable=1` on the Duo, Odin 3 and Portal; not on the Flip): it hooks SurfaceView layers of games and ran a 120 Hz vsync loop in our process (17-18 ms/s). We now render into the activity window like RetroArch's NativeActivity (`window.takeSurface`; FPS counter in its own panel window): loop gone, Duo 256-260 ms/s. No-input GoldenEye (same frames in both apps, 40-60 s after boot), Duo: ours 257/260 vs RetroArch 272/274/272; Portal: ours 251-256 vs RetroArch ~3,060 (eight near-busy threads, fps dips to 57; that RetroArch number is not trustworthy, see below). RetroArch shows a black picture on the Odin 3 with GLideN64 as well as parallel-RDP (stock Adreno 830 driver). Ocarina of Time from a copy of Joey's save (`ootab.sh`: title, file 1, measured at the Temple of Time spawn, screenshots match in every run), GLideN64: Duo ours 213/212 ms/s vs RetroArch 241/230; Portal ours 213/207 vs RetroArch 291/306; all at 60 fps. Getting RetroArch there: N64 started the way a frontend starts it needs Polling Behavior = Early (`input_poll_type_behavior = 0`) or OoT ignores input (Joey); on top of that this RetroArch build ignores every adb-injected key (even F1 for the menu), so the bench presses buttons over RetroArch's network remote (UDP 55400, `rapad.py`). The bench now also uses its own config/remap folders with auto remaps/overrides off (it was reading Joey's real `/sdcard/RetroArch` config), touch overlay off. With those changes RetroArch's Portal CPU fell from ~2,850 to ~300 ms/s, so the earlier Portal RetroArch numbers were inflated by that setup; which setting caused it is not checked yet. mupen64plus-ae launch is scripted (`ae.sh`: the VIEW intent only adds the game to its library and gives a black screen; starting it from the library works; ae needs the notification permission). First ae run on the Odin 3 reached gameplay, but its numbers are not trustworthy yet (68 ms/s CPU, last fps sample 9): check the process measured and whether the key presses paused it. Still to run: Flip and Odin 3, parallel-RDP, more games

- [x] Unbatched input on Android 11 (Flip): stick events arrive ~13 ms later than buttons because Android batches them to vsync. Joey said go 2026-11-06. The 11-03 plan (`Window.takeInputQueue`) would not help: Android 11's ViewRootImpl feeds the InputQueue from the same batched receiver. The real cause: our `requestUnbufferedDispatch` call in onCreate runs before the window exists, and the window only takes the request through a call made while attached; dumpsys showed the Flip's window with `mUnbufferedInputSource=0` (the Portal's showed 10, joystick). The request is made again once attached and on each focus gain, behind `--es env N64_UNBUF_LATE=1` until measured; `N64_INPUT_LAT=1` logs kernel-event-to-dispatch ms for stick moves and key presses every 5 s. Queued on the Flip after its texture pack run (chain94, unbuf.sh: injected stick moves and A presses through the pad's event node, old vs new, 4 rounds); if moves drop to key-press delay and the window shows 10, it becomes the default. Result (2026-11-06, `results/unbuf/flip1`): it works. Stick moves reach the app 1.55-1.71 ms after the kernel event on average (max 11-16 ms) instead of 15.2-15.9 ms (max 25-38), the same as A presses (2.4-2.8 ms). Window source 10 instead of 0, in all 4 rounds, about 340 moves each, no crashes. Flip compat with it on (`flip-unbufcompat`, chain100): 82/82 ok, nothing went black, differences only the same scenes a moment apart. 98-99% of looks stop at the spread rule (the last 8 event-time PCs in at most two 512-byte regions, which almost never holds since the interrupt handler and woken threads run between events), so only 2-20 traces ran, failing on ops the tracer doesn't take (MFC0 Status in libultra's interrupt disable, CTC1); looser rule built: core idlespread f023dbf tries a trace when the pc's region came up twice in the last 7 looks, Portal rerun (`chain188`, `portal-sp1`): spread now stops only 1-12% of looks and 123-2197 traces run per test, still 0 loops: every trace stops at a load outside RDRAM (Paper Mario/OoT `lw a0,0x10(t6)`, MK64 `lw v0,0(v0)`), so the loops read hardware registers or TLB-mapped memory; addresses (`chain190`, core idleaddr 06e392a): all three read SP_STATUS (0xA4040010); core idlesp 3e8d87e lets a trace read it (changed only by CPU writes and RSP events), Portal (`chain191`, `portal-sp2`): still 0 loops, the traces now stop at a store to SP_MEM_ADDR (0xA4040000), i.e. the scheduler starting an RSP DMA, so the event-time looks land in task start-up, not in a wait loop; uncapped fps unchanged (612/645/843 against 616/639/840); perf-map time profile of Paper Mario on the Portal, uncapped (`portal-pm2`, profbench6.sh: cpu-clock, the Portal refuses task-clock for an app record): the dynarec's blocks are 13% of samples and the hottest are big FPU blocks (8005c49c 194 ops 1.5%, 8005be84 390 ops 0.8%, 8005c7a4 331 ops 0.8%); no small block takes more than 0.12%, so this state spends no time spinning in a wait loop (the dynarec already handles loops that jump to themselves): **nothing left to skip here, item 18 stops** (the skip stays off by default; the debug branches idlespread/idleaddr/idlesp stay unmerged)); (19) threaded RSP (Project64 "Rsp Multi Threaded", master item 10; 2026-11-07: armN64's deterministic windowed RSP thread is the design to follow, emulator-review.md): set aside for timing risk and because the Adreno emulation thread waits on the GPU, but the Flip's slow stretches are emulation time; Flip first, then compat; **looked into 2026-11-08, overnight, not built: needs Joey's call on timing** (Joey 2026-11-08 morning: leave the RSP thread): worth it on paper (Duo `prof13`, uncapped: the RSP's functions are 32-62% of the core's samples, GoldenEye 32, OoT 47, MK64 47, Conker 54, SM64 62, about a fifth of all samples in OoT, and the RSP JIT's own code is on top, in "unknown"), but our RSP (parallel-RSP's JIT) runs each task to its end inside `do_SP_Task`, and mupen raises the SP interrupt 1000 cycles (graphics) or 4000 (audio) after the start, so a thread would have almost no CPU time to overlap with: the CPU would wait for it at once. armN64's design overlaps because its RSP runs in windows of CPU time with its own cycle count, so its interrupt comes when the task would really end: for us that means running parallel-RSP in cycle windows and moving the SP interrupt to the task's real end, a timing change for every game (compat on all four devices), plus the RSP's RDP commands (DPC_END from the RSP's MTC0 runs parallel-RDP's command processing now) moved to the emulation thread. The alternative, Project64's, lets the interrupt come when the thread finishes: faster to build, but runs then depend on thread timing (netplay's lockstep and our benchmarks need deterministic runs). Joey 2026-11-08: "Leave it for now" (back of the queue, item 37); **prototype measured 2026-11-09 (Joey: "Test it next"): no gain**: core branch `rsp-thread` 6c009ef (off c0a3963), dev switch N64_RSP_THREAD=1, Project64-style: graphics tasks run on parallel-RSP in a thread of their own, the SP interrupt is checked N64_RSP_THREAD_DELAY count cycles after the start (default 1000 as now) and the emulation thread waits for the task there; any CPU write to the SP or DP, a new task, a savestate or an option change waits first; the thread's MI bits go to a private word merged at the end; parallel-RDP gets a lock and smart sync marks pages before protecting them (a first build froze every game with the thread off: its fault handler took the smart-sync guard twice, fixed). With the switch off it matches the bundled core (Duo OoT 347 vs 347, SM64 394 vs 393). Uncapped, quick-set subset, 1 round (`duo-rspt-delay`, `odin3-rspt-delay`), emu fps thread off / delay 1k / 20k / 100k / 300k: Duo OoT 348/323/322/322/323, SM64 393/346/350/346/346, MK64 307/280/276/275/276, PD 201/195/196/197/196; Odin 3 OoT 756/720/718/717/712, SM64 1016/844/823/899/873, GoldenEye 1020/996/1010/942/932, MK64 942/950/968/949/962, PD 747/758/755/760/730. A later interrupt buys nothing: after starting a graphics task these games wait for it in an idle loop, which the dynarec skips straight to the next event, so the CPU reaches the check at once and waits whatever the delay; there is almost no CPU work to overlap, and the hand-off costs 0-17% at 1k (Banjo 26% on the Duo). Later interrupts also change game timing: Banjo-Kazooie draws every VI at 100k+ (254 emu = 253 presented), GoldenEye's Duo 395 at 20k came with more presented frames, Conker stops drawing at 100k+ on the Duo, and Banjo/Conker runs failed at some delays on the Odin 3. armN64's windowed design has the same overlap limit (it only overlaps CPU work that exists), so it is not built either. Flip (`flip-rspt`, 2 rounds, thread off / 1k / 20k): GoldenEye 135/138/137, OoT 140/138/129, MK64 154/158/152, SM64 135/134/136: within noise, no gain there either. **Closed 2026-11-09: not built.** (20) the parallel-n64 commits never measured (item 27): d9189e3 (command list handed over), the lock-free ring, worker and pool commits 4da080d, c75aa6b, 0223d23, b625971, 841739e, faf7431, 5672c8d, and the "low value" 0a9199c, 5631c59, c50beac, 9df1d90, one at a time on the Flip and Portal (ac9c6e8 at an upscale with downscale). **0223d23 done 2026-11-09, no gain:** ported onto the bundled core (branch pn20-0223 = b27858f + eb3cf0e: the scanout flush and resolve as a meta-op on the command thread, idle_lock gone), Portal 14-game quick set uncapped, two runs each in turns (`bench-portal-pn20a`): geomean -0.4%, every game within -1.6..+1.8% but Perfect Dark -4.3% (both runs); A return stack would remove only the returns' part of both: 1-2% at best, for a change that must survive code invalidation, exceptions and states. Then (2026-11-08, Joey: "add your recommendations to back of queue"), build-and-measure checks of the not-worth-building calls that rest on an estimate, cheapest first: (38) item 26 hot/cold layout built for real (cold tails in their own arena) and benched on the Flip, since the profile showed where refills happen but not how many denser bodies would remove [**built 2026-11-10 overnight**, core branch `hotcold` 0a84f97 (worktree C:\GithubProjects\joey64-core-split, off `combine-fix`), `N64_DRC_SPLIT=1`, off by default: the 32 MB cache is cut into 512 KB chunks whose first 128 KB holds the cold code (memory, cycle-count and exit stubs, the dirty-check entry stubs, the literal pool), filled upwards, with the block bodies in the rest. Cold code has to sit behind the body cursor: the lookups read an entry just ahead of `out` as about to be overwritten, and the entry stubs are cold. Everything a body branches to stays within 1 MB (b.cond, adr, literal loads); a block whose cold code would not fit finishes in the body area; the compile thread's dropped and bailed compiles put the cold cursor back too. App `app-split-1` (core d141c5). Queued: Duo compat with it on (`duo-split3-compat`, end of next99; next96 stopped) and the Flip A/B with item 39's trial (`flip-splitfpd1`, next95b, after next94)]; (39) items 27 and 29 together (pin the hottest guest registers, cheaper FPU operand access) on the Flip, since their 1.9-5.1% assumed samples spread evenly over a block and register loads that hit L1 [**FPU half built 2026-11-10 overnight as a speed trial**, core branch `fpdirect` d2ffdaa (on `hotcold`), `N64_DRC_FPDIRECT=1`, off by default: every FPU operand (142 sites in the arm64 back end, 8 in the shared code) took its address from `cp1_regs_simple`/`_double` with a load and then loaded the value, two dependent loads; with it on the address is an add from the state pointer worked out at compile time, so one load is left. That fixes Status.FR when a block compiles; an FR change after code was compiled is only logged ("Status.FR changed ... with direct FPU addressing"), since dropped pages can come back unchanged with the old layout, so a run that logs one does not count, and an FR-safe form is needed before it could be a default. Register pinning (the bigger half, a register-allocator change in every block) waits on this result: if the cheaper access of FPU operands shows nothing, the profile's 1.9-5.1% ceiling for pinning is the honest estimate. App `app-fpd-1` (core b6612a, both item 38 and 39 switches). Queued on the Flip as one A/B with item 38: base vs `N64_DRC_SPLIT=1` vs `N64_DRC_FPDIRECT=1`, uncapped, seven gameplay states, 3 rounds (`flip-splitfpd1`, next95b, after next94; replaces next95, stopped before it ran)]; (40) item 18 again: a Paper Mario time profile in a scene that waits (menus, a text box, a loading pause), since only one gameplay state was profiled, building only if a hot small loop shows [**done 2026-11-09 03:29, Duo, nothing to build:** `profpm.sh` / `next26-duo-pmprof.sh` (profbench3 with one key 4 s after Joey's Paper Mario bench state, uncapped, 15 s of simpleperf; results/prof/b.35.pmstart.*, b.35.pmplay.*): Start opens the pause menu (Stats page, shot checked) and it runs at 259 emu fps against 340 in the gameplay reference; in the menu the core is 12.7% of samples (21.0% in gameplay), the Turnip driver 45.3% (34.7%), libc 17.8%, kernel 13.9%; the hottest core symbol is get_addr_ht at 1.4%, then parallel-RDP's fence worker 0.9%, nothing else over 0.7%. Power could not be compared: this Odin 3 reports its GPU at 1100 MHz (its top) all the time, idle or not, on both drivers (kgsl `gpuclk`, busy % reads 0), and it was charging, so battery current says nothing; a battery-powered run would be needed. Why stock has less lag is not yet known (likely its swapchain/present path); the same play test at 1x (`odin3-drv1xcap60`, next73, 2026-11-09, same method): no real difference (so the stock driver costs nothing at 1x: **Auto now picks it at every resolution on Adreno 8xx, ae402d0, 2026-11-10**, after Joey's "Why driver at 1x if even with stock?" and the stock compat runs: `odin3-sys1x-compat` 82/82 and `odin3-sys2x-set` 34/34, 0 black against odin3-spfix2/gpur1; device check `odin3-autodrv`, next90, done 01:10: OoT from its bench state with no driver or renderer override logs the system driver (Adreno 830, Qualcomm driver) at both 1x and 2x, 60 emu fps, its normal 20 presented): every game at its normal rate on both, no frame over 33 ms on either, input lag 22.0 ms Turnip vs 21.6 ms stock (each game within 1 ms: GoldenEye 15.7/15.2, OoT 15.1/14.9, Yoshi's Story 15.8/14.8), so the 4x gap comes from GPU load, not the drivers' present path alone. (46) [**queued 2026-11-10 overnight** on the Flip after its block-list compat: `flip-gpuclk1`, next93: capped 60, Auto renderer, SM64, OoT, GoldenEye, Conker, Mario Kart 64 from the bench states, 40 s x 2 rounds, time at each GPU clock from the devfreq `trans_stat` diff (readable without root; the governor is simple_ondemand, 384-850 MHz) beside presented fps, slowest frame and frames over 33 ms; then Turok: Rage Wars' block-list reruns (next94)] GPU clock under real play on Mali (2026-11-09, from Pocket Native, github.com/whovian64/pocket-native: on RK3326/RK3566 Mali its 3DS games ran at 16 fps with the GPU and memory on their on-demand governors and 30 with them pinned at top speed, power.sh). The Flip (Retroid Pocket 3.5 Flip, Unisoc ums512, Mali-G52 MP2, Android 11, not rooted) has its GPU on simple_ondemand, 384-768 MHz (850 MHz in the table, max_freq 768), polling every 100 ms; an app can not set it there (no root, no ADPF or GameManager on Android 11), only the shape of its load moves it. Our benches run uncapped, which keeps the GPU busy and its clock up, so they would not show a capped game whose CPU waits on the GPU each frame leaving the governor low. Steps: on the Flip, capped at 60 with parallel-RDP (SM64, OoT, GoldenEye, Conker, Mario Kart 64), sample /sys/class/devfreq/60000000.gpu/cur_freq every 50 ms beside the frame times; if the clock sits at 384-512 MHz while frames miss, try what keeps the GPU busy evenly (async + smart sync where it is off, submitting each frame in one piece) and measure capped. Also log the GPU clock in the bench and log lines where it is readable (dev only). Capped remeasures on the Flip, from defaults decided on uncapped numbers there: single-threaded RDP commands (+3.7% uncapped; it puts command work on the emulation thread, the most serialized choice), repeat-frame skip (+14.7% uncapped; less GPU work can let the governor drop), and Snap with smart sync vs sync (sync costs up to a third there). Not needed: Adreno (the turbo pin, `N64_GPU_TURBO=1`, was measured capped and gained nothing on the Odin 3 or the Duo), strong devices (3-6x over 60), choices decided capped (early acquire latency, compile-thread hitches, the RK3566 GLideN64 rule), the Mali jump cache (its Flip losses came at the same GPU clock), malisx2 (14% slower). Also covers frame generation through Lossless Scaling's Vulkan layer (the Eden approach: the user supplies the DLL) for 20-30 fps games (2026-11-09, Joey via the run-ahead session: frame generation / interpolation at the back of the work order). Measure first, before any menu: cost and added latency on the Odin 3 and the Thor (the Thor only with Joey's go). The menu stays Joey's design.

Item 46 (GPU clock, `flip-gpuclk3`, capped 60, 2 rounds of 40 s): the Mali-G52 sits at its lowest 384 MHz for 70-100% of play (SM64, OoT, GoldenEye, Conker), with no frame over 33 ms in those four; Mario Kart 64 has 6-8 frames over 33 ms (slowest 64-71 ms) while the GPU stays at 384 MHz 99.7% of the time, so its misses are not the GPU clock dropping (the governor never needed to go up): it is CPU/sync work, item 35's side, and the GPU governor is not part of the misses. Items 38/39 A/B (`flip-splitfpd3`, uncapped, app-fpd-3, stopped at Banjo-Kazooie round 1): emu fps base / split / FPU direct: OoT 48.0/crash/47.9, SM64 67.0/66.8/67.1, GoldenEye 110.1/110.6/110.0, Banjo-Kazooie 90.3/83.1/88.5: **neither gains** (all within run-to-run noise or worse), so item 39's FPU half is dropped and register pinning's 1.9-5.1% ceiling is the honest estimate, and item 38's layout shows no gain even where it runs. **Item 38 crash found to its first step** (Odin 3 Portal, debug cores on core branch `hotcold-dbg`: bebfb43 bogus-address log, 3c1124e compile trace, 9801dff unmapped log, 809df4c code dump): with the layout on, the very first block (the boot code at A4000040) leaves wrongly and the game lands on the exception vector at 80000000 (empty RAM), compiles 16 KB blocks of zeros up to 807FC000 and dies at 80800000; without it the second block is A40000B8. No unmapped compile is involved, so it is a data exception in that first block. Code dumps of that block with and without the layout are in scratchpad `compat/results/dump1-split.txt` / `dump1-base.txt` (body 0x1d0 + cold 0x53c vs 0x70c together) for an offline diff. Since the layout gains nothing where it runs, the fix only matters if a later measurement shows a gain; parked with item 38. **Restart 2026-11-10 (Joey: "continue")**: Flip ddmin3 (a list is black if any of 3 runs is), Duo games 24-82, Odin 3 games 29-82, Odin 2 Portal games 42-82 on app-cfix-4, Odin 3 Portal Perfect Dark 16:9 rerun then run-ahead 4x compat on app-ph0-d6ac-e95a. **Bundle candidate cfix-5**: the Frontend session's core 3831e0d (Paper Mario 4x run-ahead flicker: RDRAM uploads in stream order after a put back; includes fdf0869) merged into core branch `combine-fix` as 46d79a5, built as core-cfix-5 (md5 db6050), app-cfix-5 and app-ph0-d6ac-db60 (launcher d6ac605 + cfix-5); it needs its own compat once the cfix-4 runs finish. Next for Cruis'n: repeat-count each list, shrink it, then see what those blocks are (code that changes after the early compile, or a page that early code makes the store path treat differently). The bundle waits on this; `N64_DRC_LIST=0` is the fallback.  Portal run-ahead 4x compat on the previous candidate (`o3p-ra4x2-compat`, core 749feb, next88): 82/82 ok. Its d970e9 follow-up (next89) was stopped before its first game and replaced by the new candidate: `o3p-ra4x5-compat` (next101, app-ph0-d6ac-5096 = launcher d6ac605 + core 5096e5, run-ahead 1 frame, 4x). It goes into the bundle once those are clean.

- [ ] Pipeline cache save stalls (found 2026-11-08 through Goemon; Joey: drops at the start of their state in RetroArch too): every 600 frames the core read parallel-RDP's whole driver pipeline cache and wrote it on the emulation thread; on the Flip's Mali that is 18.6-22 MB in Goemon, ~140 ms per save, one dropped burst every 10 s (Flip game 12 and 27 too, less often). Fixed on core branch `pcache-bg` (268500f, on 1a6aa5b = mips-default + gliden64-update): the periodic save runs on its own thread, shutdown still waits and saves last, `N64_RDP_CACHE_SYNC=1` restores the old save. Flip, 2 rounds (`flip-pc1`): Goemon worst frame after the first window 153-185 -> 41 ms, 121 -> 137 fps (+12%); game 12 188-200 -> 27-30 ms; game 27 mixed both ways. Flip 82-game compat with it (`flip-pc1-compat`, 2026-11-08): 82/82 start; the 4 black last shots (7, 36, 58, 81) are black in the previous Flip run (flip-mipd) too; largest difference Mario Tennis, a different menu at shot time, both drawn right. Follow-up (b) built: core pcache-bg 6d7a060 saves only when pipelines were created since the last save, every 10 s doubling to 80 s (`N64_RDP_CACHE_EVERY=1` = old rule); app-pc5; Flip A/B against pc1 (`flip-pc5`, Goemon/12/27 x2; `flip-pc5b` 27 again, game 2 has no bench state). **Done 2026-11-08:** the same speed: the round with a ~390 ms first window (shader builds) follows run order, not the build; leaving it out, Goemon 166.2 (pc1) vs 168.3 (pc5), game 12 132.2 vs 131.8/132.8, game 27 164.4 vs 164.3/164.5 emu fps; slowest frame after the first window, Goemon 35.5/24.3 ms on pc1 vs 14.1/16.4 on pc5 (one frame over 33 ms on pc1, none on pc5): in line with (a), 2 rounds only. pc5 (save only when new) is in ship-vk12-pc, which passed compat on the Duo 82/82. The growth is parallel-RDP's design, not a leak (Duo, `N64_RDP_PIPE_LOG=1` dev log, uncommitted): every new pipeline is the rasterizer program specialised for a new RDP state (combiner, blender), built on its async threads, ~10 per 600 frames in Goemon even standing still. Queued (Joey 2026-11-08: "add to queue"): (a) the remaining 36-41 ms Goemon frames on the Flip, which start as a background save starts (core contention with the save thread or the async pipeline builds?); (b) save less often once a game's pipeline list stops changing much (the save costs ~230 ms of a core on the Flip); then bundle into the next core

- [ ] Temporal upscaling (idea, parked 2026-11-09 when Joey asked about it; not decided): libretro's parallel-n64 added the geometry side of a frontend temporal upscaler to its parallel-RDP (`temporal_upscaler.c/.h`, 8 commits 2026-10-26..28, RetroArch team): each frame is drawn under a sub-pixel jitter and the core works out motion vectors by matching each triangle with the same triangle in the frame before (the RDP sees screen-space triangles, no camera); unmatched triangles and rectangles count as still. The frontend does the upscaling itself; where RetroArch's side of it lives was not found yet. For us it would mean porting that core side plus writing an upscaler pass in our host. Who it could help: the weak GPUs (Flip Mali-G52, Mali-G57 like the RG 476H, the Duo at 2x), sharper than native for less than drawing at 2x; the strong Adrenos already draw 4x-8x outright. Risks: ghosting where the triangle matching guesses wrong (2D, HUDs, fast motion), the pass's own cost on exactly those weak GPUs, and any new picture setting is Joey's to design. Before any work: see it running (RetroArch, if its frontend side ships) on the Flip or the Duo for picture and cost.

- [ ] Head-to-head vs RetroArch and mupen64plus-ae (FZ's open base): local profileable builds set up 2026-11-04 (RetroArch 1.22.2 as `com.retroarch.bench` in C:\GithubProjects\retroarch-bench, moved to AGP 8.5/NDK 27; stock upstream core from the joey64-core-stock worktree; Joey's mupen64plus-ae debug build on the Odin 3). First finding: on the Odin 3's stock Adreno 830 driver parallel-RDP's compute shaders fail to compile, so stock RetroArch shows a black screen (we bundle Turnip and pick it automatically). Correction 2026-11-05 (Joey: RetroArch nightly fixes this): RetroArch master gained custom Vulkan driver loading on Adreno on 2026-11-04 (commit 665064a, PR 19955, libadrenotools-style loader namespace + hook): sideloaded arm64 builds on Android 9+ load a driver zip (meta.json + .so, e.g. Turnip) picked by hand in Settings > Video > Output > Custom Vulkan Driver from <system>/vulkan_drivers, per core/content/game override, falling back to the system driver if it fails; Play Store, Quest and API 16 builds don't have it. So with a Turnip zip supplied, RetroArch nightly can run parallel-RDP on the Odin 3; on the stock driver it still can't, because the shader failure is in Qualcomm's compiler and upstream mupen64plus-next has not changed since 2026-10-22. The difference left is that Joey64 bundles the drivers and picks one per GPU with no setup. Our GLideN64 black screen in RetroArch on the Odin 3 was never traced; nothing in RetroArch master since 2026-10-01 names it (nearest: 8ca610d, a GL core moving to the other context). Next: same games and settings on the Flip (parallel-RDP, stock Mali works) and GLideN64 on all three; fps, CPU per frame, input lag

GLideN64 uncapped 1x: SM64 239, OoT 180, MK64 227, Yoshi's Story 187, Snap 112 fps (slowest frames MK64 57 ms, Snap 91 ms). Why: Mesa sets `storage_8bit` (both the feature and VK_KHR_8bit_storage) only for a7xx and keeps a6xx at Vulkan 1.3 for it (`freedreno_devices.py`, `tu_device.cc`, Mesa main 0f7977e); Granite runs on the core's Vulkan 1.1 instance, so it takes 8-bit storage only through that extension. PurpleVK says Vulkan 1.4 on the 650 (1.4 requires 8-bit storage) yet failed the same check. Done: the probe now needs 16- and 8-bit storage before Auto picks parallel-RDP (app gpu-rules 42427b5) and logs per driver the instance and device versions, 16-bit, 8-bit and whether the extension is listed (b72258d; Odin 3, Duo, Flip: all 1 and listed, so unchanged). Next (Joey 2026-11-08: "We need to solve 865 with parallel"): a kit built with that probe (kit 14:12, apk 42f7b7f8, same core e3375007) for one more tester run; if a driver shows 8-bit storage but no extension, the core's instance goes to 1.2+ (small); if none has it, parallel-RDP needs shaders without 8-bit storage (byte writes as 32-bit read-modify-write with atomics, 32-bit coverage and shade-alpha buffers, packed 8-bit struct fields), on only where the GPU lacks it, built and 82-game checked on our devices with it forced on, then one 865 run. - [ ] Sin and Punishment (English patch) on new_dynarec: jumps to a bogus address since upstream's 3.0 core update (a core from just before boots it); we use the cached interpreter (kInterpreterGames). Find the breaking commit, fix it, report upstream. **Root cause and fix 2026-11-08:** the English patch puts a hook at the end of the 32 MB ROM (0xb1ffff70: it writes a stub into RDRAM and jumps to 0x80025c00), so the CPU runs code straight from cartridge ROM; new_dynarec only compiles from RDRAM, SP memory or TLB pages and called exit(1) ("Compile at bogus memory address"). Fix: compile from the ROM image (`cart_rom_source`; verify_dirty treats ROM as unchanged), core branch sp-dynarec 19eff8c on ship-vk12-pc (worktree joey64-core-sp, core 6a9d070a, app-spfix). **Duo, recompiler forced (`duo-spfix1`, `duo-spfix2`, compat64w game 67):** ok twice, "compiling code in cartridge ROM (first at b1ffff70)" logged, no bogus/Fatal, 59.9 emu fps, title then the English main menu in all shots. The compat keys stop at the menu (its legend: Z selects), so a play run (`devspplay.sh`: Z on START GAME, then Z/Start for 2 min; recompiler vs cached interpreter, `duo-spplay1/2`) is queued after the Duo's sleep test. **Play run 2026-11-08 (`duo-spplay1` recompiler, `duo-spplay2` cached interpreter):** both ok, 2.5 min at 59.9 emu fps, no errors, ROM-compile path logged on the recompiler; but neither left the main menu: Z alone keeps the cursor in the middle (the menu moves with the stick, which keys cannot drive), the same on both CPU cores, so a test-input limit, not the fix. Retry with the D-pad onto START GAME first (`devspplay2.sh`, `duo-spplayb1/2`) queued after the Duo's sleep rerun; if that still stops at the menu, gameplay needs a hand check on a device. **D-pad run (`duo-spplayb1/2`, 2026-11-09 ~00:00):** ok on both CPU cores, still the main menu in every shot: the D-pad does not move this cursor either, only the analog stick. Next try: an injected joystick (`input joystick motionevent`, `devspstick.sh`, `duo-spstick1`, recompiler); if Joey64 does not take injected stick motion, gameplay needs a hand check. **Joystick runs 2026-11-09:** Joey64 takes injected stick motion. `duo-spstick1` (stick up-left 6 x 0.3 s) moved the cursor into the top-left corner, past START GAME; `duo-spstickb1` (`devspstick2.sh`: then nudges down-right, Z after each) **started the game: Sin and Punishment plays on the recompiler with the fix**: stage 1 with enemies, score 0 to 1029, timer 99 to 0, the pause menu, the boss; 59.9 emu fps throughout, ROM-compile path logged, no errors. The approved next step runs now: 82-game compat on app-spfix2 (core 8882eadb = ship3 + the fix) on all four (`next12-spfix2-compat.sh`, `<dev>-spfix2-compat`; Duo at once, the others after their chains); then drop NGU from kInterpreterGames + gamedb in the same change that bundles the core. **Duo done 2026-11-09 02:10: `duo-spfix2-compat` 82/82 ok**; blackcheck flags none (the 5 black end shots are black in `duo-ship2-compat` too); largest shotcmp differences (Bomberman, Wave Race 64, 11.2 and 9.1) are the same screens a frame or two apart. **Odin 3 done 2026-11-09 02:40: `odin3-spfix2-compat` 82/82 ok**; blackcheck flags none (23, 36, 58, 65 dark as always); largest shotcmp difference Bomberman 64 (4, 17.4) is the same fade a moment apart, the rest under 5.6. **Flip done 2026-11-09 05:11: `flip-spfix2-compat` 82/82 ok**; black last shots 7, 36, 58, 81, all black in `flip-ship3-compat` too; largest shotcmp difference against ship3 Wave Race 64 (77, 10.1), the rest under 5.7: same screens; S&P (67) reaches its menu (here still on the cached interpreter, as kInterpreterGames forces it until the bundle). **Duo on the bundle candidate itself, 2026-11-09 05:22: `duo-spfix3-compat` (app-spfix3) 82/82 ok**; black last shots 23, 36, 58, 65 as in `duo-spfix2-compat`; largest differences: Elise (54) now at its file select (the ini fix), War Zone (75) its menu where spfix2 caught the black moment between attract screens (item e), the rest the same screens a moment apart. **Portal done 2026-11-09 05:33: 82/82 ok** (`portal-spfix2-compat` 1-47, stopped at 49% battery, then `portal-spfix2-compat-b` 48-82 at 55%); black last shots 23, 36, 58, 65, 75, all black in `portal-ship2-compat` too; largest differences Mario Tennis (32, Character Select vs Play Mode) and Bomberman 64 (5, the same white fade), timing only. **All four passed. Joey: "Fix all" (2026-11-08): all five fixed on joey64 `audit2/fixes` 5abe612 (launcher/phase0 cf0355b + `dd`, for the Frontend to merge): the start's file work and the quit push run on one `joey64-gameio` thread, SrmSync.pull skips an unchanged folder copy; the host tells MainActivity when the core shut down and whether the 64DD IPL was missing, the game screen closes, and the library asks for the BIOS again (only for a missing IPL; a damaged disk or disk save just goes back); a 64DD disk is at least 1 MiB; a cartridge that won't unpack goes back to the cartridge question; BiosImport swaps its file in. Flip `dd15` (app-a2-3): backup on the gameio thread, game 60 fps, the three failure cases close as above; 82-game Flip compat `flip-a2-compat` (`chain150`, app-a2-3 = audit2/fixes + core dd-lazy): 82/82 ok (2026-11-08). Findings were: (1) disk-sized file work on the main thread at game start and quit (surfaceCreated / onDestroy): the zipped disk-save backup (Flip dd12: 2.1 s, thread = main, confirmed), SrmSync.pull of the 65 MB disk save from a picked folder on every start even when unchanged, BiosSync (up to 16 MB), and the disk save push in onDestroy; (2) a 64DD disk whose region has no IPL (only IPL_DEV.n64 present, a Japanese disk): black screen, no message, the user is stuck on the game screen (Flip, confirmed; the launcher's BIOS check only asks that some IPL exists); (3) .d64 is also the Commodore 64 disk extension: a C64 .d64 in a ROM folder is listed as a 64DD disk (no size check; 64DD images are 55-70 MB, C64 ones ~170 KB); (4) a cartridge that fails to unpack (EXTRA_DD_CART) starts the disk alone with no message (from the code); (5) BiosImport deletes the old IPL before renaming the new one in, so a failed rename leaves none (from the code). Checked and fine: lazy waking (no lost wakeup: the reader rechecks under the lock, the 0.5 ms timed wait bounds the rest), VI fix, normal quit (Flip: clean stop, the new "could not start" path is not taken), rumble 2-4, audio stall, utf16, ScreenScraper (login never logged), Joey64 covers, RA list and host rules, BIOS import, SrmSync/FileCopy/TreeSpace, AppBackup restore, release-build intent checks, debug receivers debug-only
- [ ] Code audit 2026-11-07 (Joey: "Fix all"): fixed on joey64 `audit/fixes` (f0530de, a0e485a, a781599, af14012; merged into launcher/phase0 by the Frontend session, fast-forward to af14012; the bundled core there is still a8ab829, so the short-state check ships with the next core bundled from drc-tier) and core `drc-tier` 2c9fc76 (short-state check): dev extras in release builds, ../ save names, in-place restore and folder-copy writes, cached failed listings, small native fixes. Device check `chain147` (Odin 3, `results/auditfix-odin3.txt`): passed: utf16_test all cases ok on the device, the dev extras still taken by a debug build (OoT state loads, 60 fps), a state cut to 9 MB is refused (`FAILED`) and the game keeps running, a handed-over save name `../evil` becomes `saves/.._evil.srm`. RetroAchievements host override: Joey chose PPSSPP's rules (f327b44 on `audit/ra-host`, in launcher/phase0 as ba1949c): http only, a port, nothing after the host. The receiver stays exported, like every emulator RAOfflineProxy reaches (Mupen64Plus AE, ARMSX2, Flycast and PPSSPP checked): none can check the sender, since RAOfflineProxy sends without a permission. Smart sync's wait for the GPU inside its SIGSEGV handler, checked 2026-11-07 and closed: the wait takes only the fence worker's mutex (CommandProcessor::wait_for_timeline), and the timeline it waits for moves on without the faulting thread. The command ring worker processes the signal (single-threaded mode submitted it already), the fence worker waits on the fence and copies results through g_rdram_alias, and the host's queue lock is held only around submit/present/wait-idle. Only CPU emulation code (dynarec, interpreter, DMA/RSP memcpy, rcheevos reads, state copies, the compile thread's snapshot check) reads the protected view, and none of it holds those locks. Same limit as plain sync: a hung GPU waits forever

### Performance deep dive (2026-11-04: aim for big gains; try ideas from any emulator, measured)
- [ ] Present only frames that changed, GLideN64 (parked 2026-11-04: Joey keeps GLideN64 as the fallback renderer, no work goes into it). Most games draw 20-30 frames a second but the core presented every VI (60). The libretro core forces swap mode 0; M64Plus FZ/ae uses 2 (on a new frame buffer). Core switch `N64_GLN_SWAP_MODE=1|2` built: Duo, OoT Temple of Time, 213/214 -> 159/159 ms/s (same CPU as ae). Left to do before it can be the default: compat run mode 2 vs 0 (the Odin 3 runs were stopped unfinished; the first was void, two runs fought over the device), uncapped bench (queued last on the Flip/Portal), the opt-in pacing modes
- [x] Present only frames that changed, parallel-RDP (the big one). **Default since core 92fe5c3 / app 07869ad (2026-11-04); `N64_RDP_SKIP_DUP=0` / `N64_SKIP_DUPES=0` turn it off.** Core `N64_RDP_SKIP_DUP=1`: a refresh is a repeat when the VI registers are unchanged, the RDP drew nothing into the rows the VI reads, and an RDRAM hash of those rows matches (catches CPU drawing); never when interlaced. The hash is only compared once the frame's GPU work has landed in RDRAM (`CommandProcessor::timeline_reached`), else a frame the GPU finished late counted as CPU drawing (GoldenEye, Paper Mario found no repeats); the rows are exact, since a margin made drawing into the adjacent buffer count (SM64, MK64 found a third). The log line says why refreshes were new. Odin 3, repeats found = the game's own rate in 14 of Joey's save spots (SM64/MK64/Paper Mario/Banjo/Snap/DK64/Yoshi/Cruis'n/Conker 300 of 600, OoT/MM 400, Perfect Dark 476; Smash draws every frame, GoldenEye's scene 268 of 300). App `N64_SKIP_DUPES=1`: the Vulkan side acquires a swapchain image only for a new frame, so repeats cost nothing. Uncapped (N64_BENCH=1) from Joey's states, median of 3, off -> on, before the two detection fixes: Flip OoT 115 -> 139 (+21%), Banjo 147 -> 175 (+19%), Paper Mario 113 -> 127 (+12%), MM 93 -> 103 (+11%), SM64 96 -> 102 (+6%), GoldenEye 92 -> 89 (-3%); Portal SM64 340 -> 397 (+17%), MM 416 -> 479 (+15%), OoT 353 -> 389 (+10%), Banjo 354 -> 389 (+10%), GoldenEye/Paper Mario +1/0%. Duo at full speed, OoT: 349 -> 274 ms/s CPU, uneven frames 0-3 per 5 s (was ~130), poll->display unchanged. Next: full compat on the Duo with the fixed detection (running), rerun the bench (SM64/MK64/Paper Mario should gain more now), find GoldenEye's Flip -3% (likely the later swapchain acquire, the hash is ~40 us), then default. Open: fast-forward shows 128 DL/s in OoT instead of ~80; recheck with the fixes. Bug found by the Duo compat run, fixed (core 98f05df): with the app skipping repeats, Banjo-Kazooie/Tooie showed stale and black frames, because the core's new-frame flag was overwritten when one retro_run ended a new refresh and then a repeat, so the new image was never presented (core skip alone was fine; found by A/B, off vs core-only vs both). The flag now stays set until the frontend reads it; Banjo shots match skip-off in both rounds. The bench numbers above predate the fix (missed presents may have flattered them). Re-measured with the fix, 14 games from Joey's states, median of 2, off -> on: Portal +21.5% (Snap +64%, Yoshi +55%, SM64 +51%, MK64 +34%, Cruis'n +31%, Paper Mario +23%, OoT +16%, MM +15%, GoldenEye/Perfect Dark +11%, Banjo +6%, DK64 +4%, Conker +2%, Smash 0%); Flip +14.7%, thermal status 0 throughout (Yoshi 205 -> 288, Snap 126 -> 161, MK64 119 -> 149, Cruis'n 117 -> 145, OoT 139 -> 158, SM64 95 -> 115, Paper Mario 114 -> 137, Banjo 99 -> 117, GoldenEye 117 -> 130, MM 86 -> 95, DK64 116 -> 123, Perfect Dark 92 -> 94, Smash 101 -> 101, Conker 118 -> 111). Full Duo compat rerunning with the fix plus precise sync. Conker on the Flip measured 4-6% slower with skip on in the first two benches; a Conker-only rerun with the current core (3 rounds, GPU clock logged) does not reproduce it: off 103.0, core skip only 104.5, full skip 105.4 fps (+2.3%), GPU clock 580-610 MHz in all three, so not Mali clocking down. Closed unless it shows up again
