- [x] Flip black screens (found 2026-11-05 checking the Yoshi early-acquire runs): Spider-Man (50) and Yoshi's Story (79) draw their intros, then go black with 0 new frames from ~25 s on, in every Flip run since 11-05 (`flip-early30`, `flip-gpu8c`, the four Yoshi runs), while 11-03's `flip` and `flip-smart` show both games playing and the Odin 3 and Duo (synchronous / smart sync) draw them in every run (centre brightness 131 and 70 vs 0.5 and 0.0 on the Flip). So something that became default on 11-04 breaks them with async parallel-RDP on Mali (the Flip runs async except `kSyncRdpGames`; Yoshi is only on the Adreno list). Prime suspect: repeat-frame skip (its 'nothing changed' is what '0 new frames' reports; with async, RDRAM can be stale when it hashes). Queued on the Flip after its item 27 run (`chain60_flip_black.sh`, app-gpu8): both games x default / repeat skip off (`N64_RDP_SKIP_DUP=0,N64_SKIP_DUPES=0`) / smart sync on. Then fix the cause, or add both to `kSyncRdpGames`. Also: the compat script's checks miss this (a game that goes black after its first screenshots counts as ok); added 2026-11-05: `scratchpad/tools/blackcheck.py <run> <reference runs>` flags games whose last screenshot is black and whose last 3 VI windows drew nothing while a reference run shows them (not wired into compat64.sh while a running compat job reads that script). Run over every full compat run since 11-04 (Duo x4, Odin 3 x2, Portal, Flip x2): only these two games on the Flip; every Adreno run clean. Result (2026-11-05, `flip-blk-def/noskip/sync`, app-gpu8): not the repeat skip. With repeat skip off both games still present 60 new frames per second, but every one is black from ~25 s (centre brightness 0.5 / 0.0); the default only showed '0 new frames' because the black frames really are identical. Smart sync draws both (151 and 62, the same as 11-03). It is not new either: on 11-03, async runs on the Adrenos already went black in these games at random (Yoshi on the Odin 3 and Duo, Spider-Man on the Portal; that is why Yoshi was on the Adreno list). Async lets the CPU read a picture before the GPU has finished it, and whether that shows depends on timing; the Flip's timing changed and now it happens every run. Fix (app f6b99e6): both games joined `kSyncRdpGames` (sync on every GPU, also in fast-forward; the Adreno-only list is gone). Cost on the Flip: 58.6-58.8 fps average with sync, lowest 5 s windows 53.5-54.3 (the async runs looked faster only because black frames cost nothing to draw). Check of the default build with the fix (`flip-sync6`, app-sync6, 2026-11-05 17:2x): passed. Both games run synchronous on their own and draw to the end (last screenshot centre brightness 151.5 and 62.0, the same as 11-03); 58.4 and 58.7 emulated fps average, lowest 5 s window 49.3 (Spider-Man) and 53.8 (Yoshi)
- [ ] Repeat skip vs pacing: poll->display and uneven frames on the Flip, Portal and Odin 3 (only the Duo checked: 17.8 vs 18.0 ms), and check that `N64_SWAP_INTERVAL=1/2` and `N64_PRESENTER` still pace right when repeats are not presented; the timed-pacing numbers predate repeat skip. Flip result (2026-11-05 06:1x-06:47, `flip-pace`, app-gpu7, 8 games x 2 rounds at 60 fps, first 5 s window left out; the Flip's panel is 60 Hz): repeat skip (default) poll->display 42.6 ms mean, 0.03 uneven frames per 5 s; repeats presented (`N64_SKIP_DUPES=0`) 50.3 ms, 1.20 uneven (MM 6.1, GoldenEye 1.9); timed pacing (`N64_SWAP_INTERVAL=2`) 42.4 ms, 0 uneven (no change at 60 Hz); present thread 51.5 ms, 0 uneven. So repeat skip paces fine and lags ~8 ms less, but not in every game: Conker 36.8 vs 73.9 ms and MK64 50.0 vs 63.5 favour it, while Smash, which draws a new frame every refresh (0 repeats), lags more with it, 61.7 / 55.6 vs 40.0 / 40.0 ms in both rounds. Why: with repeat skip the swapchain image is acquired only after the frame has run, so when acquire has to wait for a free image the wait comes after the input poll; the plain path waits at frame start, before the poll. Built `N64_EARLY_ACQUIRE=1` (app f257624, off by default): after two new frames in a row the next frame acquires at frame start, a repeat ends the streak. Flip result (2026-11-05 06:5x-07:2x, `flip-early`, 8 games x 2, poll->display per round): it fixes Smash, 61.8 / 56.1 -> 40.6 / 40.4 ms (= presenting every frame, 40.3), Banjo, MM, OoT, MK64, Conker unchanged, but GoldenEye gets worse, 41.1 / 43.3 -> 50.4 / 49.6 ms and 60.0 -> 59.6 fps, and SM64 39.2 / 42.8 -> 44.4 / 45.5; mean 41.4 -> 41.0 ms, no uneven frames either way. A two-frame streak is too short for games that mix new frames and repeats. `N64_EARLY_ACQUIRE=<n>` now takes the streak length (app 0374ba6); Streak result (2026-11-05 07:2x-07:4x, `flip-early2`, 6 games x 2, per round): off / streak 2 / streak 30: Smash 55.9, 61.6 / 40.0, 40.0 / 46.1, 40.1 ms; GoldenEye 43.8, 41.1 / 51.0, 47.7 / 42.8, 41.1; SM64 44.1, 48.6 / 40.6, 44.4 / 46.2, 46.9; Banjo, MK64, Conker within 2 ms. Mean 43.4 / 41.0 / 40.9 ms, fps 59.92 / 59.86 / 59.95, uneven 0 / 0 / 0.04 per 5 s. Streak 30 keeps the Smash gain without GoldenEye's loss. Before it can be a default: a full Flip compat run with it (`chain38_flip_compat_early.sh`, new Joey64 copies compat64.sh / devcompat64.sh, tag flip-early30, running from 07:4x) and a look on the 120 Hz Adreno devices, where presentation differs. Portal and Odin 3 pacing still to do. Joey 2026-11-05: ask them again once compat and the Adreno check are done, before it becomes a default. Adreno check queued 2026-11-05 08:5x after each device's queue: off vs streak 30 at 60 fps, 6 games x 2, app-early2 (`duo-early30`, `odin3-early30`, `portal-early30`). Flip compat result (2026-11-05 07:4x-09:46, `flip-early30`, app-early2 with `N64_EARLY_ACQUIRE=30`, all 82 games, compared with flip-smart, the last full Flip compat run, 2026-11-03 on an older build): 82/82 ok by the script, and screenshots match apart from timing in every game but one (pixel diff flagged 4: Sands of Time, Mario Tennis and SM64 are the same screens reached at different moments, all drawn right). **Yoshi's Story stops drawing ~30 s after boot**: the second and third screenshots are black, the core's log shows no new frames at all from then on (repeats 600 of 600, VI 0, drew 0, 0 DL/s), while flip-smart showed its title, Lucky Fruit and story menus. Both runs are async with smart sync on the Flip, but flip-smart predates precise smart sync, repeat skip and the earlier scanout, so it is not yet known whether early acquire causes this. Queued: Yoshi alone on the same build with and without early acquire, 2 rounds (`chain46_flip_yoshi.sh`, after the VI filter run). Until that says otherwise, early acquire is not safe as a Flip default. Result (2026-11-05 14:4x-14:52, `flip-yoshi-off1/2`, `flip-yoshi-e30-1/2`, app-early2): early acquire is not the cause. All four runs go the same way: the intro draws (249, 51, 150, 150 new frames per 300 scanouts), then 14-15, then 0 from about 25 s on, black screen, 0 DL/s, at 60 emulated fps, with or without early acquire. The same black end shows in the Flip's merged-core compat run (`flip-gpu8c`) and for Spider-Man too (below, Flip black screens item). So early acquire is cleared for Yoshi; whether it becomes a Flip default goes back to its lag numbers Duo result (2026-11-05 08:5x-09:08, `duo-early30`, 120 Hz panel, 6 games x 2, poll->display mean / uneven frames per ~20 s run, rounds 1, 2): off / streak 30: Smash 16.7, 16.5 / 16.7, 16.7 ms (no Smash problem on the Duo to fix: the Flip's 56-62 ms does not happen here); GoldenEye 17.3, 17.9 / 19.3, 16.6 ms but uneven 18, 20 -> 207, 170 (worse pacing); MK64 34.7, 34.3 / 36.4, 35.6 ms; SM64, Banjo, Conker the same (30.3/30.4, 16.1/16.2, 27.3/27.0). fps the same. So on the Duo it gains nothing and makes GoldenEye judder: if it becomes a default, Mali (Flip) only. Odin 3 result (2026-11-05 15:09-15:22, `odin3-early30`, 120 Hz panel, 6 games x 2 at 60 fps, steady windows): no gain. poll->display off / streak 30: Smash 15.3 / 15.2 ms, GoldenEye 15.9 / 15.9, Banjo 14.9 / 14.9, SM64 29.2 / 29.2, MK64 32.8 / 32.7, Conker 26.7 / 26.7 (all games 22.44 / 22.46 ms); uneven frames per 5 s 19.6 / 21.1 (a little more in 5 of 6 games, GoldenEye 26.0 -> 28.5); presented fps identical. Like the Duo, the Odin 3 has no Smash lag to fix. So on Adreno: nothing to gain and slightly worse pacing; Flip only, if anything. Portal still queued (its v3 queue, first step) Portal result (2026-11-05, `portal-early30`, 120 Hz panel, 6 games x 2 at 60 fps): no gain. poll->display off / streak 30: Smash 19.0 / 18.5 ms, GoldenEye 18.0 / 18.3, SM64 30.5 / 30.8, OoT 17.1 / 17.2, MK64 36.1 / 36.0, Conker 27.9 / 27.6; slowest frames the same (one 35 ms Conker spike in an off run). So the Adreno check is done on all three 120 Hz devices (Duo, Odin 3, Portal): no gain anywhere, GoldenEye judders on the Duo. Decision for Joey: early acquire as a Flip (Mali) only default, or drop it Early acquire decided 2026-11-05 (Joey: "yes" to a Flip-only default): MainActivity sets `N64_EARLY_ACQUIRE=30` on Mali when `--es env` doesn't set it, off on Adreno; the log says `vulkan: early acquire after 30 new frames in a row`. Check queued at the end of the Flip queue. Checked 2026-11-06 01:5x (`flip-earlydef`, app-cleanup, 60 fps, 6 games x 2, nothing set vs `N64_EARLY_ACQUIRE=0`): the default is on. Smash Bros., the one game here presenting 60 fps, poll->display 58.0 -> 42.2 ms, as in flip-early30; GoldenEye, SM64, Banjo, MK64 and Conker present 21-50 fps, never reach 30 new frames in a row and are unchanged (40.8/40.5, 39.1/39.9, 26.1/26.2, 48.8/48.6, 35.8/36.1 ms); no uneven frames either way. (The script's launch check found no log line because it started the app without a ROM, so no Vulkan context: not a failure)
- [x] Precise smart sync (from gopher64, which marks the exact bytes the RDP draws and checks each CPU access against them): our smart sync locks whole 4 KB pages, and most games hit them every frame (SM64 587, OoT 599 waits per 600), so it costs as much as plain sync (Flip ~20 fps vs async). Likely cause: pages shared between a framebuffer's edge and other data (SM64's z-buffer starts at 0x400, the page of the CPU exception vectors). Core `N64_RDP_SMART_DEBUG=1` now counts faults inside the drawn bytes vs only on a shared page; `N64_RDP_SMART_PRECISE=1` lets shared-page faults through without the GPU wait (that page only). Queued on the Portal: smart vs precise vs async, quick set, screenshots for Snap/Cruis'n/Yoshi/DK64. If precise measures near async with the readback games still right, it can replace sync on Adreno and maybe on Mali. First Portal bench (old frame flag, precise allowed for every fault): +11% over smart (OoT 390 -> 583, Paper Mario 559 -> 797, Yoshi 723 -> 1090, Cruis'n 766 -> 921, SM64 507 -> 571, MM 476 -> 511); Yoshi and DK64 still right, black in async. Bug found by the Odin 3 compat run: Cruis'n USA stayed on its boot screen (0 DL/s, its RSP looping). Cause: a copy (PI/SP DMA, the RSP, any C memcpy) faults only on its first byte; when that byte was outside the drawn bytes, the copy ran on into drawn bytes of the same page without waiting and read stale RDRAM. Fixed (core 2938164): only dynarec loads/stores (PC inside new_dynarec's code cache, at most 8 bytes) skip the wait; copies always wait. Cruis'n reaches the race, with 231 waits per 600 SyncFulls (smart: 494-599). A helper also relocks opened pages after 0.5 ms, so a later read of drawn bytes on that page waits again (a read within those 0.5 ms can still see old pixels: watch for flicker in compat). Second bug, found by the Duo compat: the relock helper protected a page before marking it locked, and a fault in between was passed on as a crash (OoT, Banjo-Tooie); fixed (core 6777ab9), both pass. Portal with the fixed core, 14 games, median of 2: precise +11.9% over smart (OoT 384 -> 581, Paper Mario 529 -> 790, Yoshi 714 -> 1028, Cruis'n 772 -> 920, DK64 434 -> 498, SM64 509 -> 566; the rest within 2%), async +30.1% but Yoshi black; precise screenshots match smart. Flip with sync forced on, same set: precise +8.0% over smart (OoT 82 -> 112, Cruis'n 152 -> 189, MM 66 -> 77, Yoshi 126 -> 143, Paper Mario 128 -> 143, DK64 97 -> 108, SM64 108 -> 116; the rest within 2%), async +22.9%. The Flip runs async by default, so there precise only matters for the games on its sync list (Cruis'n among them). Full Duo compat with skip + precise (core 6777ab9): 82/82 ok, screenshots match smart sync apart from timing (RE2, Yoshi, DK64, Snap right). **Default since core 92fe5c3 wherever smart sync runs (`N64_RDP_SMART_PRECISE=0` turns it off).** Check with the bundled build, Portal, uncapped from Joey's states, all three off -> default: OoT 351 -> 582 fps (+66%), Yoshi 511 -> 930 (+82%)
- [x] FPS counter, top right (top left is for RetroAchievements popups): `60 FPS | 20 DL/s | 100% SPEED` = emulated frames, frames the game drew (display lists; repeats not counted), speed vs the game's rate (fast-forward 4x: `240 FPS | ... | 400% SPEED`). The stats line's "uneven" counts gaps that are not a whole number of game frames when repeats are skipped. Replaced 2026-11-07 by Settings › On screen's stats overlay (Off by default; Simple, Full, Custom; hotkey button + X); the old counter shows only with `N64_BENCH=1` and Stats Off
- [ ] Profile where each frame's time goes, parallel-RDP (the GLideN64 half is parked: parallel-RDP is the only performance target), Flip/Duo/Odin 3: emu thread split (dynarec, RSP, RDP commands, VI/scanout, present), GPU time (`PARALLEL_RDP_BENCH`), simpleperf hot functions; compare game draw rate vs presents vs VI rate, not just fps. Done 2026-11-04 with the new defaults (simpleperf record + stat per thread, from the bench states): Portal: the RSP is the top CPU cost, its vector helpers (RSP_VMADN etc., one call per vector instruction from the Lightning JIT, already NEON) take 21-37% of the emulation thread; get_jit_block cost comes from IMEM overlay swaps. Kernel time (12-20%) was the RDP command worker being woken ~19,600 times a second (Duo, SM64): fixed by lazy waking. Duo: GPU-bound at ~276 fps. Flip: GPU-bound in heavy games (emulation thread only ~55% busy in GoldenEye and Perfect Dark). `PARALLEL_RDP_BENCH` GPU times work on Adreno but come back empty on the Flip's Mali-G52 (likely no timestampComputeAndGraphics; unchecked), so the Flip's GPU split needs another way (Mali counters, or stat-based)
- [ ] Lazy waking of parallel-RDP's command worker (core, uncommitted; the duo-faux-lazy compat run passed, the open question is the Portal losses and adaptive waking; `N64_RDP_RING_LAZY=0` turns it off): the writer wakes a sleeping worker only once 1024 words are queued, or when the work must run soon (signal_timeline, flush, wait_for_timeline, drain, full ring, teardown); the worker's 500 us timed wait covers the rest. Uncapped, 14 games, vs off: Portal +8% geomean (Perfect Dark 494 -> 586, Conker 162 -> 190), Flip +4.3% (Mario Kart 64 +14%, Majora's Mask +13.5%, Cruis'n USA +12%, DK64 +9%), Duo same ~276 fps (GPU-bound) with emulation-thread CPU 9.26 -> 7.59 s per 10 s. A spin-before-sleep variant gave nothing and was removed. Portal OoT after a reboot, 3 launches each: lazy waking is 3-4% slower there (inline on: 597 vs 621; off: 593 vs 610), so the per-game Portal numbers need a recheck before it becomes the default. Likely why (Duo off-CPU profile, OoT, 2026-11-04): the emulation thread spends 15.9% of its time waiting for the GPU at smart sync, every frame (600 waits per 600 SyncFulls): OoT reads its own depth buffer each frame (515 of 600 waits are reads inside drawn depth bytes; likely the sun/lens-flare check, so the wait is needed). Lazy waking starts the GPU on a frame's commands later, so that wait gets longer. Possible fix: wake the worker early in games that wait at every SyncFull, or submit sooner when a wait is expected. A tried fix that did nothing: letting the controller (PIF) DMA through when its 64 bytes miss the drawing (85 of the 600 waits came from it) only moved the wait to the next depth read, 338 -> 338 fps; patch kept in the scratchpad (rspjit/pif-copy-range.patch), not in the core. Duo compat with faux123 + lazy waking: 82/82 ok, no rendering differences vs bundled + lazy, and faux123 draws Mario Party 2's text boxes clean. Default once the Duo compat run (`duo-faux-lazy`) passes; then commit, rebuild the bundled core, install on all devices Joey 2026-11-06: ship it on by default with a per-game off list in the game DB (OoT, GoldenEye, Paper Mario, the games that lost on the Portal), committed and rebundled after compat runs on the Portal and Flip. Catch found after asking: these numbers are on the old command ring; gpu2 (the core that is to ship) rewrote the ring and has no lazy waking, so it first has to be ported onto gpu2 and measured again there.
 Portal 14 games x2 rounds (portal-rerun4, 2026-11-04): lazy +6.4% geomean (Mario Kart +26%, Perfect Dark +20%, Banjo +15%, Cruis'n +15%) but Pokemon Snap -15%, GoldenEye -6%, Paper Mario -5%, OoT -3%: the games where the CPU waits on GPU results. Trying adaptive waking (core, uncommitted, `N64_RDP_RING_ADAPT=0` turns it off): after a real wait (wait_for_timeline on unfinished work) the writer wakes eagerly for the next 65,536 words; bench portal-adapt stopped about halfway when the Portal's battery ran out (2026-11-04); rerun it in full once the Portal is charged and back (the partial log is not used). Inline RSP JIT on the same run: +2.5% alone, worst game -0.1% (Flip +1.6%, no losses). Adaptive waking, full Portal run (2026-11-04, `bench/adapt.txt`, 14 games x 2; geomeans leave out Cruis'n, whose state stops on its Controller Pak notice): lazy alone +7.3% over lazy off, lazy + adaptive +4.0%, so adaptive costs 3.0% overall. It does what it was for in OoT (558 -> 619 fps, back to lazy-off's 624) but loses Mario Kart 64's gain (791 -> 628, lazy off 602), Banjo -8%, Yoshi -4%. Pokemon Snap no longer loses with lazy waking (1467 vs 1449 off; the -15% was before repeat-frame skipping). So: lazy waking on, adaptive off, and OoT (or games that wait at every SyncFull) handled per game or by a narrower trigger than 'any real wait'.
- [ ] RSP speed ideas, from the profile (the RSP vector helpers are the top CPU cost): (1) a NEON vector JIT, emitting vector ops inline instead of a helper call per instruction (ares and gopher64 also call helpers); (2) cache JIT blocks per IMEM overlay chunk so overlay swaps stop re-looking up/recompiling (get_jit_block); (3) a threaded RSP like Project64's "Rsp Multi Threaded" (off by default there, no game in its RDB uses it: timing risk). Order: (2) then (1); (3) last. (1) built 2026-11-04 (core, uncommitted): 13 vector ops (VMULF/VMUDL/VMUDM/VMUDN/VMUDH, VMACF/VMADL/VMADM/VMADN/VMADH, VADD, VSUB, VMOV) emitted inline as NEON, the accumulator kept in registers across back-to-back ops, plus a one-memcpy-per-row RSP DMA. `N64_RSP_VU_INLINE=0` turns it off, `N64_RSP_VU_OPS=<hex opcode mask>` picks ops. Checked: device tests vs the C helpers (1,040,000 single ops, 10,000 chains, 20,000 DMAs, 0 mismatches), and OoT's RDRAM hashed every 30 frames is identical with it on and off for ~13,000 frames. It cuts the OoT emulation thread's instructions 8.7%, but that thread is busy only ~45% of the time on the Portal (it waits on the GPU side), so fps barely moves: Portal OoT +0.7% (597 vs 593, 3 launches each); Flip Majora's Mask 120 -> 127, OoT and SM64 flat. Flip Mario Tennis match-start dip (2026-11-05, 3 rounds each): no difference (56.2 vs 56.8 fps in the dip window, same emulation time). A bug fixed on the way: the raw code went in after Lightning's instruction-cache flush (now flushed again). The earlier 'OoT -10%' was the Portal itself: before a reboot it had a launch-to-launch slow mode (~520 vs ~580 fps, either build); after the reboot 12 of 12 launches were normal. Portal 2-round results from before the reboot (Conker +6.9%, MK64 +2.5%) need a rerun. Keep or drop: Joey 2026-11-05: test first (where the CPU limits: Flip uncapped quick set, Portal Conker/MK64 rerun), then ask again. Queued 2026-11-05 08:5x: Flip after its compat run (`chain39_flip_neon.sh`, `flip-neon2`, 14 games x 2, app-speed4, with Mali GPU busy per run, so games where the GPU is not the limit show what the CPU saving is worth) and Portal after its queue (`chain11_portal_next.sh`, `portal-neon2`). Flip result (2026-11-05 09:4x-10:20, `flip-neon2`, 14 games x 2, uncapped, NEON vector JIT on (default) vs off): 0.996 geomean, so no gain on the Flip. Per game (mean of 2): Banjo-Kazooie +3.1% (172 vs 167, both rounds), Majora's Mask -3.8% (120 vs 125, both rounds), MK64 -2.2%, Cruis'n -2.8% (rounds overlap: 204/190 vs 197/208), the other 10 within 0.5%. The Flip's GPU was busy 74-92% of the time, at its 768 MHz cap in most games, in both variants, so the GPU limits it and a CPU saving can't show. Portal run still queued (`portal-neon2`); then ask Joey. (2) sized from the Portal OoT profile: get_jit_block is 1.8% of the app's CPU samples, so caching blocks per overlay can win at most that much; parked behind the GPU-side work (the Portal emulation thread waits more than half the time) Portal result (2026-11-05 17:1x, `portal-neon2`, 14 games x 2, uncapped): 0.9997 geomean, every game within 0.5%, but it doesn't count: the whole run had the Portal's GPU stuck at its lowest clock (220 MHz, the Portal slow mode, see the Portal GPU clock item), so the GPU set the speed, as on the Flip. Rerun queued with the GPU at its normal clock (`portal-neon3`, `chain75_portal_reruns.sh`, after the Portal's v3 queue). The keep/drop question goes to Joey after that. Joey 2026-11-06: decide keep/drop after the clean Portal rerun (`portal-neon3`). Decided 2026-11-06: (1) stays off and unmerged (core branch `speed` keeps it). `portal-neon3` (app-speed4, inline vs `N64_RSP_VU_INLINE=0`, uncapped, 14 games x 2 rounds): geomean -0.09%, Conker +1.5%, Cruis'n USA +2.8%, Yoshi's Story -2.4%, the rest within 2%; Joey: "Keep it off".

With 2 rounds a variant can land on the slow level twice by chance, so a single Duo game can show a fake +/-40%. Guess, not checked: the emulation thread lands on a different CPU core cluster (or clock) per launch. Before trusting a Duo per-game number: log the CPU core and clock of the emulation thread per run, then pin it or use more rounds. The geomeans over 14 games are less affected, but still carry it. Check whether the Odin 3 and Portal do the same Logging started 2026-11-04 ~20:55 (Joey: 'Yes, add it'), from duo-idle on: a sampler beside the queue (scratchpad `tools/cpumon.sh --dev dff82f98`, device side `cpumon-dev.sh`) writes every 2 s the emulation thread (the app thread with the most CPU time), the CPU it last ran on, its CPU ticks, and each cluster's current/max clock to `results/cpumon-duo.tsv`; `tools/cpujoin.py <tsv> <tag> 3` joins it to each bench run (device clock ~3 s ahead). It runs outside the bench scripts (not editing scripts in use) and avoids devclean's patterns. Duo clusters: cpu0-2 max 1.50 GHz, cpu3-6 2.07 GHz, cpu7 2.15 GHz (governor walt); the first samples had the emulation thread on cpu7 at ~100% of a core. Costs a little CPU every 2 s; no app change First result (2026-11-04, duo-uber and duo-idle runs): not the CPU core. Both GoldenEye levels had the emulation thread on cpu7 (the fastest core) with every cluster at its max clock; the fast runs (~370 fps) had it busy 81% of the time, the slow ones (~283 fps) 58%. So in a slow run the emulation thread waits more, most likely on the GPU. The sampler now also logs the GPU clock, max and busy % (kgsl; the idle Duo sat at 975 of 1150 MHz) from duo-gpusw on, to see whether the slow level is a lower GPU clock
- [ ] Idle-loop skipping beyond `b .; nop` (from the emulator pass): first a dev counter in new_dynarec (where the CPU is at each event) to see which games spend time in loops; then, best match first: PCSX2's two MIPS rules (wait loops that only read/compute; `addiu`/`bne` countdown loops skipped by the count), Dolphin/melonDS's busy-wait rule, armGBA's two-lap probe Counter built 2026-11-04 (core speed branch 9eeb941, `N64_IDLE_STATS=1`): every 600 VIs it logs the top addresses where interrupts land and which status registers are read from where, with the code words; one round per game queued after the speed bench (`bench/idle.txt`). Result, Flip and Portal (2026-11-04, one round of 14 games): the plain `b .` loops (code 1000ffff) that the dynarec already skips are where most interrupts land in SM64 (34%), Banjo-Kazooie (32%), DK64 (18-30%), MK64 (8-20%), Smash (17%), Snap (15%), Yoshi (11%), so those are covered. Other top sites are `mtc0 Status` (interrupts taken the moment the OS re-enables them, not loops) and short OS routines. The one clear new candidate: Paper Mario, 27-28% of interrupts at 80026054 `sw v1,0(a0); j 80026054; nop`, a loop that only stores the same value: skippable by PCSX2's wait-loop rule (no loads, the same store each lap). Next, once every queued device run is done (Joey 2026-11-04: "Wait for all runs"): a dynarec rule for loops of identical stores + jump to self, measured on Paper Mario; then look at MK64's 800d34a8 (`sw`/`lw` of one stack slot + `bnez`, 26% on the Portal) and Perfect Dark's 7004a0c0. Duo idle stats (2026-11-04) match the Flip: Paper Mario's store loop at 80026054 has 28.0% of interrupts (Flip 28.2%), so the candidate holds on Adreno too. Odin 3 idle stats (2026-11-05 03:0x, `odin3-idle`, 14 games x 1) agree: Paper Mario's store loop 27.0-28.3% of interrupts, Mario Kart 64's 800d34a8 10-27% per 5 s window; the idle stats are now in on every device **Queued 2026-11-07 as work order item (18)** (Joey: "Queue all of these"; the rule had stalled since "Wait for all runs").
- [ ] From the emulator review (emulator-review.md "Ranked ideas"), not yet tried: (1) per-game "smoother" mode for games that lagged on hardware (Project64 counter factor 1 / gopher64 CPU overclock; the core has CountPerOpDenomPot), strong devices only; (2) unknown ROM hacks inherit the base game's other settings (CountPerOp etc.) by cartridge ID (parallel-n64); the save type part is done (core 67a1fcd); (3) ares accuracy fixes to compare with ours (timer interrupt at COMPARE=0, COUNT reads, banked SRAM for Dezaemon 3D, CACHE opcode, LL/SC); (4) accuracy test ROMs (Dillonb/n64-tests, N64brew jam homebrew). (One frame less display lag, ranked idea 1, is done: core 65f2d76, 2026-11-03, ~16 ms less, see CLAUDE.md.)
- [ ] Shader compile modes (from Dolphin, read 2026-11-04): parallel-RDP's default is already Dolphin's "hybrid ubershaders" (a generic shader draws correctly while the fast specialized one compiles on a background thread). Not yet measured: (1) "exclusive ubershaders" = `PARALLEL_RDP_UBERSHADER=1` (never compiles per-game shaders: no first-launch slowdown, likely slower overall); (2) "synchronous" = `PARALLEL_RDP_FORCE_SYNC_SHADER=1` (stutters while compiling; for comparison only). Bench both against the default, warm and on a first launch. "Skip drawing" is not needed (our fallback draws correctly instead of leaving things out). "Compile shaders before starting" = the parallel-n64 pre-build port (d8fd0cb, already listed); Dolphin also uses more compile threads during that start (cores - 2) than during play (cores - 3, 1 to 4), while parallel-RDP has one compile thread: try more threads at start. First numbers (Flip, warm cache, part of the run): exclusive ubershaders are far slower on the Mali-G52, e.g. game 24 140 -> 35 fps, game 18 136 -> 16, game 01 162 -> 41; not a default anywhere slow. Full Flip/Odin 3 table to follow. Strong devices: queued 2026-11-04 on the Odin 3 and Duo after their speed benches (`bench/uber.txt` with `benchcold.sh`: default vs exclusive, each on a first launch with parallel-RDP's pipeline cache and the driver caches deleted, measured from launch, and warm; 6 games x 2). Portal when it is back. Flip full result: exclusive ubershaders -78% geomean (0.12-0.46 of default; GoldenEye 136 -> 16 fps), never an option on the Mali. Synchronous compile (`PARALLEL_RDP_FORCE_SYNC_SHADER=1`) was left out of the first queue without saying so; added 2026-11-04 as a first-launch run (`sync-cold` in `bench/uber.txt`), so the Odin 3, Portal and Duo runs compare default, exclusive and synchronous. More compile threads at start: built as a dev switch (core 29f4957 on pn64-ports, `N64_RDP_PREBUILD_THREADS=<n>`: the saved pipeline list is built on n threads of its own instead of the single worker; the launch log line now gives the wait in ms). Queued 2026-11-04 on the Flip and Odin 3 after their queues (`bench/pbthreads.txt`, app-pbthreads: 1 vs 3 vs 6 threads, cold = driver pipeline cache deleted with the list kept, as after a driver or app update; and warm). Portal and Odin 3 result (2026-11-04, `bench/uber.txt`, 2 rounds; only 5 games ran: 07 is Conker (Europe), which has no bench state): exclusive ubershaders -57% warm and -58% on a first launch on both (range -42% to -68%), so even the strongest devices lose more than half; the default on a first launch (caches deleted) is only -1.6% (Portal) and -3.4% (Odin 3) below warm over the 20 s window, so the hybrid default already hides compiling well; synchronous compile on a first launch -11.6% / -8.3%. Exclusive ubershaders are not worth it on any device we have; drop once the Duo run agrees. (A 20 s average hides single hitches; a first-launch hitch count would be the next measure if Joey notices stutter.) Duo result (2026-11-04, same set, 5 games): exclusive -56% warm, -60% on a first launch; the default on a first launch -3.8%; synchronous on a first launch -9.2% (MK64 -26%). All three strong devices agree with the Flip. Flip pre-build threads (`bench/pbthreads.txt`, 5 games x 2): more threads are worse on the Mali: the 10 pipelines built before the game starts took 2.6 s on 1 thread, 3.3 s on 3, 5.7 s on 6, and first-launch fps from launch was -7.4% (3 threads) and -36.5% (6 threads) against 1. With the driver cache kept, 3 threads vs 1 is +1.7% but swings -17 to +27% per game (noise). Keep 1 thread on the Flip; the Odin 3 run decides for Adreno. Odin 3 (2026-11-05, `odin3-pbthreads`, OoT, SM64, GoldenEye, MK64, Banjo x 2; most runs at thermal status 3): the 10 pipelines built before the game starts took 380 ms on 1 thread, 371 ms on 3, 484 ms on 6 (driver cache deleted); with the cache kept the wait is 0 ms on any count. First-launch fps from a cold cache: 3 threads -2.6% against 1 (Banjo -9.4%, OoT -7.9%, both rounds), 6 threads +2.2% (SM64 +6.2%, Banjo -3.0%); warm launches identical (+0.0%). No gain on Adreno either: keep 1 thread everywhere. Done
- [ ] GLideN64 shader cache ("Cache GPU Shaders", on by default: loads every saved shader at start, GLideN64's form of compile-before-starting): no `shaders` folder exists on the Flip, Odin 3 or Duo (checked 2026-11-04), so it is not saving. Likely why: GLideN64 writes the store only in `CombinerInfo::destroy()` (clean shutdown), and our tests force-stop the app; check what a normal exit does, then save it on pause/background too Parked 2026-11-04: Joey keeps GLideN64 as the fallback renderer but no work goes into it.
- [ ] SoftRDP (RMG 0.9.1, MIT; threaded CPU RDP, accurate to angrylion): could it be the accurate fallback for GPUs that can't run parallel-RDP (GLideN64 today)? Built for arm64 unchanged, test core `softrdp-try` e1701c6 (SoftRDP in angrylion's slot), app-srdp. Queued on the Flip and Odin 3 (`chain62_flip_srdp.sh`, after the black-screen check; `chain58_odin3_srdp.sh`): frame-600 pictures (results/srdpshot) and `bench/srdp.txt` (parallel-RDP vs angrylion vs SoftRDP auto/4 workers, 6 games x 2, uncapped). Details in emulator-review.md. Flip result (2026-11-05, `flip-srdp`, uncapped fps, average of 2 rounds): pictures right in OoT, SM64 and GoldenEye, and byte-for-byte the same as the Odin 3's (a CPU renderer gives the same picture on every device). Speed, geomean of 6 games: parallel-RDP 148 fps, angrylion 36, SoftRDP 42 (4 workers: 44), so SoftRDP is 19-24% faster than angrylion but only 28% of parallel-RDP. Per game, SoftRDP / angrylion / parallel-RDP: Banjo-Kazooie 46 / 31 / 163, GoldenEye 34 / 21 / 137, OoT 45 / 26 / 140, Mario Kart 59 / 49 / 167, Paper Mario 41 / 55 / 159, SM64 34 / 44 / 129. It is slower than angrylion in Paper Mario (-26%) and SM64 (-22%), and no game reaches 60 fps with either CPU renderer on the Flip, even with no speed cap. So on the Flip's 8 small cores a CPU RDP is not usable, and the Flip runs parallel-RDP anyway. Odin 3 bench running now (`odin3-srdp`, chain67); its answer decides whether SoftRDP is worth anything on strong CPUs Odin 3 result (2026-11-05, `odin3-srdp`, uncapped fps, median of 2 rounds): geomean of the same 6 games: parallel-RDP 810 fps, angrylion 251, SoftRDP 269 (4 workers: 251). On the Odin 3's big cores every CPU renderer runs well above 60 fps (lowest: SoftRDP 4 workers in GoldenEye, 212), but SoftRDP is only 7% faster than angrylion (Flip: 19-24%) and a third of parallel-RDP. Per game, SoftRDP / angrylion / parallel-RDP: Banjo-Kazooie 310 / 303 / 628, GoldenEye 237 / 263 / 1049, OoT 299 / 212 / 700, Mario Kart 298 / 294 / 766, Paper Mario 257 / 228 / 1010, SM64 227 / 223 / 792. No hitches with either CPU renderer (no frame over 33 ms after the first 5 s). Verdict: SoftRDP is not worth adding. Where a GPU can't run parallel-RDP, angrylion (already in the core) is about as fast, and on the weak device that would need it most (the Flip) neither CPU renderer reaches 60 fps.
- [ ] parallel-n64's HLE geometry for parallel-RDP (new 2026-11, ~26,000 lines): the display list is turned into RDP commands on the CPU, so the RSP microcode (our top CPU cost) does not run, while parallel-RDP still draws. Measured try on the Flip plus a compat run; risks in emulator-review.md

Queued 2026-11-04 on the Flip and Odin 3 (`bench/speed.txt`, app-speed4: core speed branch 3d58f18 + app 01c5971, 14 games x 2 rounds, against the same build with no switch). Result, Flip and Portal (2026-11-04, `bench/speed.txt`, % vs no switch, 14 games x 2): `KICK_PASSES=2,KICK_US=250` median +0.5% Flip, -0.1% Portal; single outliers only (Flip DK64 +6.0%, Portal MK64 +7.4%), so no gain shown yet; speed2's kick settings and the Odin 3/Duo runs still to come. Flip speed2 (2026-11-04, `bench/speed2.txt`, 14 games x 2, geomean of per-game medians; the default's two rounds agree within 4%): kick after 4 passes +0.2%, 1 pass/100 us +0.2%, 250 us alone -0.1%: no setting helps on the Mali either Duo speed (2026-11-04, `bench/speed.txt`, 14 games x 2, geomean): kick +2.5% (median +0.1%; Cruis'n +22% is the Duo's two-level noise) Duo speed2 (2026-11-05 02:29, `duo-speed2`, 14 games x 2; plain geomeans of +3-7% are the Duo's two speed levels landing on random variants, e.g. Wave Race 485 vs 753 fps; compared on each variant's slower round with the jumping games left out): kick after 4 passes -0.1%, 1 pass/100 us +1.0%, 250 us +0.4%: no gain on the Duo either Odin 3 speed (2026-11-05 02:4x, `odin3-speed`, 14 games x 2, geomean of per-game means; the default's rounds agree within 2%): kick (2 passes / 250 us) +0.3% (0.996-1.018): no gain on any device Portal speed2 (2026-11-05 04:2x, `portal-speed2`, 14 games x 2; the default's rounds agree within 1.3%): kick after 4 passes +0.2%, 1 pass/100 us +0.7%, 250 us +0.4%; one steady per-game gain: 1 pass/100 us gives Mario Kart 64 +8% (714 vs 660, both rounds) and 250 us +5%; others flat. Not a default (headroom only; MK64 is 660 fps uncapped) Odin 3 speed2 (2026-11-05 11:2x, `odin3-speed2`, 14 games x 2, uncapped; the default's rounds agree within 3%; the Odin 3 sat at thermal status 3, severe, after most runs, the same for every variant): kick after 4 passes +0.2%, 1 pass/100 us +0.4%, 250 us +0.4%, no game moves more than 3%. Done: no setting gains on any of the four devices, so the remember-last-frame version is not built
- [ ] Spin during readback waits (PCSX2 "Spin CPU/GPU during readbacks"): busy-wait briefly on smart-sync waits instead of sleeping, and/or keep the GPU busy so it does not clock down; measure on OoT (waits every frame) Built (core 3d58f18): `N64_RDP_WAIT_SPIN=<us>` (emulation thread polls the GPU timeline that long before sleeping) and `N64_RDP_FENCE_SPIN=<us>` (the fence thread polls the GPU fence before a blocking wait, so the RDRAM copy-back starts sooner). Not the same as the dropped spin-before-sleep (that was the command worker waiting for work). Queued 2026-11-04 on the Flip and Odin 3 (`bench/speed.txt`, app-speed4: core speed branch 3d58f18 + app 01c5971, 14 games x 2 rounds, against the same build with no switch). More settings queued 2026-11-04 on all four devices after their other runs (`bench/speed2.txt`, app-speed4, 14 games x 2): `N64_RDP_WAIT_SPIN=100` and `=1000`, `N64_RDP_FENCE_SPIN=1000`. Result, Flip and Portal (2026-11-04, `bench/speed.txt`, % vs no switch, 14 games x 2): `WAIT_SPIN=300` +0.1% / +0.4%, `FENCE_SPIN=300` +0.3% / +0.3% median: noise (Portal's +3-6% in DK64/MM/PD showed for every switch, so its no-switch run was just slow there). Odin 3/Duo and speed2 still to come. Flip speed2 (2026-11-04, `bench/speed2.txt`, 14 games x 2, geomean of per-game medians; the default's two rounds agree within 4%): wait spin 100 us +0.2%, 1000 us +0.1%; fence spin 1000 us -2.2% (game 12 -8.9%): spinning longer only costs Duo speed (2026-11-04, `bench/speed.txt`, 14 games x 2, geomean): wait spin +0.5%, fence spin -1.7% (medians -0.1%); the +/-22-28% single games are the Duo's two levels Duo speed2 (2026-11-05 02:29, `duo-speed2`, 14 games x 2; plain geomeans of +3-7% are the Duo's two speed levels landing on random variants, e.g. Wave Race 485 vs 753 fps; compared on each variant's slower round with the jumping games left out): wait spin 100 us -0.2%, 1000 us -1.2%, fence spin 1000 us +0.9%: no gain Odin 3 speed (2026-11-05 02:4x, `odin3-speed`, 14 games x 2, geomean of per-game means; the default's rounds agree within 2%): wait spin 300 us +1.1% (worst Mario Kart 64 -0.6%), fence spin 300 us 0.0% but Smash -7.2% in both rounds (661 vs 709). Banjo-Kazooie gains in both rounds with either: wait spin +10.4% (540 vs 489), fence spin +13.7%; Banjo waits at every SyncFull (600 of 600). Banjo with wait spin elsewhere: Portal +3.6% (470 vs 454, both rounds), Flip and Duo none (Flip has no smart sync in Banjo). Not made a default: the gain is headroom Banjo doesn't need at 60 fps on these devices (489 fps uncapped on the Odin 3), and the spin burns CPU while it waits; kept as a per-game option if a game is short of speed Portal speed2 (2026-11-05 04:2x, `portal-speed2`, 14 games x 2; the default's rounds agree within 1.3%): wait spin 100 us 0.0%, 1000 us +0.4% (Banjo +10% but Yoshi's Story -5%, Smash -3.6%); fence spin 1000 us +2.1% (Banjo +13%, 519 vs 457; MK64 +5%; SM64 -3.2%). Same pattern as the Odin 3: spins help the games that wait at every SyncFull, cost others a little Odin 3 speed2 (2026-11-05 11:2x, `odin3-speed2`, 14 games x 2, uncapped, mostly at thermal status 3): wait spin 100 us +0.4%; wait spin 1000 us +2.3% (Banjo +17%, 571 vs 488; Majora's Mask +4.4%, Yoshi's Story +3.7%, DK64 +3.3%, nothing worse than -1%), the best overall result of the spins; fence spin 1000 us -0.1% (Banjo +14.7%, Snap +5.5%, but Cruis'n USA -7.6%, Conker -7.3%, Mario Kart 64 -5.8%, DK64 -5.6%). All four devices done. Kept as switches, not a default: the gains are uncapped headroom in games that already hold 60 fps, and a 1 ms spin keeps a big core busy while it waits (heat and battery at 60 fps unmeasured)

- [ ] Status-register polling skip (PCSX2 `IntcStat`, on by default there): on reads of MI_INTR / SP_STATUS / DPC_STATUS, jump to the next event when the CPU is just polling; dev switch first, counted alongside the idle-loop counter The dynarec's read handlers cannot safely change the cycle count (the count is saved before the call and restored after), so this has to be done when the loop is compiled (PCSX2 WaitLoop style), together with idle-loop skipping; the idle-stats counter shows which loops first. Idle stats 2026-11-04: only 21-97 status-register reads per frame on the Flip (Conker up to 200 on the Portal), mostly MI_INTR from one or two sites per game: too few for a polling skip to gain much on its own; fold into the idle-loop rule if one of those sites is a loop.
- [ ] CPU-side depth answers for depth-reading games (PPSSPP `DepthRaster`, per-game): a depth-only software rasterizer of the RDP triangles so OoT's lens-flare depth read does not wait for the GPU. Large; only after early GPU kicks and readback spin are measured

Flip speed2 (2026-11-04, `bench/speed2.txt`, 14 games x 2, geomean of per-game medians; the default's two rounds agree within 4%): nice -10 +0.3%, -19 on every thread +0.2%: no gain (checked 2026-11-05 on the Portal: Android applies it, the log says `emu thread priority -19 (ok)` and every thread of the app shows nice -19 with N64_THREAD_PRIO_ALL=1, so these runs were valid) Duo speed (2026-11-04, `bench/speed.txt`, 14 games x 2, geomean): -0.3% (median -0.3%): no gain on any device Duo speed2 (2026-11-05 02:29, `duo-speed2`, 14 games x 2; plain geomeans of +3-7% are the Duo's two speed levels landing on random variants, e.g. Wave Race 485 vs 753 fps; compared on each variant's slower round with the jumping games left out): nice -10 +0.5%, -19 on every thread 0.0%: no gain (the app's priority line is not in the Duo's logs either, so whether Android applied it is still unchecked) Odin 3 speed (2026-11-05 02:4x, `odin3-speed`, 14 games x 2, geomean of per-game means; the default's rounds agree within 2%): nice -19 -0.5% (0.984-1.002): no gain on any device Portal speed2 (2026-11-05 04:2x, `portal-speed2`, 14 games x 2; the default's rounds agree within 1.3%): nice -10 -0.2%, -19 on every thread +0.1%: no gain on any device Odin 3 speed2 (2026-11-05 11:2x, `odin3-speed2`, 14 games x 2): nice -10 -0.5%, -19 on every thread -0.4%: no gain on any of the four devices with any setting (whether Android applied the values is still unchecked). Under background load it is different: see the recheck under the review of dropped items (Odin 3 2026-11-06: per-frame run time 1.6-4.4x shorter at -19, slowest frames 12-28 -> 4.5-7.6 ms; Portal only Conker) Joey 2026-11-06: make the emulation thread at nice -19 the default on every device once the Odin 3 compat run with it (`odin3-priocompat`, chain99) is 82/82.

- [ ] Project64 game database vs mupen64plus.ini, rest of the compare: ViRefresh (44 games), Delay SI (22), and the 34 games where Project64 uses Counter Factor 2 and we use CountPerOp 1 (Smash Bros, Gauntlet Legends, Army Men...): check those games for timing problems
- [ ] RDRAM size: 3 games run with 4 MB in Project64 and 8 MB in ours (list in the research scratchpad, pj64cf.txt): check them with 4 MB

Emu thread work per frame: Balanced 3.62 ms, Max (N64_PERF_HINT=2) 3.65, Saver (N64_PIN=0) 3.79 (+5%); slowest frame, round 2: 11.7 / 12.2 / 12.8 ms. Power (USB input plus battery draw, battery full, so coarse): medians 2.92 / 2.83 / 2.75 W, within the run-to-run spread (single runs 2.6-5.4 W). No gain from Max; Joey decides on Performance mode (docs/launcher-build-plan.md, Emulation)

- [ ] Adreno GPU clock readout without root (DroidDeck kgsl_clock.c: the GPU copies its cycle and always-on counters) as a bench diagnostic for "is the GPU clocked down"

Checked 2026-11-04: already done. Granite (device.cpp init_pipeline_cache) saves the GPU pipelineCacheUUID and a hash in front of the cache and starts fresh if either differs; drivers change that UUID per build, and the Vulkan spec makes the driver reject data from another driver or GPU anyway. Nothing to build. Still to check (hakuX's other point): which thread the ~10 s pipeline-cache save runs on; hakuX saves a copy off-thread at most once a minute because live saves froze their game for 0.2-0.8 s.

- [ ] Audio time-stretch (SoundTouch, as DuckStation/PCSX2/mupen64plus-ae): keep sound in fast-forward and below full speed instead of muting/dropping; option for later. Fast-forward now has sound without it (Settings › Audio, resampled, pitch goes up). Tested 2026-11-07 on the Thor with `N64_SLOW_PCT` (the game capped below full speed), underruns per 5 s: 100% 0, 98% 22-42, 95% 79-94, 90% 127-134, 80% 230-238, so every percent below full speed is heard as gaps. Time stretch would play that slower, without the gaps, at the same pitch. It helps only where a game runs below full speed: never seen on the Thor; the Flip in heavy games (Star Road 35-40 fps), the Duo at 2x in some menus (48-51 fps). Needs SoundTouch (LGPL-2.1, a download) or a WSOLA of our own. Joey 2026-11-07: not now, tracked; bring it back if the Flip or Duo sound breaking up comes up, or with a Flip/Duo audio pass
- [ ] Audio target growth below full speed: at 98% speed the game still counts as keeping up (58.8 fps against the 58.5 threshold), so its underruns raise Auto's target 10 ms per window toward 150 ms, adding latency that can't fix a steady shortfall (seen with `N64_SLOW_PCT=98` on the Thor). Fix idea: grow only when the fill dips while the frame rate is a full 60; check on the Flip, which grows to ~70 ms at full speed
- [ ] Latency Low crackles on the Thor even in light games (10-22 underruns per 5 s at 10 ms; the Thor's output is the shared non-RAW path, 90-99 ms device latency). Check on the Portal, Odin 3, Duo (exclusive MMAP) whether Low is clean there; if not, raise Low to 15 ms
- [ ] Temporal upscaling (parallel-n64's HW_RENDER_UPSCALER_INTERFACE: motion vectors, depth, translucency mask, jittered frame; FSR2-style) as a display option; Joey designs display options
- [ ] RetroAchievements hardcore rules (flycast): stock clock/CountPerOp, no widescreen or 60 fps hacks, no cheats, no slow-motion, no state loading

### Emulator review: master test list (every idea, 2026-11-04; Joey: "make sure we don't miss testing any of them")
One line per idea from emulator-review.md. An idea leaves "Not tested" only with a measured result, a written reason it can't be tested, or Joey parking it. Details live in the item named in brackets. Order = test order.

**Not tested yet**
- [ ] 1. ADPF session covering every thread (WatermelonDS): parallel-RDP's worker, the fence thread and audio in the same session as the emu thread, target scaled in fast-forward; Portal, Odin 3, Duo (the Flip has no ADPF) [perf hint item]. Built 2026-11-04: `N64_PERF_HINT=2` adds the core's unnamed worker threads at the first thread split; while uncapped (bench, fast-forward) the target is now 1 ms (`N64_PERF_HINT_FF_US`), because the old 8.3 ms target told the scheduler a 3 ms bench frame needed less, so the earlier "no gain" runs may have under-asked. Queued on the Portal, Odin 3 and Duo after their queues (`bench/clock.txt`, app-clock.apk, capped and uncapped). Duo result 2026-11-05 (14 games x2, slower round, speed-level jumps left out): `N64_PERF_HINT=1` -1.0%, `=2` -0.4%, `N64_GPU_TURBO=1` +0.5%, `=2` +0.9% (all within noise); capped at 60 fps, slowest frames and frames over 33 ms are the same with or without hint2 or turbo2 (Conker's slowest 21.7 / 22.1 / 21.9 ms). No gain on the Duo; its GPU already reads 975 MHz, its cap, in every sample, so a clock pin has nothing to add there. Portal and Odin 3 runs still queued
- [ ] 2. GPU clock pin with a KGSL `KGSL_CONSTRAINT_PWR_MAX` context kept alive every 25 ms (DroidDeck), against `N64_GPU_TURBO`; Portal and Duo (the Duo's GPU sat at 975 of 1150 MHz in every sample of the 2026-11-04 log: its kgsl `max_pwrlevel` is 2 and `min_pwrlevel` 3, so the driver only allows 900-975 MHz; the Portal and Odin 3 have `max_pwrlevel` 0, their top clock. Duo setting `performance_mode=1`, `fan_mode=4`: probably the device's performance mode caps it; a PWR_MAX pin would only reach 975 there. Joey 2026-11-04: test the Duo as it is, mode left alone, so Duo results are at the 975 MHz cap) [GPU clock pin item]. Built 2026-11-04: `N64_GPU_TURBO=2` (app gpu_clock_pin.cpp, written from msm_kgsl.h, no DroidDeck code); the stats line now ends with the GPU clock where the app can read it. Queued with item 1. Note: adrenotools' turbo (`=1`) closes its KGSL file right after setting a device-wide property, so whether it ever took effect was never checked; the new clock column shows it. Duo 2026-11-05: turbo1 +0.5%, turbo2 +0.9%, no gain (GPU already at its 975 MHz cap); Portal and Odin 3 queued
- [ ] 3. GPU clock and load readout during benches. kgsl sysfs checked 2026-11-04: the load reading works on the Portal (67% in a run) and Odin 3, but on the Duo the kgsl busy counters never change while a game runs (`gpubusy` frozen at the same numbers), so the Duo needs DroidDeck's counter method [GPU clock readout item]
- [x] 4. PCSX2 "Spin GPU": a small busy compute job after a readback so the GPU does not clock down while it waits (only the CPU-side spins were built and measured) [spin item]. Built 2026-11-05 (core gpu2 d1f58d9): `N64_RDP_GPU_SPIN=<thousands of iterations>` submits one 64-thread busy-loop dispatch (joey/gpu_spin.comp) after every scanout. Queued on the Flip after its chunk-cache bench (`bench/spin.txt`, app-gpu5.apk, at 60 fps, 300/1000/2000 vs off, GoldenEye, Perfect Dark, Conker, MK64, OoT, SM64 x 2, clock logged); the iteration counts are guesses, since the Mali has no GPU timers Flip result (2026-11-05 03:4x-04:14, `flip-spin`, 6 games x 2 at 60 fps, clock logged): every spin holds the Mali at 768 MHz (384 MHz without, in most runs), but the counts were far too long: 300 costs ~7 ms of GPU time per scanout (GoldenEye 60 -> 43 fps, Perfect Dark -> 35; OoT, SM64, MK64, Conker stay at 60 with the same slow frames as without), 1000 and 2000 drop every game to 8-39 fps. Queued: 30 / 60 / 120 (`bench/spin2.txt`, GoldenEye, Perfect Dark, MK64, OoT) to find a spin that keeps the clock up for less than it costs Short spins (2026-11-05 04:3x-04:5x, `flip-spin2`, 4 games x 2 at 60 fps): 60 cuts slow frames with no fps loss: GoldenEye 10 -> 5 frames over 33 ms (slowest 44 -> 34-36 ms), Perfect Dark 25 -> 9 (59.0 -> 59.5 fps); 30 is in between; 120 already costs PD 5 fps (54) with more slow frames; MK64 (the recompile burst, item 23) and OoT unchanged. The clock log shows spin runs climbing to 614-768 MHz where the default sits at 384-512. Confirmation queued (`bench/spin3.txt`: 45 / 60 / 90, GoldenEye, Perfect Dark, Smash, Conker, 3 rounds); a Mali default only if it holds and compat passes (the spin changes no emulation, but costs GPU power). Confirmation (2026-11-05 04:5x-05:23, `flip-spin3`, 3 rounds at 60 fps; Smash was not in it, game 12 is DK64): it did not hold. Frames over 33 ms after the first window, three rounds added: off 49 (GoldenEye 4, Perfect Dark 42, DK64 0, Conker 3), spin 45: 29 (3, 23, 0, 3), spin 60: 50 (8, 39, 0, 3), spin 90: 78 (9, 65, 1, 3). Perfect Dark's slowest frame is worse with every spin (off 68-92 ms, spin 82-100). Spin 60's earlier halving was noise; only 45 looks better, on one game, within the round-to-round spread (PD off 12-15 per round). Dropped: no default, nothing kept (the switch stays in the gpu2 core as an experiment)
- [ ] 5. Adreno compute wave size: which one Turnip picks for parallel-RDP, and forcing the other (VK_EXT_subgroup_size_control) [wave size item]
- [x] 6. Turnip GPU priority above the compositor (hakuX KGSL draw-context priority 7), late frames with HW overlays off [Turnip priority item] Done 2026-11-07: priority 1 on the Odin 3 only, see T4.
- [ ] 7. Recompiler compile thread (PCSX-ReARMed): Flip compiles cause 4-10 ms hitches; build and measure [compile thread item] Evidence 2026-11-05 (item 23): Mario Kart 64's 95 ms hitch on the Flip is a burst of 2122 block compiles (340 ms in 5 s), and Perfect Dark's slow frames come with 2009 (273 ms). Item 23 settled that it is first-run compiling (104 of 2122 blocks invalidated, no flushes), so a compile thread or item 17's saved block list are the fixes **Step 1 result 2026-11-07 (Joey: "Yes, that way": interpreter fallback first, tested as hot-block compiling, then the thread):** built (core `drc-tier` 90b2980, `N64_DRC_HOT=<n>`, off by default: a block runs in the pure interpreter until its n-th lookup; it stops before COP0, CACHE, traps, LL/SC, FPU arithmetic, memory outside RDRAM, idle loops, unsafe delay slots and the next interrupt). Runs and draws right; no speed cost once running (`flip-drctier`: MK64 164 fps, Perfect Dark 102, GoldenEye 136-139 with and without). But it does not help the race-start burst (`flip-mkburst`, MK64 from the boot-era state, capped, 3 rounds each, identical round to round): off 4 frames over 33 ms, 262 ms of hitch, worst 90 ms, 2122 blocks / 345 ms compiling; n=2 306 ms, worst 111; n=4 284 ms, worst 72; n=16 257 ms, worst 77, and 2233-2361 blocks / 404-411 ms compiling. The burst is hot code (still compiled at n=16), and blocks entered where an interpreted stretch stopped compile as extra, overlapping blocks, so compile time goes up. So the tier stays off, and the compile thread (step 3) would mostly interpret hot code during the burst (slow frames anyway). Better fit: compile that code before it is needed, the block-list roadmap (order item 17 (a)), if MK64's race code is in RAM before frame 1271. **Step 3 first result 2026-11-07 (Joey: "Try the thread anyway"):** built (core `drc-tier` c7a0d9b, `N64_DRC_THREAD=1`, off by default, arm64): every compile runs on one worker thread; a new RDRAM block is queued with a snapshot of its code and runs in the interpreter meanwhile; the worker compiles from the snapshot and finishes (links, lists, hash table) only while the emulation thread waits at a lookup, dropping the block if the code or the TLB state changed. Other compiles (TLB-mapped code, SP memory, a block the interpreter can't start) still make the emulation thread wait. The interpreter now also runs FPU instructions except CTC1 and RDRAM loads/stores in delay slots. Unlike the tier it does help: `flip-mkthread2` (MK64 boot-era state, capped, 3 rounds each, identical round to round): frames over 33 ms in the race start 4 -> 1, hitch total 258-261 -> 46-47 ms, worst frame 88-89 -> 46-47 ms; ~5330 compiles queued, ~177 waited for (~90 ms), 0 dropped; the waits are blocks the interpreter could not start (interrupt due 50, memory outside RDRAM 20, COP0 12, FPU 7, branch 4, CACHE 2). Running on the Flip now (`chain136`): uncapped quick-set speed with and without (`flip-drcthread`), then 82-game compat with the thread on (`flip-drcthread-compat`). **Flip result and a bug (2026-11-07):** `flip-drcthread-compat` 77/82: Banjo-Tooie, F-Zero X, Jet Force Gemini, Pokemon Snap and Tony Hawk 2 died (new_dynarec's exit(1) on "branch at end of block with no delay slot", then a destroyed-mutex abort). Cause: the line pointing queued compiles at the snapshot sat inside the TLB branch of the source selection, so queued RDRAM compiles decoded live RAM while the game changed it (so the MK64 numbers above are from that build too). Speed on that build (`flip-drcthread`, uncapped, 14 games x 2): -1.2% geomean, MK64 -7.1%, Conker -10.5%, the rest within -1.9..+5.3%. Fixed in core `drc-tier` 0c49642: the snapshot line moved after the chain; a queued compile that meets a sequence new_dynarec can't compile is dropped instead of ending the app, and that address then compiles at once on the normal path (with check_addr); no lock at a lookup when nothing is queued or ready. Such compiles still happen on the snapshot (Banjo-Tooie 27, Jet Force Gemini 11 in a run), most likely because queued compiles skip check_addr and run past already compiled code into data. Smoke test on the Flip: both of those games ok. Rerun on all three devices with app-drcthread3 (app bc87319 + core f84d56 = 0c49642, built in worktree joey64-wt-bc87): Flip `chain139` (MK64 hitch `flip-mkthread3`, speed `flip-drcthread3`, compat `flip-drcthread3-compat`), Duo `chain140` (speed, compat), Odin 3 `chain141` (MK64 hitch, speed, compat). The first Duo and Odin 3 runs (`chain137`, `chain138`) were stopped: they ran the buggy build. **Duo (`chain140`, 2026-11-07):** compat 80/80 ok (74 and 75 lost to "push failed", rerun queued as `chain143`); speed (14 games x 2) -1.5% geomean: Conker -33% (229 -> 154 fps, both rounds), the rest -2.0..+3.5% (GoldenEye +9.7% and Smash +15.5% are one fast round each). Why Conker: it compiles ~24,000 blocks a second and with the thread every one is waited for (1.48-1.67 s of each 5 s handing blocks over); compile on the calling thread when it would wait anyway, and DuckStation's interpreter fallback for often-recompiled blocks, are the fixes to try. Prior art found in the second pass: Cemu's recompiler thread (the interpreter marks addresses it reaches, whole functions compile with many entry points and go in without stopping the interpreter). **Flip (`chain139`, 2026-11-07):** Mario Kart 64 race start (`flip-mkthread3`, capped, 3 rounds each): thread off 4 hitches after boot, worst 88.7-89.0 ms (262 ms in all); thread on 1 hitch, worst 44.3-48.3 ms, in every round. So the thread does what it is for. Odin 3 (`odin3-mkthread3`): no hitches after boot either way. Flip compat 82/82 with the thread on. Flip speed (uncapped, 14 x 2) -1.1% geomean: Conker -13.2%, Mario Kart -7.4%, Snap +6.4%, Cruis'n +4.9%, the rest -2.4..+3.1%. **Conker fix (core `drc-tier` d169167, 2026-11-07):** a compile the emulation thread has to wait for (all TLB-mapped code, such as Conker's, and the compile-at-once cases) now runs on the emulation thread, since the worker is idle by then, instead of a hand-off and a wait. app-drcthread4 (app bc87319 + core 2db6a1) queued: Duo `chain144` (speed, then compat), Flip `chain145` (MK64 hitch `flip-mkthread4`, speed), Odin 3 `chain146` (speed). Odin 3 `chain146` done: uncapped geomean +0.2% over the 14-game set (game 23 +2.8%, the rest within +-0.9%), but worst frames worse in 40 (8.6 -> 23-31 ms) and 59 (12.8 -> 19 ms). Duo `chain144` done: geomean +0.2% (18 +19.8%, 59 -13.1%, the rest within -1.5..+2.8%), compat 82/82 with N64_DRC_THREAD=1. Flip `chain145` (app-drcthread4): MK64 race start (`flip-mkthread4`, capped, 3 rounds) worst frame after boot 88.4-90.2 ms off, 44.6-45.3 ms with the thread, every round (the ~300 ms frame 0 is boot, both ways); speed (`flip-drcthread4`, uncapped, 14 x 2) -0.40% geomean (game 09 +10.4%, game 27 -6.8%, the rest -2.4..+1.3%). **Default (2026-11-08, Joey: "your call on compile thread default"): on for Mali only**, where the hitches are (Flip); Adreno keeps it off (no hitches there, Odin 3 worst frames worse in games 40 and 59, Duo game 59 -13.1%). App `drc-default` bb49379 (audit2/fixes + `N64_DRC_THREAD=1` on Mali unless `--es env` sets it) with core `dd-drc` fd9f40e (dd-lazy + drc-tier, clean merge, md5 e1fa2a): Duo smoke 3/3 ok with the thread off (`duo-drc1-smoke`); Flip 82-game compat `chain151` (`flip-drc1-compat`, 2026-11-08 04:45): **82/82 ok**, the thread's log line in 81 (game 67 runs the cached interpreter by its per-game rule, as it should); the Frontend session told it can merge drc-default 04c51c3 with core dd-drc fd9f40e.
- [ ] 8. Idle loops beyond `b .; nop`: dev counter first, then PCSX2's FF-block and timeout-loop rules, Dolphin/melonDS busy-wait rule, armGBA two-lap probe; waits for all runs (Joey) [idle-loop item]
- [ ] 9. parallel-n64's HLE geometry for parallel-RDP (RSP microcode skipped): Flip try plus a compat run [HLE geometry item] First look queued 2026-11-05 on the Flip after the MK64 trace: the libretro/parallel-n64 core built as is (NDK 27.3) inside the Joey64 app (`bench/hle.txt`, app-pn64core.apk), its RSP LLE vs HLE on 6 games, our core for scale; the port waits on that result. Found 2026-11-05: the Flip's GPU (devfreq 60000000.gpu, governor simple_ondemand) is allowed 384-768 MHz while 850 MHz exists (like the Duo's cap). Result (2026-11-05 00:49, `flip-cap`, 14 games x 1, clock sampled each second, first 10 s left out): uncapped, the GPU sits at its 768 MHz cap almost the whole time (GPU-bound at the cap; 850 never used). Those hold 60 fps with no frame over 33 ms after the first window, so the low clock costs nothing there and saves battery. Where frames run slow at 60 fps after warm-up: GoldenEye (slowest 47 ms, 7 frames over 33 ms, clock 384-512 most of the time), Perfect Dark (54.7 ms, 17 over, clock mostly 614), Conker (41 ms, 1). MK64's 95 ms hitches show both capped and uncapped (4 and 13 over 33 ms), so they are not the clock. So: yes, the clock drops at 60 fps, and GoldenEye / Perfect Dark are the candidates for a keep-the-clock-up fix (item 4); MK64's hitch needs its own look (new item 23). Mali tuning inside parallel-RDP: SMALL_TYPES off cost the Flip 12.9%, so its shaders are sensitive to data width; read which sizes (workgroup, tile, binning) parallel-RDP fixes at build time and whether an 8-wide Bifrost GPU wants others (unchecked). Read 2026-11-05: parallel-RDP fixes the workgroup size at 64 for its helper passes and runs one workgroup per 8x8 tile in the main passes (rasterizer, depth/blend; the shaders take the size from the host, nothing in them assumes 8x8); tile binning groups tiles 8 wide per subgroup. An 8x8 tile is 64 threads: 8 Bifrost warps, but only half of a 128-wide Adreno wave, so this matters on Adreno too. Built (core gpu2 b6b6831, `-DJOEY_RDP_TILE_W=16` = 16x8 tiles, 128 threads; app-tile16.apk) and queued on all four devices at the end of their queues (`bench/tile.txt`, 14 games x 2, uncapped; check screenshots) Flip result (2026-11-05 02:17-02:5x, `flip-tile`, 14 games x 2): 16x8 tiles +0.2% geomean (0.93-1.06 per game), pictures the same; the first 16x8 round hitches while its shader variants compile (one-off). No gain on the Mali; Adreno devices still to come. Duo result (2026-11-05 07:0x-07:4x, `duo-tile`, 14 games x 2, each variant's slower round, two-level noise rule): 16x8 tiles 0.999 geomean over 11 games (0.965-1.034); left out: Cruis'n USA (static menu state) and game 40 (1.36 / 1.50, both on the Duo's two speed levels) and Smash (0.79, one tile16 round on the low level). Pictures the same (OoT, Conker, SM64 checked side by side). No gain on the Duo either; Odin 3 and Portal still queued **Portal (2026-11-07, `chain118`, 14 games x 2 unless noted; queued 2026-11-05, run only now because of the battery rule):** `portal-tile` (app-gpu6 vs app-tile16, uncapped): 16x8 tiles +4.1% geomean; Yoshi's Story +17% in both rounds (494 vs 422 fps), DK64 +9% (one round: 493/412 vs 413/414), Cruis'n USA +34% (one round, its usual swing), the rest -4..+5%. The Duo was flat (0.999), so only Yoshi on the Portal is a repeatable gain; not a default on its own. Odin 3 run still to do if this item continues.
- [x] 22. Flip dips (41-50 fps in heavy scenes): find what present/acquire blocks on; the present thread lifted them to ~55 but added 8-13 ms lag [present thread item]. From the traces already run (2026-11-05, app-gpu6, `N64_HITCH_LOG=1`, at 60 fps): every slow frame in GoldenEye (5 frames), Perfect Dark (25) and MK64 (8) is emulation time inside the core's frame; acquire and present are 0.0 ms. Only uncapped Perfect Dark shows acquire time (150-184 ms over ~48 slow frames, vs ~1750 ms of emulation). So at 60 fps the Flip does not block in acquire or present in those games. Mario Tennis, the original dip scene (2026-11-05 04:2x-05:3x, `flip-dip22`, boot into a match, app-gpu6, hitch log and dynarec stats, 5 s windows from the menu to 15 s into the match; screenshots show the match): on today's build the dip is one window at match start: 51.7 / 55.6 / 56.3 fps (3 rounds), every other window 58.8-60.3. Inside it are ~3 s of 35-46 ms frames, all emulation time (per round 36-60 slow frames: emulation 1442-2765 ms, acquire 63-192 ms, present 0). Dynarec compiling is small there (1197 blocks, 175 ms in 5.8 s), and the scene's old profile has the RSP vector helpers on top of the emulation thread (VMADN 6.8%, VMADH 4.9%, VMADM 3.1%...). The present thread (2 rounds) gives the same dip (55.3 / 55.6) and raises poll->display from ~22-30 to ~43-52 ms, so it no longer helps. Answer: the Flip's dips are the emulation thread being too slow for the scene (RSP-heavy), not acquire or present. NEON RSP vector JIT on/off on the same scene (2026-11-05 05:3x-05:47, `flip-dipneon`, app-speed4, 3 rounds each, alternating): no difference. Dip window on 56.6 / 55.7 / 56.4 fps, off 57.0 / 56.9 / 56.6; emulation time per frame the same (dip ~10.5-11.1 ms, match ~8.9 ms both). So the RSP vector helpers are not what limits this scene on the current build (the old profile is from 2026-11-03, before smart sync, repeat frames and the parallel-RDP update). Fresh profile (2026-11-05 05:5x, `flip-dipprof`, app-drcinv, simpleperf 16 s across match start + 10 s in the match): the emulation thread is on the CPU only ~56% of the time even in its busiest 3 s (match: 52%), with the same top symbols as the match (RSP VMADN/VMADH/MTC0, get_jit_block, RDP command processing) plus dynarec compiling (new_recompile_block 18 ms/s, absent in the match). Yet those frames take 36-46 ms. So during the dip the emulation thread mostly waits, off the CPU, inside the core's frame (a GPU or worker-thread wait), which is why making the RSP faster changed nothing. Off-CPU profile (2026-11-05 05:5x, `flip-dipoff`, simpleperf --trace-offcpu, dwarf stacks): stacks through the core's code don't unwind, so only the frontend's waits are named. On the emulation thread the one wait present in the dip and absent in the match is `VkContext::beginFrame` blocked in the Mali driver (ppoll on our blit fence from two frames back): 3.6% of the 16 s, ~0.6 s, mostly inside the ~3 s dip. Audio-sync sleep drops from 45.6% (match) to 34.8%. So at match start the Mali GPU falls behind and the CPU waits for it, i.e. the dip is GPU-bound. Mali clock through the dip (2026-11-05 06:1x, `flip-dipclk`, malimon each second, logcat in epoch time, 2 rounds): the governor is too slow. Both rounds enter the dip window at the bottom clock, 384 MHz, from the menu, and take ~2 s to reach 768 (384, 384, 614/512, then 768); that window is 55.7 / 56.0 fps. The next window is already at 768 for 3 s and nearly back to 60 (58.8 / 59.0), and the match then settles at 512. So the dip is the first ~2 s of a heavier scene running at the low clock. Testing whether holding the clock up removes it: `chain34_flip_dipspin.sh` (GPU spin 60 vs off, 3 rounds each, clock logged). Spin result (2026-11-05 06:2x-06:4x, `flip-dipspin`, 3 rounds each, alternating): spin 60 does not lift the clock (still 384 MHz through the menu and into the dip) and the dip is a little worse: 55.0 / 55.6 / 55.2 fps and 41-43 slow frames, vs 55.7 / 56.8 / 56.1 and 34-36 without. The spins that do hold 768 (300 and up) cost ~7 ms of GPU time per scanout (item 4), the very time this scene lacks, and an on-demand spin would switch on only once the governor is already ramping. Answered: the Flip's remaining dips are the Mali governor (simple_ondemand, 384-768 MHz) taking ~2 s to clock up when a heavier scene starts; acquire and present don't block, the present thread no longer helps, and the app has no cheap lever (devfreq limits need root). Left as is: one 5 s window at ~56 fps on a match start
- [x] 23. Mario Kart 64 frame hitches on the Flip: 95 ms slowest frame in every 5 s window, capped and uncapped (flip-cap 2026-11-05), not the GPU clock: find the cause (dynarec compile, smart-sync wait, audio?) Queued 2026-11-05 on the Flip after the spin run: `bench/hitch.txt` (app-gpu6.apk; new `N64_HITCH_LOG=1` logs each frame over 33 ms split into acquire / emulation / present, plus `N64_DRC_STATS=1`), MK64, GoldenEye, Perfect Dark, capped and uncapped. Traced 2026-11-05 04:1x (`flip-mkhitch`, `N64_HITCH_LOG=1` + `N64_DRC_STATS=1`, 2 rounds each): not periodic. In Joey's MK64 state it is one fixed event: frames 1271, 1275, 1276 and 1313 after boot (89, 94, 45, 44 ms) in both capped rounds. All of it is emulation time (acquire and present 0.0 ms), and the 5 s window holding it compiled 2122 dynarec blocks in 340 ms (the window before: 17 blocks). So the hitch is a recompile burst: ~2100 blocks needed at once, 272 ms of the 340 ms landing in those 4 frames (worst single block 6.5 ms). Perfect Dark's slow frames sit in a window with 2009 blocks / 273 ms too. Next: count invalidations to see whether that code is new or was thrown away and rebuilt (a self-modifying-code or DMA flush would be cheaper to fix than a compile thread); this is what item 7 (compile thread) would hide. Counter built (core gpu2 ccebaf2: the dynarec stats line also gives blocks invalidated, full flushes and expiry steps) and queued on the Flip after the short spins (`bench/drcinv.txt`, MK64, Perfect Dark, GoldenEye at 60 fps) Invalidation counts (2026-11-05 03:53, `flip-drcinv`, core gpu2 ccebaf2): the MK64 burst window (2122 blocks, 338 ms) invalidated only 104 blocks with no full flush; Perfect Dark's (2009 blocks) none. Expiry steps track output size (about 2.5 per block in every window) and say nothing about reuse. So the code is new: the state load starts with an empty cache, and frame 1271 is the first time the game runs it. In normal play that is a one-off hitch the first time an area's code runs, not a repeat; nothing is being thrown away and rebuilt, so the cheap fix isn't there. What would hide it: a compile thread (item 7) or a saved block list (item 17). Answered. Odin 3 (2026-11-05, `odin3-hitch`, same hitch-log set, 6 games x 2; uncapped runs at thermal status 2-3): no frame over 33 ms in any game at 60 fps or uncapped. Slowest frame at 60 fps: MK64 28.9 ms in both rounds (its compile burst, a third of the Flip's 95 ms), Majora's Mask 23.5-28.6, GoldenEye 9.7-9.8, OoT 6.3-6.4, SM64 5.3-7.1, Banjo 6.9-7.2. The burst still exists on the Adreno but fits inside two frames

Our merged core uses push descriptors where offered and passed compat on Mali (Flip 82/82) and Turnip (Duo 82/82, Portal 82/82). Our ring is not parallel-n64's (the upstream merge takes everything queued per wake-up and copied the whole batch out of the ring before running it), so the idea is adapted: the worker walks the batch in the ring, runs each command in one piece straight from it, copies out only a command that wraps, and hands each command's space back once it has run (the processor keeps no pointer to a command's words). Checked with parallel-n64's ring test ported to our ring (core `tools/test_command_ring.cpp`, cb40e68, build line in the file; 64-word ring so commands wrap all the time, 200,000 commands of 1-44 words, each checked before and after a stretch of work): passes on the Portal for the new ring and the old. - [ ] 28. Mali shader limits per game (targeted pass Q1, emulator-review.md): malictr now logs arithmetic / load-store / texture use, full-register warps, divergence, L2 misses and read stalls; first sample (768 MHz, 96.5% busy): ALU 40%, LS 28%, 37% of warps at full registers, 28% diverged, so latency/occupancy rather than throughput. Read flip-neon2's per-run counters (all 14 games), then test what they point at: fewer registers in parallel-RDP's per-tile shaders, or spirv-opt -O over its SPIR-V. Read 2026-11-05 (`flip-neon2`, 14 games, 4 runs each, uncapped): GPU busy 74-92%; arithmetic 40-53%, load/store 20-30%, texture 6-21% (Perfect Dark the highest); warps using every register 16-38% (Conker 38, SM64 37, OoT 34, MM 33; Snap and Smash lowest, 16 and 20); diverged instructions 2.5-33% (Conker 33, MM 30; Snap 2.5); L2 read misses 16-29%; external read stalls 0.1% everywhere, so memory bandwidth is not the limit. The compute queue does 2-6x the work of the fragment queue in every game (parallel-RDP is compute shaders). No unit is near full, and the slowest games are the ones with the most full-register warps and divergence, so the next test stands: lower register use in the per-tile compute shaders (or spirv-opt -O) on the Flip, starting with Conker, SM64, OoT and MM. Built 2026-11-05 (Joey: "Yes, build it"): `devtools/spvopt.py` runs the NDK's spirv-opt (27.3, no download) over all 37 shaders embedded in parallel-RDP's slangmosh.hpp, checks each with spirv-val (Vulkan 1.1) and writes a header with the new offsets (a no-flag run reproduces the header word for word). -O: 1,736,740 -> 1,633,620 bytes (94.1%), -Os: 94.2%, all 37 valid, so the shaders were already fairly lean; whether the Mali's compiler then uses fewer registers only a run can show. Cores built from gpu2 023dd9e with each header (core-spvO / core-spvS; the header in the tree is unchanged), APKs app-spvbase / app-spvO / app-spvS from the same app commit. Correction 2026-11-05 11:2x: the first -Os core had some -O objects in it (the second build only recompiled part of parallel-RDP); rebuilt from clean objects (core md5 3d8e7068f412) and app-spvS.apk replaced before its run started; the -O core rebuilt clean is identical to the first. Portal check (SM64 and Conker from Joey's states, all three builds): pictures right (SM64 identical, Conker the same scene at a different camera moment), fps the same within noise. Queued on the Flip at the end of its queue: `bench/spv.txt`, 14 games x 2 with Mali counters (`flip-spv`) Result (2026-11-05 15:0x, `flip-spv`, 14 games x 2 rounds, uncapped, Mali counters): no gain, dropped; the shipped shaders stay as they are. Second round (warm caches): -O +0.2%, -Os +1.0% against the unchanged shaders, within the run-to-run spread (Banjo's two base rounds alone differ 3%). The Mali counters are the same to the decimal: full-register warps 25.5% in all three, diverged 19.2-19.3%, arithmetic 47.2-47.3%, GPU busy 87.5-88.3%: the driver's own compiler already does what spirv-opt does. First round: -6% for both, with 380-550 ms stalls (OoT, MK64, DK64, Paper Mario, Perfect Dark, Conker) because the changed shaders miss the saved pipeline cache and compile again: what a shader update costs once on every device, not a speed difference. Adreno GPU busy and clock from inside the app (targeted pass Q3): GpuClockPin's own KGSL context reserves RBBM counters and copies them with CP_REG_TO_MEM (DroidDeck's method; kgsl blocks reading them from outside: perfcounter=0 on Duo, Portal, Odin 3). Aim: see what the Duo waits on in its slow-level runs. tools/duogpu.sh logged the Duo's GPU bus-bandwidth vote (kgsl-busmon) beside its runs: it reads 0 even while a game runs (40 samples, with gpu_busy_percentage stuck at 3% and gpubusy frozen), so the Duo's sysfs gives nothing; the in-app readout is the way. Duo GPU priority rerun with the fixed hook started 09:1x (launch check: `KGSL priority hook: context 18 flags 00000013 -> 00007052 (ok)`). Built (app, `gpu_counters.cpp`, dev switch `N64_GPU_COUNTERS=1`): its own KGSL context reserves RBBM countables 0 (GPU cycles) and 6 (GPU busy) and every 100 ms a CP_REG_TO_MEM command buffer copies them and CP_ALWAYS_ON_COUNTER into memory. Portal result (2026-11-05, Adreno 740): KGSL hands out the counters (registers 0x322, 0x320) and the always-on counter moves (19.2 MHz, as expected), but the cycle and busy counters stay at 0, and so does every RBBM counter register from 0x31c to 0x32b, including KGSL's own. So the counters do not run at all on this kernel; the likely reason is that KGSL only switches them on when its perfcounter setting is 1, which needs root to change (not checked in KGSL's source). Writing the enable register from our own command buffer is not an option: the registers above 0x4ff are write-protected for apps and a write would fault the GPU. The code now notices a cycle counter stuck at 0 after 30 samples, logs it and stops. Not tried on the Duo (also Adreno 7xx, perfcounter 0, so most likely the same) while its queue runs; the Odin 3 (Adreno 830) isn't handled. **Result: this way gives nothing on our Adreno devices.** Next way to see GPU load: item 32 (Vulkan timestamps) **Duo and Odin 3 queued 2026-11-07 as work order item (23)** (Joey: "Queue all of these").
