- [ ] 33. Texture load snapshots (gopher64 bc17f40 + its parallel-RDP fork ce68763, 2026-11-05; read 2026-11-06 after Joey's "Yes" to updating the source copies). Normally the GPU reads a texture load's source from RDRAM when it runs the load, so with async parallel-RDP a CPU write made after the load was queued but before the GPU got to it leaks into the texture. gopher64 now copies each load's RDRAM range (64-byte aligned) when the load is queued and uploads TMEM from the copy; ranges the RDP still has pending writes to (render-to-texture) are read in place and marked as RDP reads instead. Our smart sync only tracks what the RDP draws (colour/depth pages, waits on CPU reads of them), not what it will still read, so async (the Mali default, fast-forward on Adreno) can show these texture races. Test: port it (needs parallel-RDP's single-threaded command processing, which the snapshot hook requires; cost to measure against our worker thread), then (a) compat on the Flip (async) for texture glitches, (b) whether `kSyncRdpGames` games (Yoshi's Story, Spider-Man, RE2, Cruis'n USA, Snap, New Tetris) run right async with it (async is faster), (c) speed. Ported 2026-11-05 (Joey: "Yes, port it"): core `texsnap` 40fe652 (gpu2 023dd9e plus gopher64's renderer part, by hand where our rdp_device.hpp differs), off by default, switch `N64_RDP_TEX_SNAPSHOT=1`, which also turns on single-threaded command processing. rdp.cpp tracks what the RDP draws (colour/depth rows inside the scissor) in every mode, tagged with the timeline value that will cover it. The filter reads a load in place when it overlaps a write not yet finished, or a page smart sync holds protected (copying such a page would fault inside the renderer). Every 600 frames the log gives loads copied / read in place. Test APK app-texsnap (core md5 3266b3). Smoke test (Flip, SM64, async): draws right at 60 fps, 10-40k loads copied per 10 s, none read in place. (b) The sync-list games forced async (`--es opts mupen64plus-parallel-rdp-synchronous=False`), snapshots off then on, compat screenshots at three points (`flip-ts-async-*`, `duo-ts-async-*`): on the Flip snapshots change nothing. Spider-Man, Yoshi's Story, Cruis'n USA and RE2 go black the same way (Cruis'n's loads drop from ~2,400/s to 0 once it stops drawing), and New Tetris and Perfect Dark look the same on and off. On the Duo the same, with one exception: **Cruis'n USA plays with snapshots on** (race on screen at the last screenshot) and is black with them off. Both devices import RDRAM the same way (no VK_EXT_external_memory_host), so on the Flip Cruis'n hits something snapshots don't cover, most likely its CPU reading back what the RDP drew, which only sync (smart sync) handles. So no game can leave `kSyncRdpGames`. These games break on CPU reads of RDP output, not texture races. None of the 12 runs read a single load in place. (c) Speed (2026-11-05, `bench/texsnap.txt`, 14 games x 2, uncapped; ring = default command-ring worker thread, st = single-threaded command processing alone, snap = snapshots, which also turn st on). Flip (`flip-texsnap`, both rounds within ~1% of each other): snapshots cost 0.8% against st alone (geomean 0.992; Paper Mario -2.6%, the rest -1.8% to +0.4%), and st alone is **+3.7% faster than the ring** (geomean 1.037, no game slower: Smash Bros. +10.4%, Cruis'n USA +10.2%, Banjo-Kazooie +7.3%, Mario Kart 64 +7.2%, Majora's Mask +5.9%, Yoshi's Story +5.9%, Conker +2.7%; OoT, SM64, Paper Mario, GoldenEye equal). That is a separate finding, now item 36. Duo (`duo-texsnap`): too noisy to call: geomean st 0.995, snap 0.959 against the ring, but several games sit in two speed levels that don't follow the variant (Mario Kart 64 479 or 722 fps, Yoshi's Story 386-553). Two differ in both rounds: Banjo-Kazooie st 260-268 vs ring 367 fps, and Smash Bros. snap 236-238 vs st 362-363. Recorded, Duo is low priority. Still running: (a) the full Flip compat run with snapshots on (`flip-texsnap`, `chain73_flip_texsnap.sh`). Snapshots are only worth keeping if they cost nothing and fix something in the compat run.
- [ ] 34. Per-block timeline stamps (gopher64 80cfbc4 `rdram_tracker.hpp`): every 8-byte RDRAM block holds the timeline value of the last RDP command that wrote or read it; an emulated read waits only if the block's write stamp is not done yet, a write also checks the read stamp, and it waits only for that value, not the newest SyncFull. Ours locks whole 4 KB pages at SyncFull and waits for the latest SyncFull (precise mode only lets single dynarec accesses outside the drawn bytes through). Test against our page-protection smart sync on OoT, Snap and GoldenEye (the games whose waits cost most: lazy-waking losses), counting waits and time per frame; its interpreter-hook style check costs a lookup on every access, so it may only pay as a narrower wait inside our fault handler.

Texture packs on parallel-RDP: step A (do low-level keys match) done, step B (draw them) built and on the device queues (added 2026-11-05, Joey approved step A, "no rush"; step B approved 2026-11-06, "do step b"; research in docs/texture-packs-parallel-rdp.md, the launcher chat's, Joey commits it). Only GLideN64 loads packs today. But GLideN64's pack key (`_loadHiresTexture`, GLideN64/src/Textures.cpp) comes only from RDP command state: loadInfo per TMEM address (texture-image address, uls/ult, width/height, size, load type, dxt from LoadTile/LoadBlock), the tile descriptor (lrs/uls, masks, clamps, line, size, palette) and the TLUT palette, hashed with txfilter_checksum (Rice CRC). parallel-RDP gets the same commands, so it should compute identical keys and use existing GLideN64 packs (.hts/.htc, PNG) unchanged. Step A, CPU side, no rendering change, behind a dev switch: (1) in parallel-RDP's command path, keep a GLideN64-style loadInfo per TMEM address and a palette copy, and compute the key for each tile of every textured draw with GLideN64's exact rules (width/height from mask/clamp, bpl, ReverseDXT, the MK64 odd-height hack, the CI4/CI8 palette offset). (2) Look the keys up in a real pack's index, read with GLideNHQ's code (our GLideN64 copy has the .hts file-storage reader, TxFileStorage in TxCache.cpp; GPLv2-or-later). Count per frame: textured draws, unique keys, keys found. (3) Run GLideN64 with the same pack on the same save states, log the keys it finds, and compare the two sets. (4) Games: OoT and Majora's Mask (Djipi packs), SM64, Paper Mario, from Joey's bench states. (5) Report the match rate per game and what kinds of texture miss. Expected misses: S2DEX backgrounds (GLideN64 keys the whole bgImage, LLE sees strips) and framebuffer textures; any other miss is the interesting part. Default build must behave exactly as now: check the uncapped bench with the switch off. Steps B-D (shader sampling, storage, backgrounds, UI) are not approved: step A's numbers go to Joey first. Pack: Joey pointed to OoT Reloaded v11.0.0 (GhostlyDark, evilgames.eu; GLideN64 HTS, HD 1.31 GB or 4K 3.95 GB, same keys); waiting on Joey's go for the download. MM, SM64 and Paper Mario packs still to name. Step A results (2026-11-05, Duo, OoT from Joey's save state, OoT Reloaded HD pack; 4K pack gives the same): parallel-RDP finds 110 of the 112 textures it draws with (99% of texture uses); GLideN64 finds 113 of 113 after its key log was fixed (b308996: GLideN64 fills its palette copy only with a pack loaded, so the first GLideN64 log hashed an empty palette and found 49). Side by side parallel-RDP finds 96% of what GLideN64 finds. Why not 100%: (1) parallel-RDP's 2 misses are 2x8 CI4 textures used as the second texture in two-cycle draws, in nearly every window; GLideN64 never looks these up, so no pack can contain them: not a real loss, and the key code should skip them as GLideN64 does. (2) GLideN64 finds 3 textures all run (two CI8 32x32, one RGBA16 64x32) for which parallel-RDP produces no key at all: a real gap in how parallel-RDP works out some loads, to find by logging each key's load (address, size, tile, load type) in both renderers. (3) 2 more appear in only 1-2 of 31 windows: the two runs show slightly different frames. Switch off costs nothing (`duo-texkeys2-off`, Duo, 3 rounds, uncapped, vs the gpu2 base build): 11 of 14 games within 0.5% (OoT 334.5 vs 335.2); Cruis'n USA, Paper Mario and Yoshi's Story jump between two speeds from run to run in both builds (Paper Mario 329 or 415 in each), so their medians say nothing. Key hashing on (`flip-texcost`, Flip, 2 rounds, uncapped, 1x; every texture load hashed as a pack lookup would, plus the key file written every 60 frames, which a pack does not do) costs 1.9% on average. 4 games are within 0.5%, and 7 lose 0.8-2.9% (OoT -0.8%, Mario Kart 64 -1.1%, Pokémon Snap -2.2%). Three lose more: Donkey Kong 64 -3.6%, Majora's Mask -5.2% and Cruis'n USA -8.2%; Cruis'n's off rounds were themselves 198 and 215 fps. Hitch counts stay the same, apart from OoT's slowest frame (22.5 → 34.9 ms, 2 frames over 33 ms in one round). Duo (`duo-texcost`, 2 rounds, uncapped, 1x and 2x): at 1x the 10 games that keep one speed lose 1.0-4.3% (about 2.4% on average; OoT -4.3%, Mario Kart 64 -3.7%). Cruis'n USA, Paper Mario, Pokémon Snap and Smash Bros. jump between the Duo's two speed levels from run to run, so their numbers say nothing. At 2x the GPU is the limit and hashing is within 1% in most games; Conker loses 5.9% (and has fewer slow frames, 67 vs 95). No hitches added at either scale. Still to do for step A: the load logging for (2) (built 2026-11-06, core texpack 8409ea1: L and F lines in both key logs; queued on the Duo as chain95 after its texture pack run), and skipping the tex1 lookups of (1). Result (2026-11-06, `results/texkeys/duo3`, OoT): parallel-RDP finds 110 of its 112 keys in the pack (99% of uses), GLideN64 113 of 113. They share 108. The GLideN64-only keys (5 this run) are Link's eyes, closed and open mouth, a hand and a hole texture (pictures from the pack: `duo3/glnonly-sheet.png`). Link has his back to the camera in this state, and GLideN64 (high-level) textures every triangle the game sends, leaving culling to OpenGL. The RSP microcode drops those back-facing triangles before the RDP, so parallel-RDP never draws them, and a pack never needs them there: not a miss. The parallel-RDP-only keys (2) are tex1 lookups, CI4 2x8 at the same address GLideN64 keys as tile 0's RGBA16 texture. Cause: `uses_texel1` checked both combiner cycles in 1-cycle mode, but GLideN64 builds a 1-cycle combiner from the second cycle only (Combiner.cpp `Combiner_GetStages`), so a TEXEL1 left in the unused first cycle made us hash tile+1. Fixed to match (core texpack 0bce9d5, 2026-11-06; app-texkeys4, core 3f0b2b), rerun queued on the Flip after chain105 (chain106). Rerun result (2026-11-06, `results/texkeys/flip4`, app-texkeys4 with core 3f0b2b installed): no change. OoT still looks up the same two CI4 2x8 tex1 keys (5754 of 634179 uses, as in `duo3`), and Paper Mario and SM64 give the same key sets as before, so the 1-cycle rule was not the cause (0bce9d5 stays, as it matches GLideN64, but changed nothing here). Next candidate, not yet shown: GLideN64's tile-7 swap (`TextureCache::update`, Textures.cpp: when the texture tile is 7, tile 0 was loaded by LoadBlock and tile 1 shares its TMEM address, it keys tile 1's descriptor instead), which would make its tex1 lookup collapse into the tile 0 key. Checking it needs the tile number in the K lines of both key logs. Either way these are lookups the pack does not have, so nothing is drawn differently; the cost is the hashing (0.9% of uses). The two RGBA16 triangle keys that only parallel-RDP finds in the pack (16x16, 8 uses; 32x64, 49 uses) are also in both runs. Step B (2026-11-06, core branch `texpack` 122d4d1, local): `--es env N64_RDP_TEXPACK=<folder>` draws a GLideN64 pack (`<ROM name>_HIRESTEXTURES.hts` or a PNG folder) on parallel-RDP. GLideNHQ reads the pack entry by entry from disk (a 9.5 GB .hts never loads whole); a worker thread looks up each new key and writes RGBA8 texels into one host-visible storage buffer (`N64_RDP_TEXPACK_MB`, default 256, capped at the GPU's maxStorageBufferRange); until a texture is in, the draw keeps the N64 texture, so loading never stalls a frame (what Dolphin, PCSX2, PPSSPP and Azahar do). A draw with a loaded replacement gets it through a new queued command (Op::MetaTileReplace) carried in the free upper bits of the tile's coordinates, and separate TEXPACK builds of the ubershader and rasterizer sample it in place of TMEM (bilinear or nearest as the tile asks, with its wrap, mirror and clamp). Copy mode and YUV keep TMEM. With the switch off the stock shaders run (same SPIR-V byte for byte). Round 1 of almost every game with the switch on has one stall of 0.49-0.56 s (the pack shader variants compiling; they are not in the pipeline cache yet). Round 2 doesn't, apart from Perfect Dark. Duo bench, same 1x test (`duo-texpack-1x`): of the 8 games that keep one speed, OoT drawing the pack loses 6.9% (335.5 → 312.4 fps). Without a pack, Perfect Dark loses 6.9%, Majora's Mask 5.6% and Mario Kart 64 5.2%; Banjo-Kazooie, Donkey Kong 64 and Conker lose 1.7-3.8%, and Super Mario 64 nothing. The other 6 jump between the Duo's two speed levels. First-use stalls with the switch on are 57-86 ms in round 1 (Donkey Kong 64, Majora's Mask, Mario Kart 64, Conker), against 0.5 s on the Flip. At 2x (`duo-texpack-2x`, GPU-bound, and the Duo holds one speed here): OoT drawing the pack loses 9.2% (144.9 → 131.6 fps in both rounds). That's the pack shaders' GPU cost, so a cheaper sampling path (T5: real textures instead of a storage buffer) is worth it for games that do have a pack. Without a pack, GoldenEye loses 13.3% (128.4 → 111.3), Perfect Dark 5% (and more slow frames, 66 vs 45) and Paper Mario 3.5%; most others are within 1%. Round-1 stalls are 70-95 ms. GoldenEye's loss with nothing replaced says much of the cost is the pack path's presence (registers, decode), not the HD sampling. Measurement queued (core texpack 21d5d01: dev shader switches; app-tp-nosample core 1df066, app-tp-nearest core 476a77): OoT with its pack, off / pack / no sampling / nearest only, 3 rounds, Duo 2x (chain104) and Flip 1x (chain105). Duo 2x result (`duo-tpvar-2x`, OoT, uncapped emu fps, rounds 2-3; round 1 of the two new variants had first-use shader stalls): off 144.9, pack 132.0 (-8.9%), no sampling 143.3 (-1.1%), nearest 138.8 (-4.2%). So decoding the replacement tile, masking and the extra binding cost about 1%; the cost is fetching the replacement from the storage buffer: one texel per pixel (nearest) costs about 4%, and the three more that the shader-side bilinear filter reads cost about 5% on top. Flip 1x result (`flip-tpvar-1x`, same method): off 142.8, pack 131.4 (-8.0%), no sampling 141.9 (-0.6%), nearest 142.5 (-0.2%). On the Mali-G52 one storage-buffer read per pixel is almost free and the whole cost is the bilinear path (three more reads and the float blend); on the Adreno 722 the single read already costs half. Round 1 of each newly installed variant had a 750-835 ms stall on the Flip (shader compiles with an empty pipeline cache), as on the Duo. Next step: put replacements in a sampled image (an atlas), so bilinear is one filtered fetch through the texture cache instead of four storage-buffer reads. A cheaper first try is queued: the same buffer read as a uniform texel buffer (R8G8B8A8_UINT view, so the four reads go through the texture cache and the unpack is free; core 0a3d18, app-tp-tbuf, not committed until it measures better), Duo 2x OoT off / pack / tbuf, 3 rounds (chain110, then the Duo compat run with the switch on). First try drew wrong (`duo-tbuf-2x`, 2026-11-06): the replaced textures came out black, so its 145.5 fps (off 144.6, pack 132.0) measured nothing. Cause: the generator (joey/texpack_shaders.py) adds binding 13 to slangmosh's stored reflection by hand, and still marked it a storage buffer, so the descriptor never matched the shader's texel buffer. Fixed in the generator (it now also checks no other descriptor type uses 13); rebuilt as app-tp-tbuf2, Flip 1x first (chain112; the Flip's chain111 with the broken build was stopped). Flip result (`flip-tbuf2-1x`, core f3ff1e): no usable number. The Mali-G52 driver caps uniform texel buffers under 1 MB (Vulkan only requires 65536 texels), so the replacement buffer came out at 0 MB and only 4 of 110 replacements loaded (106 failed, 3% of draws replaced); 141 fps against off 143 / pack 131 measures a mostly unreplaced frame. So a texel buffer cannot carry packs on Mali as built: it would need many views, or (better, and the plan for both GPUs) a 2D image (an 8192-wide RGBA8 image holds 256 MB), filled by a buffer-to-image copy, which also opens hardware bilinear. Kept on a side branch (core `texpack-tbuf` c036fe9, local), not on `texpack`. Duo run of tbuf2 queued after its compat run (chain114), where the limit is large (the first try loaded all 110). Flip limits (`cmd gpu vkjson`, 2026-11-06): maxTexelBufferElements 65536 (256 KB, the cause above), maxImageDimension2D 16383, maxStorageBufferRange 256 MB, so an 8192x8192 RGBA8 replacement image fits. Pack proof (Joey asked whether the pack really applies): `shots/oot-pack-proof.png` puts the wall from the pack-on screenshot beside the pack's own entry for that texture (N64 32x64, pack 256x512, key 4369c7a181810a90), same grooves; the scene's 8 most-drawn textures are all 8x replacements from the pack (OoT Reloaded, a 110-entry cut of its 9.5 GB .hts). Flip pictures with the pack check core (`results/texpack/flip-tp2`, 2026-11-06): OoT draws its pack (110 replacements loaded, 0 failed, all textured draws replaced once loaded), Majora's Mask logs "no pack" and draws as with the switch off, both 60 fps, as on the Duo. Flip pack check bench at 1x (`flip-texpack2-1x`, quick set, 2 rounds): the 13 games without a pack average 0.2% below the switch off (was 5.8%, up to 12.6%, before the pack check); all within 0.7% except Donkey Kong 64, 165.2 vs 162.6 (-1.6%; both rounds lower, by 3.7 and 1.6 fps, against an off spread of 2.6, while the Duo had it at 318 vs 318). Hitch counts match (off/pack: Mario Kart 64 8/8, Perfect Dark 103/87, Conker 320/316). OoT, drawing its pack: 143.3 -> 131.5 (-8.2%). Duo pack check bench at 2x (`duo-texpack2-2x`, quick set, 2 rounds): the 13 games without a pack match the switch off within 0.5% (GoldenEye 128.3 vs 128.1, was -13.3% before the pack check), Conker +3.5% only because its first switch-off round was slow (127.7, then 136.7 like both pack rounds); hitch counts match (Mario Kart 64 4/4, Perfect Dark 81/78, Conker 74/66). OoT, drawing its pack: 144.9 -> 131.6 (-9.1%), as in `duo-tpvar-2x`. Flip compat with the switch on (`flip-texpackcompat`, app-texpack1, so every game ran the pack shaders and hashing; 2026-11-06): 82/82 ok, nothing drawn wrong against `flip-st`. Two first shots read as black: Jet Force Gemini caught on a blank transition (0 display lists that second; its later shots match) and Majora's Mask (its third shot shows the name entry as normal). The other differences are the same games caught at earlier or later screens, because every game started with the pack shaders uncached. The pack check above removes the no-pack cost; the cost for games that do have a pack is still to cut (the per-texel tile check in the pack shaders), and the first-use compile stall wants the pack variants pre-built when a pack exists. Not in step B: the 3 missing keys and the tex1 skip (step A gaps above), mipmaps, S2DEX backgrounds (step C), UI (step D, Joey designs). What other emulators do (researched 2026-11-06, Joey asked): all load asynchronously and show the original meanwhile (Dolphin's resource worker, PCSX2, PPSSPP with a per-frame load budget, Azahar with 8 uploads per tick); DuckStation is synchronous behind a preload screen. All cap memory and evict: ARMSX2 an LRU byte budget (RAM - 2.5 GB, at least 192 MB, and it recommends BC-compressed packs), Azahar preloads up to half of RAM, PPSSPP drops textures unused for a while, Dolphin's GPU cache drops after 64 unused frames, DuckStation 512 MB, mupen64plus-nx's GLideN64 `MaxHiResTxVramLimit`. Mipmaps: Dolphin and PCSX2 use the pack's own, Azahar generates them, PPSSPP takes either. Compressed formats: BC1/3/7 everywhere, Azahar also ASTC, PPSSPP transcodes KTX2 (UASTC to BC7/ASTC, ETC1S to BC1/ETC2). Dolphin and DuckStation hash only the palette range a texture uses; Dolphin and PPSSPP allow wildcard keys; PPSSPP and DuckStation take per-game pack config (hash ranges, filtering, ignore address). DuckStation, a low-level renderer like ours, composites replacements into decoded texture pages and replaces VRAM writes at upload, merging adjacent writes: the model for S2DEX background strips in step C. Settings they offer: Dolphin "Load Custom Textures" and "Prefetch Custom Textures" (loads the whole pack into RAM at start against stutter); PCSX2 load replacements and preloading; Azahar custom textures, preload (off) and async loading (on); PPSSPP replace textures, save new textures and load speed (Slow (smooth) to Instant (may stutter)); DuckStation replacements, preload, VRAM-write replacements and cache sizes; GLideN64 hi-res on/off, cache size (100 MB), full alpha, cache compression, 16-bit textures, VRAM limit. Tests from this (each a dev switch first; any menu entry is Joey's design): (T1) async vs synchronous loading: hitch counts entering new areas (benchhitch); (T2) memory with the HD vs the 4K pack, and the buffer cap that fits each device; (T3) generated mipmaps for replacements at 1x: shimmer vs cost; (T4) 16-bit texel storage (GLideN64's FORCE16BPP) vs RGBA8: memory and speed; (T5) ASTC in VkImages instead of a raw buffer, only if T2 shows memory is the limit; (T6) preload the whole pack at start (Dolphin's prefetch) vs on demand: start time, RAM, hitches; (T7) a per-frame load budget (PPSSPP's load speed) if T1 shows the worker hurting frame times; (T8) palette-range hashing and wildcard keys: whether they recover keys that differ only by unused palette entries. Steps C-D wait for Joey's go Other authors' whole packs (2026-11-06, Joey: "try a few to make sure all is good"; evilgames.eu GLideN64 HTS HD packs: Banjo-Kazooie Reloaded 0.2.1, UI only, 244 MB; GoldenEye 007 HD 2025.12.30, 593 MB; Super Mario 64 Reloaded 2.6.0, 699 MB; Mario Kart 64 Reloaded 2026.04.03, 3.6 GB; scratchpad evilpacks.sh, Flip 1x, `results/evilpacks/flip-evil`): all four load and draw, 0 failed, no crashes, 60 fps as with the pack off. Banjo-Kazooie replaces 1 texture (1% of draws: the pack is HUD/menus only), GoldenEye 5 (99% of draws), SM64 46 (100%, 12 MB), Mario Kart 64 1596 (96%). The 3.6 GB pack reads fine entry by entry from storage. Found: Mario Kart 64's attract-mode race filled 217 MB of the 256 MB replacement buffer in 40 s (26 MB on the title screen, then the whole course). Nothing is ever evicted, so a few courses in, new textures stop being replaced ("buffer full"). The 256 MB is also the Mali-G52's storage buffer limit, so a bigger buffer is not the fix: the replacement store needs eviction (least recently used, as GLideN64's texture cache limit). Goes with the 2D replacement image work (order item 5). 4x run of the same packs queued on the Odin 3 after its queue (chain120). Eviction built (2026-11-06, core texpack 27f93f3; the tested binary 2a8b4a differs only by a comment, app-tp-evict): the replacement buffer is a ring; when full, the worker evicts what no draw used in the last 10 s, then 2 s, then 8 frames (past what the GPU can have in flight), skipping what is in use; draws and eviction meet Dekker-style so a draw never samples a replacement being overwritten; an evicted replacement loads again when a draw wants it, one that found no room retries after 2 s. Flip check (`results/evict/flip-evict`, `evicttest.sh`): Mario Kart 64 Reloaded 150 s, default 256 MB: filled at about 110 s, then 553 evicted, 0 failed, still 97-100% of textured draws replaced; in 48 MB: 4685 evicted, 0 failed, 95-99% replaced; OoT test pack in 8 MB (its scene needs 27 MB): 66% replaced, the rest keep the N64 texture and retry; 0 fatal, 60 fps, screenshots draw right (no wrong or garbled textures). Duo texel-buffer bench at 2x (`duo-tbuf2-2x`, 2026-11-06, OoT, 3 rounds; tbuf2 loaded all 110 replacements into a 128 MB texel buffer on the Adreno 722, 100% of draws replaced): off 144.4, storage buffer 130.7, texel buffer 130.8. Reading the four texels through the texture cache saves nothing on Adreno either, so the cost is the four reads and the shader-side blend themselves: the 2D replacement image (one hardware-filtered read per sample, 1-texel gutters for repeat, coordinates clamped for clamp and mirror) is the remaining way down, on both GPUs. Duo compat with the pack switch on (`duo-texpackcompat`, app-texpack2, games 1-31 before the pause and 32-82 after): 82/82 ok. 2D replacement image built (2026-11-06, local branch texpack-img off texpack 27f93f3, uncommitted while tested; core 84b78d, app-tp-img): the replacements go into one linear-tiled host-visible RGBA8 image (8192 wide; the Mali-G52 and Adreno 722 both allow sampling and linear filtering of linear images, so the loader writes it directly, no copies), on shelves of height 2^k + 2 with a 1-texel wrap gutter, each shelf its own eviction ring; the new _img shader variants (TEXPACK_IMAGE=1) take one textureLod per sample; N64_RDP_TEXPACK_IMAGE=0 keeps the buffer for A/B. Mipmaps not yet (next, if the image measures well). Queued: Flip chain124 (the four new packs and OoT pictures, then OoT off / buffer / image 1x, 3 rounds), Duo chain125 (OoT pictures and bench at 2x). 2D image path tested 2026-11-06 (core 84b78d, committed as texpack-img b55002b): pictures on the Flip (BK, GoldenEye, SM64, MK64, OoT) and the Duo (OoT 2x) match the buffer path, nothing missing or garbled; OoT uncapped, image vs buffer: Flip 1x 134.3 vs 131.3 (+2.3%), Duo 2x 132.3 vs 130.9 (+1.1%); pack off 140.7 / 144.9. Next: mipmaps (order item 5), then the Odin 3 / Portal 4x runs. **Mip levels built (2026-11-08, overnight, order item 5; core `texpack-mips` 0defbe2 on texpack-img, local):** on the image path each replacement up to 2048 wide gets its chain down to 1 x 1 (2 x 2 box, colour weighted by alpha), each level with its gutter, left to right after it on its shelf (about twice the width); a mips bit in the tile (thi[25]); the shader takes the level from the perspective-correct st gradients at the pixel (per output pixel, so upscaling picks finer levels) times the tile shift and the replacement's scale, and blends the two levels around it (trilinear, two hardware bilinear reads); point-sampled draws keep level 0. `N64_RDP_TEXPACK_MIPS=0` turns them off. Duo (`chain154`, app-texmips1 = drc-default + core 7e3074): pictures at 1x and 2x (OoT subset, GoldenEye, SM64 evilgames packs; `results/evilpacks/duo-texmips-1x`, `-2x`): the grain on distant HD walls, floors and grass is gone, the HUD is the same, every state loads, nothing missing; OoT uncapped (3 rounds) 1x 310.7 -> 316.6 fps (+1.9%), 2x 132.0 -> 137.1 (+3.9%): smaller levels read less memory. Portal 4x (2026-11-08, overnight, `chain156`, `results/evilpacks/portal-texmips`, app-texmips1): the same three packs draw the same with and without mip levels (0.0-0.3% of pixels differ, the pack textures are already sharp at 4x), every state loads, no crash (the logs' "fatal" count is Wi-Fi log noise and our own force-stop); OoT uncapped at 4x 124.0 -> 121.8 fps (-1.8%, all 3 rounds the same way), still 2x full speed, so at 4x mips change nothing you see and cost a little. Flip 1x (2026-11-08, overnight, `chain155`, `results/evilpacks/flip-texmips`, app-texmips1): the grain on the OoT and SM64 walls is gone as on the Duo (1.0-1.9% of pixels differ; GoldenEye's pack textures 0.0%), every state loads, no crash; the first 10 s window has more draws still waiting with mips (OoT 247 -> 719: making the levels takes a little longer), the same from the next window on; OoT uncapped 1x 135.3 -> 129.8 fps (-4.0%, all 3 rounds), still over 2x full speed. So mips: clearly better pictures at 1x/2x on both phones' GPUs, speed +2-4% on the Duo, -4% on the Flip, -1.8% at 4x on the Portal where nothing changes on screen; Joey 2026-11-08: "On by default": core branch `mips-default` c100ddb = the shipped dd-drc fd9f40e + texpack-mips (merged clean, no angrylion, has a8ab829), built as core 66a33e / app-mipd1; Flip 82-game compat (`chain193`, `flip-mipd-compat`): **82/82 ok** (2026-11-08 11:15); sent to the Frontend session to bundle. Odin 3 at 4x (2026-11-07, chain120, `results/evilpacks/odin3-evil-4x`, app-texpack2): all four evilgames.eu packs (Banjo-Kazooie, GoldenEye, SM64, Mario Kart 64) load and draw at 60 fps; GoldenEye's walls are clearly HD, the others subtler at this distance; textures still loading at the 38 s shot: Banjo 172, GoldenEye 40, SM64 402, MK64 855 (the 3.6 GB pack), so loading speed is the next thing to look at. **Portal (2026-11-07, `chain118`, 14 games x 2 unless noted; queued 2026-11-05, run only now because of the battery rule):** `portal-texcost` (pack check keys on vs off, no pack loaded): 1x uncapped -5.2% geomean (Paper Mario -18%, Pokemon Snap -12%, MM -7%, MK64 -7%), at 4x -0.1%; slow frames the same. So the key check costs a few percent only where the GPU has plenty of room (1x uncapped), nothing where packs are used (4x).
- [x] 36. Single-threaded command processing on Mali (found 2026-11-05 in item 33's speed run). parallel-RDP normally hands RDP commands to a worker thread through a ring; `PARALLEL_RDP_SINGLE_THREADED_COMMAND=1` processes them on the emulation thread instead. On the Flip that is +3.7% uncapped (geomean, 14 games x 2, no game slower; Smash Bros. +10.4%, Cruis'n USA +10.2%). Fits item 27's result that the ring is not where the Flip's time goes: handing commands to another core costs the Flip's small CPU more than the work it moves. Duo mixed (see item 33). To become the Mali default it needs compat: full Flip compat with it on queued after chain73 (`flip-st`, `chain77_flip_st.sh`, app-texsnap with snapshots off). Adreno (Portal, Odin 3) to bench after that; they run smart sync by default, where the ring may matter more. Duo (2026-11-06 00:45, `duo-st3`, quick set x 3 rounds, uncapped, app-texsnap; medians of games that stayed on one speed level): faster in 8 of 11, MK64 +7.6%, SM64 +8.5%, DK64 +6.6%, MM +4.8%, OoT +3.0%, Perfect Dark +2.5%, Conker +2.0%, F-Zero +0.5%; slower in Banjo-Kazooie -6.4% and Paper Mario -5.7%. Cruis'n USA, Smash Bros., Yoshi's Story and GoldenEye jump between the Duo's two speed levels from run to run, so they say nothing (GoldenEye: ring 370/260/371, single-threaded 268/262/266). Roughly +3% on the Duo, with two clear losers, so not a Duo default; Portal and Odin 3 still to bench. Flip compat with it on (`flip-st`, 2026-11-06 01:28): 82/82 survived; against the previous Flip run (`flip-texsnap`) no shot went black and the biggest differences are all timing (Mario Tennis on Play Mode vs Player Select, Pokemon Stadium a different frame, the AKI logo mid-fade), nothing drawn wrong. Faster and compat clean, so it is now the Mali default (app, MainActivity sets `PARALLEL_RDP_SINGLE_THREADED_COMMAND=1` on Mali unless `--es env` does; `=0` turns it off); Adreno keeps the ring Joey 2026-11-06: keep it as the default.
- [ ] 8x upscaling on Adreno (2026-11-07, Joey via the Frontend session; work order item (25)). Thor (Adreno 740): 8x was a black screen at 19-35 fps (5120x1920) on Qualcomm's driver and the bundled Turnip, started at 8x or switched to it. Cause: parallel-RDP binds the upscaled RDRAM as one storage buffer of 8 MB x factor^2 (rdp_renderer.cpp ~584, upscaling_multisampled_rdram; the hidden RDRAM copy is scaled too): 4x = 128 MB, 8x = 512 MB, and the Thor reports maxStorageBufferRange 128 MB on both drivers (`vulkan limits:` log line, vk_context.cpp), so 4x fits exactly and 8x can't. Frontend commit 1862a6f (launcher/phase0): the Vulkan probe string gained the range in MB and MainActivity.capUpscaling caps Resolution at the largest factor that fits (8x runs as 4x at 60 fps on the Thor; `--ez nocap true` lifts it). To look into, none tried: (a) Mesa Turnip source: is 128 MB MAX_STORAGE_BUFFER_RANGE a6xx/a7xx hardware descriptor limit or a chosen value (if chosen, a patched Turnip might run 8x) (**answered 2026-11-08 morning**, Mesa 6b41b56: it is the hardware's: `freedreno_devices.py` gives a6xx_base and a7xx_base `max_storage_buffer_range_bytes = 1 << 27` (128 MB) and a8xx_base `(1 << 31) - 1`; on a7xx only, Turnip can emulate larger storage buffers for vkd3d (`tu_device.cc`: the driconf option `enable_ssbo_emulation`, which then reports 2 GB; its cost and whether the bundled V38 build has it are not known yet). Our compat logs agree: Odin 3 (Adreno 830, a8xx) reports maxStorageBufferRange 2047 MB, so capUpscaling already lets it run 8x; Portal and Duo report 128 MB. 8x steps, in order (2026-11-08, Joey: "add everything 8x to queue ... go ahead"): (25b) 8x on the Odin 3 as it is: picture, fps, against 4x; (25b result, 2026-11-08, `chain195`, `up8-odin3-plain`, bundled Turnip Gen8 V38, capped, from bench states: the driver reports 2047 MB and 8x starts (core "Using 8x upscaling", 5120x1920), but every 8x screenshot is black (OoT 32.7 fps, SM64 33.7, GoldenEye 18.7; 4x 60 fps and drawn), with no Vulkan error logged; the Portal draws 8x with emulated storage buffers, so this is not the buffer size; then (`chain200`, `up8-odin3-sysdrv`): on Qualcomm's stock driver (`--es driver system`) 8x draws correctly (OoT screenshot right) at OoT 55.3 fps and SM64 34.6 fps (4x 60 on both), so the black screen is the bundled Turnip Gen8 V38 on the Adreno 830, not our code; RetroArch's bench build doesn't start on the Odin 3 at all (as on 2026-11-03), so 25d is answered by this instead: RetroArch can only use the stock driver, which draws 8x on the Odin 3 and nothing right on the Portal; follow-up item 43) (25c) 8x on the Portal with Turnip's `enable_ssbo_emulation` (driconf, through the environment), whether the bundled V38 takes it (the `vulkan limits:` line) and what it costs (**first runs 2026-11-08**, up8.sh, `--ez nocap true`, OoT and SM64 from bench states, capped: plain (`up8-portal-plain`) 4x 60 fps, 8x 19.6-20.3 fps and a black screen, as on the Thor; `enable_ssbo_emulation=true` (`up8-portal-ssbo`) changed nothing, still 128 MB: the driconf option is named `tu_enable_ssbo_emulation` (Turnip's 00-turnip-defaults.conf turns it on for some Unreal Engine 5 games; xmlconfig.c reads an environment variable of the option's name), and the bundled V38 has that string, so it was rerun under that name (`chain197`, `portal-ssbo2`): **it takes**: the driver reports 2047 MB and 8x draws correctly (SM64 screenshot right), but slow: OoT 8x 14.6 fps, SM64 8x 8.5 fps, and the emulation slows 4x too (OoT 60 -> 56, SM64 60 -> 35), so it is no way to play at 8x on a7xx and must never be on for 4x or lower; RetroArch on the Portal (25d, `chain196`, `results/h2h/ra-53.up4x`): its parallel-RDP draws garbage on the Portal at every scale, 1x included (`ra-53.up1x`, 55 fps of noise; 4x 17 fps of noise; 8x does not start); RetroArch uses the stock Qualcomm driver and cannot load Turnip, so on the Portal it cannot answer 8x; 25d moves to the Odin 3 after `chain195`); (25d) RetroArch's Mupen64Plus-Next with parallel-RDP at 8x on the Portal (Joey: RetroArch and M64Plus FZ offer 8x; FZ is GLideN64, which has no such buffer), only that run's core options changed and Joey's put back, to see whether its parallel-RDP draws 8x on a7xx; (25e) if neither works on a7xx: the upscaled RDRAM split into several bindings, or reached through VK_KHR_buffer_device_address (core shader changes); (25f) whether 8x is worth having on a 1080p screen at all (8x is 2560 wide, ~2.5x a 1080p screen; 4x plus downscale may look the same)); (b) split the upscaled RDRAM into several bindings of at most maxStorageBufferRange and index the right one in the shaders (core shader change); (c) VK_KHR_buffer_device_address for that buffer, which has no range limit (core shader change); (d) the Duo's 2x cap (geometry drops at 4x) may be the same limit if the Adreno 722 reports under 128 MB: read its `vulkan limits:` line on its next run. Even black, the Thor ran 8x at only 19-35 fps, and 8x is ~2.5x a 1080p screen's width, so it likely pays off only on stronger GPUs or as supersampling. (a) and (d) are reading; (b)/(c) are core changes and need Joey's go. **Turnip at 8x on the Odin 3, 2026-11-09 (Joey: "Turnip can't be fixed at 8x?", then "Yes, run both"; app-gpur1, bundled Turnip Gen8 V38, parallel-RDP 8x with `--ez nocap true`, 25 s, scratchpad compat `up8o.sh` / `up8s.sh`):** (1) Turnip's storage-buffer emulation (`--es env tu_enable_ssbo_emulation=true`, as `portal-ssbo2`; `odin3-t8-ssbo3`): SM64 and OoT from the bench states both **black** (brightest pixel 1 of 255) at 34 and 33 emu fps, against 54-55 without it, so the option took effect and still draws nothing. (2) The largest buffer that draws: 4 MB RDRAM (`mupen64plus-ForceDisableExtraMem=True`, the core logs "Using RDRAM size of 4194304 bytes"), so 8x needs 256 MB instead of 512, booted without a state (an 8 MB state does not fit): SM64 (`odin3-t8-4mb`) and OoT (`odin3-t8-4mb2`) **black** at 8x (54 / 36 emu fps, new frames presented); the control, the same 4 MB boot at 4x (`odin3-t8-4mb-4x`, 64 MB), draws Mario's head at 60 fps. So on Turnip the Adreno 830 draws nothing with a 256 MB or 512 MB upscaled RDRAM buffer although it reports maxStorageBufferRange 2047 MB, and the 8 MB games' 4x (128 MB) is the most that draws: the same 128 MB as a7xx, the limit Turnip reports is not the one it keeps. No option of Turnip's fixes it; what is left is (b) splitting the buffer or (c) buffer device address (core shader changes, Joey's go), or the stock driver at 8x on the Odin 3 (draws in SM64 and OoT; the "82/82 at 8x" was GLideN64, see (43), its real compat `odin3-8xsysprdp-compat` runs now), or 4x as Turnip's top there like a7xx. **Decided (Joey 2026-11-09, stock driver at 8x) and built:** `GpuRules` gives Adreno 8xx at 8x the system driver + parallel-RDP, `odin3-8xsysprdp-compat` 82/82 (work order item 43); Turnip stays capped at 4x on Adreno.

- [ ] 37. From Grarak's PS Vita emulators (read 2026-11-06, Joey: "there may be tricks we can use or learn"; DSVita (DS), AdvancedSlop (GBA), the azahar 3DS port, the Vita3K fork, the Monster Hunter Tri static recompilation; their DEVELOPMENT/TODO/optimizations notes). Ranked by what our own numbers say: (a) compile only hot blocks: DSVita interprets a block until it has run 100 times, then compiles it, the one CPU change of theirs that "survived" hardware tests; for us it attacks the Flip's first-run compile bursts (Mario Kart 64: 2122 compiles, 340 ms in 5 s) with no second thread, so try it beside item 7 (new_dynarec + the cached interpreter as the cold tier); (b) texture packs in the smallest exact format (Monster Hunter port: each texture in the smallest GPU format that holds it losslessly, RGB565 / RGBA4444-exact / 8-bit; 769 -> 558 MiB): for item 35's 2D image, so packs like Mario Kart 64 (217 MB in 40 s) evict less; (c) frame skip that also skips the drawing (azahar: "a skipped frame that still draws costs everything and saves nothing"; skip only frames nothing reads back, keep a floor of one shown frame a second): refines item 14's VI-only skip, which keeps the RDP work; smart sync already knows which drawn pages the CPU reads; (d) smart sync: stop re-protecting pages the CPU touches every frame (azahar's write tracker marks such pages hot and stops re-arming them); our games mostly wait once per SyncFull, so a dev counter of faults per page first; (e) a return fast path in the dynarec (DSVita: inline BX LR check, +4%); ours sends every `jr` through `get_addr_ht`, 0.4-0.6% of samples in the Flip/Duo profiles, so at most that much; (f) hot/cold function order at link time (lld `--symbol-ordering-file` from a profile; DSVita: iTLB misses -29%, +0.6% on an A76): small, like the dropped LTO/PGO. Already done here (checked): RDRAM mapped twice through a memfd, VI interrupts rescheduled from their due time, the emulation thread alone on a big core with the other threads moved off it, at least 3 swapchain images. Not for us: running guest code natively (3DS ARM on Vita ARM; N64 is MIPS), the Vita kernel/GXM work, static recompilation (one game at a time). Also useful: DSVita has an Android port (Kotlin UI, ROM-launch intent, broadcast debug bridge) and reads libretro .cht cheat files, which the launcher could do too.
- [ ] 38. Drivers on GPUs we have not run (read 2026-11-06, Joey: "I am worried that we only support the 5 devices we've tested so far"). Today (`setupGpu`): Turnip faux123 on the Adreno 722, Turnip Gen8 on any name matching `Adreno (TM) [678]xx`, the system driver everywhere else; parallel-RDP only with a custom driver or stock Mali (Vulkan 1.1 + 16-bit storage), else GLideN64; the only safety net is the probe (a custom driver with no GPU falls back). Run on four GPUs (Adreno 740 Thor/Portal, 830 Odin 3, 722 Duo, Mali-G52 Flip). How others do it: DroidDeck classifies the GPU into families (kgsl `gpu_model`, then the Vulkan name, then a board-platform table; Mali by Vulkan name or a MediaTek/Tensor SoC table), picks from an ordered rule list (family, model numbers, Android version, stock driver version range; built-in plus a remote JSON it can change without an app update), and runs each new pick as a trial: two starts that never reach a ready session roll back to the previous driver and mark that release bad. ARMSX2 offers its own Turnip on Adreno 6xx/7xx and malisx2 on the Malis it supports (by GL_RENDERER), loads it through adrenotools with a system-loader fallback, and keeps a driver-bug table (35 rules by API, vendor, driver, model range, driver version range, Android SDK, SoC) that turns workarounds on, ported from EmuCoreX. Eden only shows a recommended driver from a model-range table; xendroid-plus suggests a downloaded driver whose name fits the model or family; GameNative asks its server for a per-game config matched by exact GPU, then GPU family, then a fallback, with hard family overrides (Adreno 6xx, 8 Elite Gen 5, A12). Proposals, waiting on Joey: (a) a GPU family table instead of the regex: model from kgsl sysfs, Vulkan name, then platform; Qualcomm's marketing names ("Adreno (TM) A12" = 613) map to a model, so those Adrenos get Turnip and parallel-RDP instead of the stock driver and GLideN64; (b) a start-up trial with automatic step-down: a driver + renderer pair this device has not finished a session with is a trial; if two starts die or never show a drawn frame, the next start steps down (Turnip -> system driver -> GLideN64) and remembers the pair as bad; the session logs already record the exit reason; (c) one table for the GPU-specific settings now spread through the code (722 upscale cap, Mali early acquire, single-threaded commands, async), keyed by vendor, driver, model and driver version, logged at start; (d) rules that can change without an app update (remote JSON like DroidDeck's, or just with each in-app update once the Phase 0b updater exists); (e) for testers on new GPUs, the first-start log line says which rule picked the driver, so a report shows it. Not covered by any of these: a driver that renders wrong without crashing; only a tester's report or a self-test frame (parallel-RDP drawing a built-in frame compared to a stored hash) would catch that. Offline coverage (looked into 2026-11-06, Joey: "Look into offline coverage"): our bundled Turnip Gen8 V38 is released for Adreno 8xx, but its device table (checked with llvm-strings) has every Turnip part: FD605-FD690, FD702-FD750 and Adreno 722/735/753/810-840, so every Adreno 6xx-8xx we would hand it is known to it; not known: anything newer than the 840 (the probe then falls back). What it does on a 6xx is not run (no 6xx device); a second, general build (StevenMXZ `Turnip_v27.0.0-R1.zip`, same release, 2 MB) would be its step-down. Open drivers for the rest, all shipped as adrenotools zips (meta.json + .so), the format `setVulkanDriver` already loads (malisx2 ran that way on the Flip): malisx2 v0.3.3 (MIT, 3.6 MB; G52, G57, G68, G76, G77, G78, G615, G715; ~14% slower than stock on the Flip's G52), FristOneRR PanVK v3.7.0 (MIT, 5.9 MB; Bifrost G52/G72/G76 and Valhall G57/G68/G77/G78, tested on many G57 phones), PanVK-kbase v26.3.1 (zenithblue, Mesa licences + MIT, 9 MB zip, 33 MB unpacked; Mali v6-v14 passed its 41 tests, needs Android 15, so not the Flip on Android 11), RADV-Xclipse v1.8.0 (MIT, 4.1 MB; Exynos 2200/1480/2400). PowerVR has no open driver: stock only. Qualcomm's own driver packs (K11MCH1 v840, StevenMXZ v891.x, crueter's 8 Elite packs) are proprietary and can't ship. All five open ones add about 25 MB to the 18.6 MB APK; only the one a device uses is unpacked. On stock drivers today: Mali with Vulkan 1.1 + 16-bit storage already gets parallel-RDP, so Mali packs only matter where the stock driver is broken or slow; stock Xclipse and PowerVR get GLideN64 only because they are untested, not because of a known fault. Testable here: FristOneRR and malisx2 0.3.3 against stock on the Flip, the general Turnip against Gen8 on the Duo, Odin 3 and Portal; RADV-Xclipse, PanVK-kbase and every 6xx result need a tester. Results: FristOneRR PanVK v3.7.0 on the Flip (Mali-G52, downloaded with Joey's go 2026-11-06, sha256 checked): loads, reports Vulkan 1.3, draws SM64 right, but uncapped from the bench states, 2 rounds: SM64 73 vs 118 fps stock, OoT 44 vs 136, GoldenEye 34 vs 116, and a 2.2 s stall at every launch; not for the G52 (scratchpad compat/benchdrv.sh, results/bench/flip-drvfrist.tsv). Turnip on the Duo (Adreno 722, app-logs2, uncapped, 14 games x 2 rounds, results/bench/duo-drvturnipr1b.tsv): against the bundled faux123 v1.2.5, the general StevenMXZ R1 is -5.8% geomean (-11.5% to +1.0%) and the bundled Gen8 V38 -15.8% (-33.2% Smash to -2.0% Conker), so on an a7xx part the a8xx-tuned Gen8 is the slowest; R1 or faux123 suit the 6xx/7xx step-down better. malisx2 0.4.0 (released 2026-11-06 with Joey's parallel-RDP patches; downloaded on Joey's go, sha256 checked) on the Flip vs stock, uncapped, 2 rounds (results/bench/flip-drvmalisx2040.tsv): draws right (OoT checked), SM64 79 vs 111 fps, OoT 84 vs 135 (8-9 frames over 33 ms per run, stock 0), GoldenEye 78 vs 113: 29-38% slower, more than the ~14% measured before; stays unbundled on the G52. faux123 v1.2.7 (sha256 checked; adds large-submit splitting, tested by faux123 on Adreno 640/650/660/730/740/722/830) vs the bundled v1.2.5 on the Duo (results/bench/duo-drvfaux127.tsv): the same speed; 11 of 14 games within 1.1%, and the three outliers (Snap and Smash +21%, OoT both ways) are single launches at about two-thirds speed that hit either driver (the Duo now and then starts a run slow; the same games ran at full speed on v1.2.5 in duo-drvturnipr1b). Portal (Adreno 740, `chain129`, `portal-drv740`, 2026-11-08, 1x uncapped, 14 games x 2): its default, Turnip Gen8 V38, is the fastest; Turnip v27.0.0-R1 -0.67% geomean, faux123 v1.2.7 -0.98% (both lose most in game 27, -1.5 / -6.0%; every other game within -3.7..+1.6%), so the 740 stays on Gen8. Decided 2026-11-06 (Joey: "sounds good"): the rules are built into the app and change with app updates (ARMSX2's way); no rules file fetched from the internet, since the app bundles its drivers and needs no network to play. From DroidDeck take the GPU family classification and the start-up trial with automatic rollback; so (a), (b), (c) and (e) are the plan, (d) is dropped unless the user base outgrows update-speed fixes.

First-launch stutter: pre-build port, `bench/pbfirst.txt` (app-pnhitch.apk = Joey64 app + pn64-ports core 29f4957; list kept, caches deleted; pb on vs off, 6 games x 2) on the Portal and Odin 3. The shipped per-game list (item 20) gets built if pre-build helps. Duo (2026-11-06): no help. With caches deleted, pre-build at launch turns several short stalls into one stall of 1.25-1.44 s (pb-cold, slowest frame) against 0.23-0.34 s without it (pb0-cold). It also lowers emu fps over the run, e.g. Mario Kart 64 (27) 279 vs 375 fps and Banjo-Kazooie (01) 189 vs 251 fps. Mario Kart 64 stalls about the same either way (1.25-1.31 s vs 1.10-1.24 s). Warm runs have no frames over 33 ms. Portal and Odin 3 runs still in their queues **Portal (2026-11-07, `chain118`, 14 games x 2 unless noted; queued 2026-11-05, run only now because of the battery rule):** `portal-pbfirst` (6 games, 2 rounds, app-pnhitch, driver pipeline cache deleted before the cold runs, measured from launch): warm 100%; cold without the pre-build list (`N64_RDP_PREBUILD=0`) -1.6% fps and a worst frame of 63-170 ms (MK64 507-511 ms); cold with the pre-build list (the default) -11.3% fps and a worst frame of 573-638 ms in every game (MK64 574-577). So on the Portal the pre-build adds about half a second of stall at start, more than compiling on demand costs, and the frames over 33 ms after it are the same (1-3 per run either way). The Odin 3 (pbthreads) built its 10 pipelines in 380 ms. Decision for Joey: pre-build off on Adreno (it only matters after a driver update or with a deleted cache; warm launches wait 0 ms either way). **Decided 2026-11-07 (Joey: "Yes, off on Adreno"):** app 494f713, MainActivity sets `N64_RDP_PREBUILD=0` on Adreno unless `--es env` sets it; Mali keeps the pre-build. Check queued (`chain142`): one game on the Duo (no pre-build line expected) and the Flip (line expected) after their thread chains. **Rechecked 2026-11-09 with the known pipelines (joey64#6 port, core 7a016e33, app-known1)**, where an Adreno first launch compiles slowest, the stock Qualcomm driver at 8x (Odin 3 Portal, `o3p-pbknown-sys8x`, quick set + Goemon, 2 rounds, every cache and the saved list deleted before each run, measured from launch): pre-build off (the default) vs `N64_RDP_PREBUILD=1` (30 known pipelines built before the game starts, 1160-1190 ms): the first frame comes 0.9-1.9 s -> 1.8-1.9 s after launch (about +0.8 s), fps from launch +3.2% geomean (+1.3..+3.8%), and the slowest frames after the first 5 s are the same (GoldenEye 75.5 vs 75.8 ms, SM64 113 vs 114; one Banjo round 919 vs 38 ms is the only big one); at 8x on the stock driver these games run 26-57 fps uncapped, so their many frames over 33 ms are GPU time, not compiling. Not worth 0.8 s more at every first launch: the pre-build stays off on Adreno. (The first known-pipelines runs on the Odin 3 Portal and Duo, `o3p-knownfirst` and `duo-knownfirst`, compared nothing: the pre-build is off there, and the known list is part of it.) **Known pipelines on Mali, where the pre-build runs (joey64#6, b681e24 ported as core 944a669 on branch known-pipelines, off with `N64_RDP_KNOWN=0`; Flip, quick set + Goemon, 3 rounds, app-known1):** at a true first launch (every cache and the saved list deleted) 17 known pipelines are built before the game starts in 3.3 s, so the first frame comes ~0.8 s later (2.4-3.0 s -> 3.5 s). Uncapped (`flip-knownfirst`): the first 5 s run about twice as fast (SM64 46 -> 100 fps), later windows equal; the big compile hitches after that mostly go (Cruis'n 80 -> 61 ms with 20/25/17 -> 0/1/0 frames over 33 ms, DK64 273 -> 217 ms 10/5/12 -> 1/0/0, MK64 285 -> 150, Perfect Dark 64 -> 37, Goemon 45 -> 23). At 60 fps, as played (`flip-known2`, kn0cap/kncap-first): frames over 33 ms after the first 5 s Cruis'n 62/95/61 -> 5/4/91, DK64 16/14/14 -> 0/0/0, Perfect Dark 12/11/18 -> 2/3/4, Goemon 7/5/7 -> 0, OoT 7/0/0 -> 0; slowest frame DK64 94 -> 11 ms, MK64 325 -> 73, Snap 364 -> 111, Goemon 89 -> 29; no game worse except one Cruis'n round (91, as bad as without). Later launches (caches kept, `flip-known2` kn0/kn-warm rounds 2-3): the build waits 7 ms either way (mean of 30, max 11 ms), fps the same (+0.4%). (Conker's uncapped over-33 count looked worse with it on only because the faster start reaches its heavy intro scene inside the measured time.) A measured gain on Mali at the cost of 0.8 s on a game's very first launch: kept (standing approval), on by default in the core; Adreno does not run it (pre-build off there). Bundle candidate: core branch ra-known 163cf82 = the Frontend session's run-ahead core runahead-state b4d76b0 + known-pipelines (core md5 dad849f3, app-rakn-163c), compat running on the Flip (`flip-rakn-compat`); the run-ahead core alone on the Duo (`duo-ra-compat`, Adreno, where the known list is inactive). **Results 2026-11-09**: Flip 82/82, 0 black; Duo 82/82, 0 black; Odin 3 Portal (`o3p-rakn-compat`, app-rakn-163c) 82/82, 0 of 82 end black where `o3p-main-compat` draws. - [x] T4. GPU priority above the compositor: built 2026-11-05 (app 8c9ecd0): our bundled Turnip creates its KGSL contexts with no priority (Mesa `kgsl_submitqueue_new`; Vulkan's global-priority request never reaches KGSL), so a hook library loaded into the driver's namespace sets `N64_KGSL_PRIO=<1-15>` on each context it creates (lower = higher; hakuX used 7). `bench/gpu2.txt` (app-gpu2.apk), capped and uncapped, Duo, Portal, Odin 3. Duo's run comes first (after its clock test) and doubles as the hook's first check. Bug found 2026-11-05 06:0x: the hook never loaded. During a Duo prio7 run the app had libhook_impl and libmain_hook mapped but not libkgsl_priority_hook, because MainActivity set the `--es env` values after setupGpu() had already loaded the custom driver, and the hook loader checks `N64_KGSL_PRIO` at load time. So the prio rows of `duo-gpu2` (and of every earlier build) measured nothing; its wave rows are valid. Fixed in app 52ecf00 (dev env set before setupGpu; no default changes). `cores/app-gpu2.apk` now holds the fixed build (app-gpu7: app 52ecf00 + core gpu2 ccebaf2; the old one kept as app-gpu2-nohook.apk), so the Portal and Odin 3 gpu2 runs, not started yet, get a working hook. Duo rerun queued after its tile run (`chain33_duo_prio.sh`, `bench/prio.txt`, one launch to log the hook line first) The `duo-gpu2` prio rows came out as expected with no hook: prio 7 +0.0%, prio 1 +0.1%, capped slowest frames 14.9-15.1 ms for all three; they don't count Rerun void again (2026-11-05 08:5x): the hook library loaded in exactly the priority variants (checked in /proc/pid/maps) but never logged a context, because it exported a plain `ioctl` while both bundled Turnips import the versioned `ioctl@LIBC`, and Android's linker binds a versioned reference only to a definition of that version. Fixed in app 7da5785 (version script, exports `ioctl@@LIBC`); Portal launch check: `KGSL priority hook: context 15 flags 00000013 -> 00007052 (ok)`. All earlier prio rows (duo-gpu2, duo-prio) measured nothing. app-gpu2.apk for the queued Odin 3 and Portal gpu2 runs is now this build (core unchanged, gpu2 ccebaf2); Duo rerun queued after its early-acquire check (`chain40_duo_prio2.sh`, `duo-prio2`, runs only if the launch check logs the hook). Duo result (2026-11-05 09:1x-11:0x, `duo-prio2`, app-prio2, 14 games x 2, uncapped and at 60 fps; launch check at the start: `context 18 flags 00000013 -> 00007052 (ok)` with priority 7; the per-run logs can't show the hook, because the bench clears the log after the state loads): at 60 fps no difference, every game holds 60.0 with the same slow frames (frames over 33 ms in 28 runs: default 5, priority 7 4, priority 1 4; all but one are MK64's recompile burst). Uncapped, mean of 2 rounds: priority 7 0.986x, priority 1 1.020x the default. Cruis'n USA is 26% slower with either priority in both rounds (359 vs 483 fps); without it, priority 7 1.008x, priority 1 1.046x. Most of priority 1's gain is the Duo's two speed levels: with priority 1 the games that jump between levels sat on the high one in both rounds (Snap 720 / 720 vs default 479 / 721, Paper Mario 418 / 414 vs 417 / 328, SM64 341 / 340 vs 320 / 320, Yoshi 557 / 555 vs 389 / 506), so higher GPU priority may keep the Duo on its fast level more often; two rounds can't tell that from chance. The rest are within 3% (Banjo +3%, Perfect Dark -2.5%, Conker -1.3%). Not a default: no gain at 60 fps, Cruis'n loses a quarter, and Duo-only effects get little effort. Portal and Odin 3 runs still queued **Decided 2026-11-07 (Joey: "Odin 3 only"):** priority 1 is the default on the Odin 3 (MainActivity, by Build.MODEL; `--es env N64_KGSL_PRIO=` turns it off): `odin3-gpu2` +3.6% uncapped, 13 of 14 games up; `portal-gpu2` flat (prio1 -0.7%, all of it one Cruis'n USA round at 335 fps, its other round +0.8%; prio7 +0.5%), so the Portal and the rest keep the driver's own priority. Wave64/128 forcing flat on both (Portal +0.3 / +0.6%): stays off. Odin 3 compat on that build: `odin3-kgsl1` 82/82 ok (2026-11-07, `chain133`); launch check: `KGSL priority hook: context 16 flags 00000013 -> 00001052 (ok)`, so the default reaches the driver.
- [ ] T5. CPU one frame ahead of the GPU (DuckStation): profile first. Read 2026-11-05: parallel-RDP's scanout never waits for the GPU itself; it drains the command ring (waits for the worker thread's CPU work), flushes and submits the VI pass; GPU waits come from smart-sync reads (wait_for_timeline) and the app's frame wait. Measurement built (core gpu2 3d6dffa, `N64_RDP_WAITSTATS=1`: scanout, drain and GPU timeline wait ms per scanout, logged every 300); `bench/waits.txt` (app-gpu3.apk, uncapped and capped, 14 games) queued on all four devices after their other runs. Flip done 2026-11-05 (`flip-waits`, 14 games, uncapped and at 60 fps; the run died after game 79 and the last 4 games were rerun as `flip-waits-b`; found later: the first run was killed by an overlapping queue script, and the rerun overlapped another one, but its repeated game matches the first run, 160.9 vs 161.1 fps): a scanout costs 0.36-0.45 ms uncapped and 0.44-0.71 ms at 60 fps (~3% of a 16.7 ms frame), of which waiting for the RDP worker (drain) is only 0.02-0.11 ms; GPU timeline waits are 0 in every game except Cruis'n USA at 60 fps (0.85 ms per scanout; it runs with smart sync). So on the Flip neither the scanout nor waits on the GPU explain the slow frames, and a CPU-runs-ahead change would gain nothing there. Same run's slow frames at 60 fps: Perfect Dark 60.9 ms / 12 over 33 ms, Mario Kart 64 94.9 / 4, Smash 57.2 / 6, GoldenEye 42.3 / 4, the rest under 27 ms with none over. Adreno devices (smart sync, so real GPU waits) still to come Duo result (2026-11-05 07:5x-08:01, `duo-waits`, 14 games x 1, uncapped and capped, first 5 s left out): a scanout costs 0.20-0.59 ms with its drain 0.04-0.29 ms, so the scanout does not wait on the GPU there either. GPU timeline waits come from smart sync (the Duo runs it by default), at most one per scanout: Banjo ~2.1 ms per scanout (both modes), MM 1.6, Paper Mario 0.7, Yoshi 0.6, Conker 0.5, the rest 0-0.3. Same picture as the Flip: the frame-ahead idea has nothing to hide at the scanout; Banjo's and MM's waits are the smart-sync reads (items 11 / early kicks) **Portal (2026-11-07, `chain118`, 14 games x 2 unless noted; queued 2026-11-05, run only now because of the battery rule):** `portal-waits` (1 round, app-gpu3): a scanout costs 0.11-0.28 ms (drain 0.02-0.05 ms); GPU timeline waits about 1 ms per scanout (one wait each), uncapped and at 60 fps; worst frame 14.5 ms uncapped, 21.6 ms at 60 fps, none over 33 ms. Same picture as the Flip and Duo: the scanout doesn't wait on the GPU, so nothing for this item on the Portal.

Wave size: built 2026-11-05 (core branch gpu2 0fb7d8e): parallel-RDP fixes the subgroup size only for tile binning; `N64_RDP_WAVE=64/128` forces it on every other compute shader where the driver allows; in `bench/gpu2.txt`. Plus 4x upscaling (Joey: "is 1080p achievable", 4x = 1280x960): `bench/up4.txt`, uncapped and at 60 fps 1x/2x/4x, Portal and Odin 3 (Odin 3 result, 2026-11-06, `odin3-up4`, 14 games x 2: yes. Uncapped at 4x every game runs 144-382 fps against 236-2016 at 1x, e.g. OoT 660 -> 200, Mario Kart 64 707 -> 201, Perfect Dark 680 -> 194, Conker 236 -> 174, so 4x keeps 2.4-6x headroom. At 60 fps all 14 hold 60.0 at 1x, 2x and 4x; slowest frames at 4x match 1x (Mario Kart 64 29/29 ms, Donkey Kong 64 23/20, others 2-16 ms) with no hitches. At 2x, round 1 had a 29-39 ms frame in every game and round 2 did not (2-30 ms, as 1x), so a one-time cost, most likely first-use shader compiles for the 2x pipelines.) Duo result (2026-11-05 05:2x-07:5x, `duo-gpu2`, 14 games x 2, each variant's slower round, speed-level jumps left out): wave 64 -0.7%, wave 128 -0.3%: no gain (Paper Mario +26% and GoldenEye +42% are the Duo's two speed levels). Portal and Odin 3 still queued
- [ ] T7. RSP: NEON JIT (built; Joey 2026-11-06: off); overlay block cache built 2026-11-05 (core gpu2 c334334, `N64_RSP_CHUNK_CACHE=1`): the JIT already reuses compiled code across overlay swaps, but clears its lookup table and re-hashes block by block; now each 256-byte IMEM chunk's table is saved by the contents of that chunk and the next and restored in one copy. Flip queued: RDRAM hash check on 4 games (on vs off must match), then `bench/chunk.txt` 14 games x 2. RDRAM hash check on the Flip 2026-11-05 (OoT, SM64, Banjo-Kazooie, Conker, 90 s each, off vs on): the hashes part from frame 30-90 in all four, but the code reads right (a block ends at most at the end of the next chunk, which the key covers), and the Flip runs parallel-RDP async, which may make RDRAM timing-dependent; nobody has checked that this build repeats itself there. Queued: off twice and on twice (OoT, Conker) after the Flip's HLE first look; the speed bench runs meanwhile, and nothing changes by default until the check is clean. parallel-n64's HLE geometry: first look queued on the Flip (item 9) Correction 2026-11-05 02:5x: that hash check is void. A separate queue script (the off-twice repeat check) ran on the Flip at the same time, 01:25-02:04, force-stopping the app under it (it also overlapped the first chunk-bench games, and the repeat check itself got 0 hashes). Redone in one sequential Flip script (`chain23_flip_seq.sh`): repeat check, chunk hash check, chunk bench (`flip-chunk2`), then GPU spin and the MK64 hitch trace Repeat check (2026-11-05 03:0x, clean): the Flip build does not repeat itself with async parallel-RDP: cache off vs off differ from frame 90 (OoT 197 of 266 hashes, Conker 57 of 161), on vs on likewise, so RDRAM hashes can't judge the cache on the Flip that way. Queued after the Flip sequence: the same check with plain synchronous parallel-RDP (`hashab64s.sh`: smart sync off, synchronous=True), off twice and on twice Speed result (2026-11-05 03:5x, `flip-chunk2`, clean, 14 games x 2): -1.2% geomean; Conker -10.9% (101 vs 113 fps, both rounds) and Majora's Mask -6.1%, nothing faster beyond noise (Smash +4.3%, Cruis'n swings). Saving and restoring each changed chunk's table (hashing two chunks, copying 64 pointers, a hash-map lookup) costs more than re-finding the blocks one by one. Dropped (stays off; `N64_RSP_CHUNK_CACHE` left in the gpu2 branch only); the synchronous hash check was cancelled as moot. Remaining T7 piece: the NEON JIT, Joey's call

- [ ] T9. Run-ahead: Run-ahead measured 2026-11-09 (Joey: "Measure run-ahead"; dev switch `N64_RUNAHEAD=<1-4>`, lag probe `N64_LAG_PROBE=<frame>[/0]`, scratchpad results/ra and results/rabench): the gain is real, the core isn't ready. Gain: SM64 from its bench state answers A 3 frames after the press without, 2 with run-ahead 1 (16.7 ms sooner), 1 with run-ahead 2, the same on GLideN64; poll->display (29-32 ms) is unchanged. Cost on the Thor: take state 2 ms, put back 7 ms, plus the extra frame; uncapped the quick set fell from 208-572 to 52-101 fps (Snap and MK64 frozen), under 60 in Conker 52.5, Cruis'n USA 56.4, Perfect Dark 59.6; run-ahead 2 drops SM64 to 55-58 fps with underruns. Broken: Pokemon Snap and Mario Kart 64 freeze (their RAM stops changing, both renderers, repeat skip on or off), and SM64's RAM drifts from the plain run from frame 60 on (plain runs match each other exactly, capped and uncapped). Cause: the core's state return (92e7fba): with `N64_STATE_RETURN=0` Snap moves again, at 20 fps. Needs a core change (Joey's go): a state taken and put back at the frame boundary that resumes exactly, and a cheaper load (it copies RDRAM and the 8 MB TLB tables through a fresh 16 MB buffer). Core fix 2026-11-09 (Joey: "Yes, fix the core"; core branch `runahead-state`, da8c7c9 + a3bec9e + the RSP commit, not bundled; main merges it after an 82-game compat with run-ahead off): states taken and loaded at the frame's end in place; an MI_INTR_MASK write no longer ends the frame inside the recompiler's store helper (that froze Snap); a load empties the jump cache (N64_JC_CHECK=1: 0 of 300); only changed RDRAM pages drop their code, TLB-mapped code included (SM64 put back 6.4 -> 2.0 ms; Conker still flushes all, its TLB changes every frame). Thor, run-ahead 1, parallel-RDP: SM64, Snap, MK64, GoldenEye, Perfect Dark, Conker, DK64 move; Snap and MK64 on GLideN64 too; SM64 lag probe 3 -> 2 frames. Uncapped (results/ra/rach1/A.tsv): OoT 439 -> 148 (RA2 108), SM64 319 -> 134 (100), GoldenEye 561 -> 126 (92); bundled core vs new with run-ahead off: SM64 318 / 319, GoldenEye 519 / 561, OoT 511 / 439 (OoT swung 415-520 between rounds earlier: needs rounds). Snap presents 60 a second with run-ahead (30 without): right pictures, more GPU work. Frames whose state the core refused (an RSP task running, ~5% in SM64: a repeated picture, then a jump) are fixed in the RSP commit (GET_SAVESTATE_CONTEXT = RUNAHEAD_SAME_INSTANCE: parallel-RSP's registers and the task flags kept with the state; `N64_RA_RSP=0` the old way), NOT YET RUN ON A DEVICE. Still to do when a device is free (scratchpad ra_chain2.sh): the RSP build on the quick set (refusals 0, all move), SM64/Snap RAM hash vs plain, lag probes off/RA1/RA2 and GLideN64 with poll->display, 1x/2x/4x capped play, the rest of the uncapped quick set, OoT off-case rounds, and why GoldenEye's RA1 run ended at 370 fps with 0.06 ms extra frames (run-ahead seemingly stopped part-way). Conker's per-frame TLB flush (6.4 ms) could drop only the changed TLB entries' pages.

    - [x] Snap's RAM with run-ahead 1 differed from the plain run in 64 of 1137 frames (Thor 2026-11-09, core c328ab4, results/ra/rach4/H), in bursts of 4-20 frames. Fixed 2026-11-10 (core runahead-state 85fb1f5, c18acd8, 27aa4c9): RDRAM dumps (`N64_RAM_DUMP`, results/ra/snap2, snap4) showed one framebuffer pixel at 0x3CB6A0 drawn differently from frame 1036. parallel-RDP keeps state outside RDRAM that no save state has: its registers (other modes, combiner, colours, scissor, tiles, images), TMEM, the hidden RDRAM bits (coverage) and the primitive count that seeds the dither noise. The extra frames changed them and the rollback left them. Now a meta op in command order keeps them with the run-ahead state and puts them back on load (`CommandProcessor::ra_state`). The noise seed alone didn't do it (210 frames still differed, snap3); all of it: 0 of 2336 frames differ in two runs (snap6) and 0 of 2339 at the final build (snap7). Cost, uncapped run-ahead 1 from states, OoT / 18 / 01 / Snap: 1x 195 / 134 / 152 / 145 before against 186 / 141 / 151 / 145 (noise, results/ra/cost1); 4x with the upscaled hidden RDRAM copied too (64 MB, twice a frame): 81 -> 62, 62 -> 43, 64 -> 67, 59 -> 45, so that copy was dropped (it only feeds the picture, and the upscaled domain update rewrites its samples from the 1x bits wherever the put-back RDRAM differs): 87.3 / 62.2 / 64.0 / 53.4 before against 87.4 / 60.0 / 63.6 / 54.3 (cost2).

Dumps (pd2): both runs change the same ranges at the same frames with different contents. Not the cause: copy-on-write, the RSP side state, the TLB tables, a full flush, the direct path, the pak (pd4). Exact with the cxd4 RSP (0 of 2045, pd5cx), different with GLideN64 too: the cause was HLE audio (on by default under parallel-RSP), whose audio lists keep state from one task to the next (alist buffer, audio/naudio/nead variables, MP3 buffer) outside the core's state. Fixed 2026-11-10 (core runahead-state d3ac04b + 2ab1193): run-ahead states carry it (8 KB copy, any RSP; `N64_RA_HLE=0` the old way). Thor (hle1, hle2): Perfect Dark 0 of 2307 differ (twice); Conker 0/2289, GoldenEye 0/2331, Majora's Mask 0/2325, OoT 0/2325, Snap 0/2339 and 0/2338. Also in the core (4308f0d): after any state load the host FPU's rounding mode is set from FCR31 (ARM64 recompiler; it was set only at CTC1). It wasn't this bug's cause (cxd4 was exact without it), but a load kept the rounding mode from before it.

After it: 663 shows 662's black, 664 the text, matching off within dither (the VI's dither moves on each scanout, so one held picture shifts it by one: at most 1-3 of 255 after).     - [x] Tony Hawk's Pro Skater 2 with run-ahead at 4x: 60 fps without it, about 20 with it (Thor 2026-11-09, results/ra/slow1, booted, title screen); the default native draws don't matter (19.6 vs 19.4). Where the time goes (2026-11-10, results/ra/th1-th3, core 2ab1193): the GPU. Its upscaled render pass costs 14-19 ms of GPU per drawn frame at 4x (`PARALLEL_RDP_BENCH=1`); the game draws 30 a second, so without run-ahead the GPU keeps up (401-475 MHz). Run-ahead runs two frames per shown frame and the take and the put back each wait for the GPU's drawing (smart sync's settle): take 19.8 ms, put back 20.3 ms, GPU at its 680 MHz top, 19.5 fps. Not the state itself: at 1x the take is 0.8 ms and put back 0.5 ms (60 fps), and the full copy (`N64_STATE_COW=0`) is the same 19.5 fps. 2x holds 60 fps with run-ahead (take 3.5 ms, put back 3.2 ms; parallel-RDP has no 3x, a `3x` run fell back to 1x). So at 4x even perfect overlap would leave two ~17 ms draws per shown frame; what would help is drawing the extra frames at a lower size, which is the native-draws setting (waits for Joey's design) or dynamic resolution (item 8 below).
    - [x] New Tetris at 4x is slow without run-ahead too: 28-35 fps (Thor, booted), 14 with run-ahead. Where the time goes (2026-11-10, results/ra/nt1, nt2): the GPU. The game reads back what the RDP drew every frame (smart sync: 600 CPU waits in 600 SyncFulls), and its upscaled pass grows with the pixels: 1x 2.3 ms, 2x 7.0 ms, 4x 24.8 ms of GPU a frame (`PARALLEL_RDP_BENCH=1`). 1x and 2x run 60 fps; 4x is GPU-bound at about 37 at best. With run-ahead at 2x it ran 23-27 fps with the GPU at only 550 MHz: each wait covered the upscaled pass too, though the CPU only needs the 1x pass that writes RDRAM.
    - [x] Split submit (core runahead-state, on by default since core 9a83733; `N64_RDP_SPLIT_UPSCALED=0` turns it off): the 1x pass always goes to the GPU on its own before the upscaled pass, so the fence the CPU waits on (smart sync, synchronous SyncFull, run-ahead's take and put back) signals once RDRAM is written. Thor (sp1): New Tetris 2x with run-ahead 24.7-27.7 -> 38.8-45.3 fps (extra frames 19.6 -> 11.9 ms, GPU now 680 MHz), picture right; New Tetris 4x without run-ahead and Tony Hawk 2 4x with it unchanged (GPU-bound). Uncapped 4x (split, split2; OoT / GoldenEye / Banjo / Snap): run-ahead off round 1 -1.1 / 0 / +23 / +2.6%, round 2 +1.2 / 0 / +5.7 / +2.2%; run-ahead on round 1 +4.5 / +11 / +8 / +5%, round 2 (split3, order swapped) +21.9 / +8.1 / +1.8 / +8.0%. Thor 82-game compat at 4x with run-ahead and the split on (`thor-split4x`, 2026-11-10): 82/82. Games 1 and 5 passed on a rerun: 1 lost focus to the Thor's own settings app; 5 (Bomberman 64: The Second Attack) died at 20 s in the first run, on an older build (before 3831e0d), its log stopping 2 s into the game with no crash line. The flicker sweep runs it again.
    - [x] Paper Mario flickers with run-ahead at 4x (Joey saw it live 2026-11-10 in the thor-split4x compat run; fixed in core 3831e0d): on the file-select screens about 300 frames a minute (results/ra/pmrec, back-and-forth frames in 60 s) showed the other scene (title vs. the bordered map picture). Off, split alone and run-ahead at 1x had none; 2x had 91. The VI registers were right every frame (three buffers in turn), so it was the upscaled picture. Ruled out: repeat-frame skipping (429 with it off), full RDRAM uploads (284), waiting for all RDP work at take and put back (298); keeping the upscaled buffers with the state only cut it to 78. A whole-device wait before or after the host's copy, or right before or after the put back, gave 0. Cause: on the Thor the GPU's RDRAM is host-mapped (no external host memory), so a direct page upload is a CPU memcpy straight into it, landing under GPU work still in flight; after a put back the next frame's uploads of the older RDRAM went under the shown extra frame's VI fetch, which rewrote the upscaled picture from them. Fix: once a run-ahead state has been taken, those uploads are copies in the command stream (`N64_RDP_ORDERED_UPLOAD=0` never, 1 always). Thor: 0 in 1488 frames (off 256); uncapped run-ahead 4x within 1% on OoT, 18, 01, Snap and Paper Mario, 1x 1-2% (results/ra/ord-cost); RDRAM every frame matches a plain run at 4x (Snap 0/1436, OoT 0/1921). A compute-queue wait at the put back (`N64_RA_PUTBACK_WAIT=1`, off) also gave 0 but cost 15-20% (OoT 4x 71 -> 57).
    - [ ] Spider-Man flickers at 4x on the title screen, run-ahead or not (Joey saw it live 2026-11-10, compat game 50, Thor; pointed at it in the recording without run-ahead). What flickers: the strip left of the title art (about 12 N64 pixels), where the game leaves an old frame (a dark city). Title frames with the strip lit (results/flick, titlestrip.py): 1x 100%, 2x 100%, 4x 53-56%, 8x 38%, 4x with run-ahead 0% (always black). Not split (56% off), not ordered uploads (53%), not repeat skip (39%), not native tex rects (38%). Dumped core frames at 4x (N64_LAG_PROBE_DUMP): lit 2 of every 6, so one of the game's three framebuffers keeps the old frame in its upscaled copy and two have black there, while 1x shows it in all three: the upscaled copy of those pixels is stale and the VI's pre-pass (update_upscaled_domain_pre: 1x vs reference) does not refresh it. Followed up 2026-11-10 (core debug logs N64_VI_LOG, N64_STRIP_LOG, N64_RDP_LOGALL, RDRAM dumps; results/flick/sm-*): the VI reads all 640 pixels the same at 1x and 4x; the strip is in RDRAM itself (4x: one buffer has it, two are black; 1x: all three); the still title is drawn from x 32 with no clear, so x 0-31 keeps what the frames before left in each buffer (the zoom-in clears and draws from x 10). From a state on the copyright screen (sm_copy.raw) with no input, and with A pressed at frame 1500, 1x and 4x play the same sequence (logos, title, menu, demo) with no flicker (sm-st4a, sm-pa4, sm-pa4c, sm-st1c, sm-pa1c: 0 back-and-forth frames); a fresh boot with no input at 4x too. So far it shows only in the compat runs (button presses on host time), and slowing 1x to 60% there did not bring it back. Open: reproduce it from a state with the compat presses at fixed frames; the flicker sweep runs it again. Also: run-ahead at 4x runs Spider-Man at 24-44 fps (off 40-60, 1x 60): the take waits in smart_settle for the last SyncFull, which covers the 4x pass (take 8-14 ms vs 0.6-1.9 at 1x).
    - [x] Upscaled 480i games shake with Bob deinterlacing (Joey saw Tony Hawk's Pro Skater 2 and Spider-Man shake, 2026-11-10, Thor): with upscaling, parallel-RDP's Bob (upscale_deinterlace) moves each field a quarter line x scale up or down, so a still 480i picture bounces every frame; at 1x the fields are woven and it holds still. Whole-picture shifts frame to frame (results/flick, shake.py, back-and-forth): THPS2 Bob 4x 2451, 2x 3220, 1x none, run-ahead 4x 1115 (fewer frames), Weave 4x 0; Spider-Man Bob 4x 1946, Weave 4x 0. Done: Weave is the default (Joey 2026-11-10: "Yes, Weave default"); Bob stays a choice in Settings › Video › Deinterlace (combing on movement in 480i games with Weave).
    - [x] Tony Hawk 2's 19.5 fps with run-ahead at 4x is one dark intro screen in the boot (off drops to 50-52 there too): after 240 s its attract demo runs 54-59 fps with run-ahead at 4x and draws right (th4b).
