**Measuring now (results go into their items)**
- [ ] Upscaled retest (2026-11-05, Joey asked whether 60 fps at 1x was the right test): no. Only `bench/up4.txt` (not run yet) ever used upscaling; every other bench ran at native 1x, where the Adrenos have 4-12x the GPU they need (uncapped 260-720 fps on the Duo), so GPU-side and pacing ideas could not show. Judged at 1x and worth a second look upscaled: GPU priority (T4), KGSL clock pin / turbo and the perf hint (T3), wave size (T6), 16x8 tiles (item 21), subgroup / small-types switches, early kicks / spins / relaxed depth / coherent memory, item 32 GPU time, and for lag the 2-image swapchain, early acquire, repeat-skip pacing and T5 (smart-sync GPU waits grow when the GPU works longer). Not affected: CPU-side work (NEON RSP, RSP block cache, lazy waking, MK64 compile hitch, pre-build threads, compiler flags, item 27 ring port) and the Flip (GPU-bound at 1x, plays native). Also never measured: heat and battery at 60 fps. Queued: `bench/upretest-2x.txt` (Duo, its 2x cap) and `upretest-4x.txt` (Portal, Odin 3), 60 fps, GoldenEye, Perfect Dark, Conker, OoT, MK64, MM x 2: default, priority 1, priority 7, clock pin, early acquire 30, 2-image swapchain, 16x8 tiles, GPU ms per pass and wait timing logged in every run (app-spvbase = merged core 023dd9e; app-uptile16 = same tree with -DJOEY_RDP_TILE_W=16, objects deleted first: a header touch rebuilt only 5 files and left the define half-applied, caught by md5). Duo after duo-gputime (`chain48_duo_upretest.sh`), Odin 3 after its heat runs, Portal after portal-gputime (battery guard). Portal smoke test (GoldenEye 4x, 60 fps cap, Portal in its slow mode, one run each): default 28-39 fps with the upscaled pass 28.6-30.7 ms per pass (x2 per frame), 16x8 tiles 31-45 fps with 25.1-27.6 ms: a hint that tile size matters upscaled; priority 1 applied (`context 16 flags 00000013 -> 00001052 (ok)`). Duo 2x (2026-11-05 13:0x, `duo-upretest`, 6 games x 2 at 60 fps): every variant holds 60 fps in every game (GoldenEye 59.9-60.0, Perfect Dark 60.2, the rest 60.0), with the same slowest frames once the first run is past: the ~50 ms frames come only in the first run of each game for the first variant and for 16x8 tiles (a fresh install: pipeline cache misses), and Mario Kart 64's ~52 ms compile burst shows in every variant. GPU time per drawn frame at 2x, second round (the first round's first runs carry the compile cost): GoldenEye 8.3 ms, Perfect Dark 8.5, Conker 9.6, MK64 12.5, OoT and MM 16.6 (both draw 20 fps, so the GPU is about a third busy). Against the default: priority 1 -0.8% (-2.4% to +0.8% per game), priority 7 +1.0%, clock pin +0.2%, early acquire 30 +0.1%, 2 images +0.6%, all within noise; 16x8 tiles +3.3% GPU time (+0.5% to +6.1%, worse in every game), the opposite of the Portal smoke test. Lag and judder alike in all: poll->display 29.0-31.1 ms, 55-63 uneven per 5 s. On the Duo at its 2x cap the GPU has room (8-17 ms per drawn frame), so none of these ideas shows anything there either; 16x8 tiles are out for the Duo. Portal and Odin 3 at 4x still to come Portal 4x (2026-11-05 17:2x-19:0x, `portal-upretest`, 6 games x 2 at 60 fps): once the GPU was at its normal clock, every variant held 60 fps in every game (Perfect Dark 59.4-60). GPU time per drawn frame at 4x (second round): GoldenEye 11.5 ms, Perfect Dark 13.0, Conker 12.5, MK64 15.3, OoT 20.1 and MM 20.3 (both 20 drawn frames/s), so the GPU is 24-75% busy. Priority 1, priority 7, clock pin, early acquire 30 and 2 images: the same GPU time as the default to within 0.3%, and the same slow frames (Mario Kart 64's 44-47 ms compile burst in every variant). 16x8 tiles: 8-11% less GPU time in every game (GoldenEye 10.4 vs 11.5 ms, Perfect Dark 11.5 vs 13.0, MM 18.5 vs 20.3), the opposite of the Duo at 2x (+3.3%). Its extra slow frames come in first runs after its own install (cold pipeline cache), like the default's first runs. So tile size depends on the GPU (Adreno 740 gains, Adreno 722 loses). All games already hold 60 fps at 4x on the Portal, so the saving only means less power and heat there. Odin 3 4x (2026-11-05 15:2x-16:2x, `odin3-upretest`, same set): every variant holds 60 fps in every game, GPU 15-47% busy (GoldenEye 6.9 ms per drawn frame, Perfect Dark 7.9, Conker 7.9, MK64 9.2, OoT 12.3, MM 12.5). Slow frames only in each game's first run after an install (cold cache; Conker 51-59 ms); after that no frame over 33 ms in any variant. 16x8 tiles: 0.6-4.5% less GPU time (second round, average about 2%). Priority 1: Perfect Dark 7.5 vs 7.9 ms in both rounds (-5%), Conker -3%, the rest within 1%. Early acquire, 2 images, clock pin and priority 7: the same as the default. So 16x8 tiles: Adreno 740 -8 to -11%, Adreno 830 about -2%, Adreno 722 +3%. Every game already holds 60 fps at 4x on both strong devices, so the saving is only power and heat. 16x8 is kept as a build option and not made a per-GPU default unless a game turns out GPU-bound at the upscale people actually use. The first three runs of round 1 (default, priority 1, priority 7 in GoldenEye) ran at 28 fps with the GPU stuck at 220 MHz (34 ms per frame). From the clock pin run on, it ran at 680 MHz (see the Portal GPU clock item). **Portal (2026-11-07, `chain118`, 14 games x 2 unless noted; queued 2026-11-05, run only now because of the battery rule):** `portal-up4` (app-clock): uncapped 4x is -79% against 1x (95-249 fps; Conker 132, -35%); at 60 fps the Portal holds 60 in all 14 games at 1x, 2x and 4x (worst frame 32.8 / 62.0 / 32.5 ms; 2x had 14 frames over 33 ms, 1-2 in each of 10 games and all in the first round, so the first use of the 2x pipelines; 1x and 4x none). So 4x is safe on the Portal, as the Odin 3 found.
- [ ] Portal GPU clock: the Portal "slow mode" (about half speed from launch to launch, seen since 2026-11-04 and blamed on the device) is the GPU stuck at its lowest clock, 220 MHz, even when 90% busy (`portal-gputime` and `portal-neon2`: all 560 samples at 220 MHz; the msm-adreno-tz governor never raised it). It ended during the upscaled retest's clock pin run (`N64_GPU_TURBO=2`: a KGSL power constraint held by a keep-alive context), and every sample since has been at 680 MHz (`portal-pn27b`, `portal-recheck4x`; uptime 23 h, no reboot), roughly doubling uncapped fps (SM64 263 -> 586). Not yet known: whether the pin reliably gets the GPU out of the slow mode or only happened to come first, and whether the slow mode hits normal play (at 1x 60 fps 220 MHz is enough, at 4x it isn't: GoldenEye 28 fps). Next: when the slow mode comes back (a sample at 220 MHz in an uncapped run), run one pin launch and check the clock after. If the pin clears it every time, apply it automatically when the GPU sits at its minimum under load. - [ ] Duo's two speed levels: not the GPU, the core or the CPU clock (2026-11-05 `duo-gputime`: same GPU ms per frame and same CPU time in fast and slow rounds); memory/cache speed checked 2026-11-05 (Joey: "Check it"): not that either. `memmon.sh` logged every readable memory and cache clock on the Duo (bus_dcvs: DDR, LLCC, L3, each cluster's vote, the bandwidth monitors) and the prime core's time above 2.15 GHz, about once a second, beside `duo-memlv` (uncapped, GoldenEye, Snap, Smash, Paper Mario, Yoshi's Story x 4). Both levels showed: GoldenEye 365 / 263-266 fps, Snap 722-723 / 479, Paper Mario 404-408 / 326; Smash (239) and Yoshi (533-536) stayed on one level. Memory and caches ran at the same speed in fast and slow runs: DDR 2.04-2.09 GHz (its maximum) in every run, LLCC 0.99-1.06 GHz, L3 1.44-1.57 GHz, with no pattern by level (Snap slow 2.04-2.06 / fast 2.06-2.08 GHz DDR). The prime core never went above 2.15 GHz during these runs (0 boost ticks), and the emulation thread was on cpu7 in every run. What does differ: the core's time per frame (`run` in the stats line) is 1.5x longer in a slow run (Snap 2.05 vs 1.35 ms), but the emulation thread's CPU time per frame is the same (Snap 0.98 vs 0.94 ms; GoldenEye 2.5 vs 2.3 ms; Paper Mario 2.3 vs 2.2 ms, from its CPU ticks). So in a slow run the thread spends about 1 ms more per frame not running, inside the core's frame (no smart-sync waits: 0 in both): waiting for something, or runnable but not given the CPU (another thread on cpu7). Scheduler check (2026-11-05, `schedmon.sh` beside `duo-sched`: uncapped GoldenEye, Snap, Paper Mario x 4, every app thread's run time, time waiting for a free CPU, CPU and context switches each second): both levels showed again (GoldenEye 359-363 / 261-262, Snap 727-730 / 478-479, Paper Mario 399 / 325-326). It is not CPU contention: the emulation thread stays on cpu7 and waits for a free CPU only 2.6-3.5% of the time in fast and slow runs alike, with the same involuntary switches (35-54/s). It sleeps by itself: Snap runs 69-70% of the time when fast and 50% when slow, GoldenEye 86% / 66%, Paper Mario 89% / 74-75%, about one voluntary sleep per frame in both. So a slow run spends ~0.7-1 ms more per frame blocked on something, most likely the GPU side (scanout waits for parallel-RDP's work, or submission latency: GPU execution time per frame is the same in both levels, item 32), which would also fit GPU priority 1 keeping these games on the fast level (`duo-prio2`). Next: the same games with parallel-RDP's scanout wait stats (`N64_RDP_WAITSTATS=1`), queued after the merged-core run (`chain53_duo_waits2.sh`) (Duo low priority) Wait stats result (2026-11-05 16:3x, `duo-waits2`, app-gpu8, uncapped, 4 rounds; both levels showed in every game): the extra time is not in parallel-RDP. GoldenEye fast / slow: frame 2.68 / 3.71 ms (+1.03), but scanout 0.35 / 0.31 ms (drain 0.085 / 0.070) and GPU timeline waits 0 in both; Snap 1.36 / 2.05 ms (+0.69), scanout 0.22 / 0.27, GPU waits 0. Paper Mario is the exception: 2.37 / 3.01 ms (+0.63), and its GPU timeline waits (its smart-sync reads) go 0.16 -> 0.76 ms, which is all of it. The stats line's `run` covers retro_run and the present (endFrame), so for GoldenEye and Snap the missing ~0.7-1 ms per frame sits outside parallel-RDP's scanout: most likely in our present (vkQueuePresentKHR / submission), which would also fit GPU priority 1 keeping these games on the fast level, and Paper Mario's longer GPU waits fit the GPU side running later too. Next, if the Duo gets the time (low priority): split `run` into emulation and present in the stats line (the hitch log already times the three parts, only for frames over 33 ms)

**Not speed, waiting on Joey's menus or the roadmap**: SGSR/FXAA, temporal upscaler interface, 60 fps hacks, widescreen table, netplay, accessories, driver zips, second-screen output, auto/temporary savestates.

### Options for the eventual menu (Joey designs the menu; until then these are dev switches)
Things we measured that are not right as a default but help in some cases. Each line: what it does, when it helps, what it costs, how to turn it on today. Joey asked 2026-11-04 to keep these for the menu.

Speed
- [ ] Plain async parallel-RDP, no smart-sync waits (`--es opts mupen64plus-parallel-rdp-synchronous=False`): fastest, but RE2, Cruis'n USA, DK64, Yoshi's Story, New Tetris and Snap break; per game, only for games known safe
- [x] Lazy waking of the RDP worker (`N64_RDP_RING_LAZY`): on by default for Adreno (Joey 2026-11-07, see the Lazy waking item: on the merged build OoT gains, none below -0.5% on the Portal). Earlier: +4-8% on the Portal and Flip; on the current build only OoT still loses (Portal -10.5%; Snap no longer does); adaptive waking fixes OoT but costs 3% overall (Mario Kart 64 -21%), so per game or a narrower trigger
- [ ] VI filters off (`--es opts mupen64plus-parallel-rdp-vi-aa=False,mupen64plus-parallel-rdp-vi-bilinear=False,mupen64plus-parallel-rdp-divot-filter=False,mupen64plus-parallel-rdp-gamma-dither=False,mupen64plus-parallel-rdp-dither-filter=False`): Flip +5% (Snap +20%, GoldenEye +9%); costs the console look: dither grain shows, edges stair-step; dither filter alone +2.3%, AA alone nothing. Joey 2026-11-05: make this a setting ("sounds like something that should be a setting"): picture choice of console look (all filters on, as the real N64's video chip blurred every frame; the default), Sharp (`--ez sharp true`: AA and bilinear off, sharp-bilinear final scale) and Sharp2 (`--ez sharp2 true`: also the dither filter off, sharpest, may show dither grain on gradients); waits on Joey's menu design. Item 29
- [x] Max GPU clocks (`N64_GPU_TURBO=1`, Adreno only): Odin 3 GoldenEye 3.0 -> 2.8 ms per frame; more heat and battery; "performance" setting Removed 2026-11-05 (Joey), see the Force max GPU clocks item; no setting
- [ ] Android performance hint (`N64_PERF_HINT=1`): small worst-frame gain on the Odin 3 only
- [ ] Exclusive ubershaders (`PARALLEL_RDP_UBERSHADER=1`): no shader compiling at all, so no slowdown on a game's first launch; 4-8x slower on the Flip's Mali-G52 (warm cache); Portal and Odin 3 -57% (2026-11-04), so not even a first-launch choice: dropped once the Duo run agrees Not measured yet: hitches. The benches give average fps, and exclusive ubershaders' one point is no stutter while shaders compile (Joey 2026-11-04 asked). The stats line now gives the slowest frame and the frames over 33 ms every 5 s (app 'slowest frame' commit); queued 2026-11-04 on the Flip and Odin 3 after their queues (`benchhitch.sh`, `bench/hitch.txt`: default, exclusive and synchronous on a first launch, plus default warm; 6 games x 2, MM instead of 07; Joey64 app, app-hitch.apk). Keep the default unless it shows real first-launch stutter The Duo agrees (-56% warm, -60% first launch), so it is dropped as a default everywhere; it stays a switch, and comes back as an option only if the hitch runs show real first-launch stutter with the default Flip hitch result (2026-11-04 22:4x, `bench/hitch.txt`, 2 rounds; slowest frame / frames over 33 ms after the first 5 s, per round; uncapped): default warm: SM64 25 ms/0, GoldenEye 29-35/0-1, MK64 40-41/1-3, Banjo 18-31/0, MM 40-43/5-6. Default on a first launch: SM64 25-26/0, GoldenEye 34-49/1-3, MK64 98-100/9-10, Banjo 18-29/0, MM 93-108/40. Synchronous compile on a first launch: fewer slow frames but long single stalls (MK64 1.7 s, MM 363 ms). Exclusive ubershaders: worst everywhere (110-266 frames over 33 ms, slowest 120-233 ms) because every frame is slow on the Mali (14-35 fps uncapped). So on the Flip the default does stutter on a first launch in MK64 and MM (up to ~100 ms, the first 30 s), but exclusive ubershaders are no cure. OoT (rerun flip-hitch-oot; the first run gave no numbers because the bench copied the first game's ROM before Joey64 was installed, fixed in benchhitch.sh): warm 23-27 ms/0, first launch 126-304 ms/33-62 (the worst of the six), synchronous 591 ms stall/5-6, exclusive 98 ms/258-273. Odin 3 run: the first try (2026-11-05) ran the wrong set (`bench/hitch.txt` had been reused for item 23), so the real set is requeued as `bench/hitchfirst.txt` after the Odin 3's upscaled retest (`chain55_odin3_hitchfirst.sh`) Odin 3 hitch result (2026-11-05, `odin3-hitchfirst`, `bench/hitchfirst.txt`, 2 rounds, uncapped; slowest frame / frames over 33 ms after the first 5 s): with the default on a first launch (caches deleted) no game had a frame over 33 ms (slowest 15 ms, OoT), the same as warm (slowest 23 ms, MM), and fps within 4% of warm. Synchronous compile on a first launch stalls: MM 847-861 ms (4 slow frames), Mario Kart 78 ms (19), Banjo 87-88 ms (4). Exclusive ubershaders: no slow frames, but 26-80% lower fps (GoldenEye 990 -> 198). So on the Odin 3 the default already has no first-launch stutter, and neither other mode helps. First-launch stutter is a Flip-only problem (MK64 9-10 and MM 40 slow frames there), which is what the pre-build port (T1) is for.

Latency
- [ ] Frame delay (fixed 4 ms sleep after acquiring the image): -4.5 ms lag in Flip menus, but heavy scenes drop to 48 fps; light games or strong devices only
- [x] 2-image swapchain: one frame less queued; the Flip fell to ~40 fps; not tried on the Portal, Odin 3 or Duo, where it may work Dev switch back (app 08ffdaa, `N64_SWAP_IMAGES=<n>`); queued 2026-11-04 on the Portal, Odin 3 and Duo as a capped run (`benchlag.sh`: normal speed with vsync, records poll->display and uneven frames; uncapped runs can't show it), `bench/swap2.txt`, app-speed5. Duo (2026-11-05 03:0x, `duo-swap2`, 14 games x 2 at 60 fps): holds 60 fps in every game like the default, but poll->display is no lower: 25.2 vs 24.8 ms average (max 30.3 vs 29.1). No gain on the Duo. Checked 2026-11-05 from the app's log lines in the run logs: the Adreno drivers never give 2 images. Their minimum is 4 on the Duo and Odin 3 and 3 on the Portal (the Flip's Mali allows 2), and the switch asks max(n, minimum), so these runs compared the driver's minimum (4 / 3) against the default (minimum + 1 = 5 / 4): one image fewer, not two. A true 2-image swapchain is not possible on our Adreno drivers; `img2` in the upscaled retest is the same minimum-count run Portal (2026-11-05 05:0x, `portal-swap2`, 14 games x 2 at 60 fps): same as the Duo, 60 fps everywhere and no lag gain: poll->display 26.2 vs 25.8 ms average. Odin 3 (2026-11-05 11:5x, `odin3-swap2`, 14 games x 2 at 60 fps): the same, 60 fps everywhere, poll->display 24.1 vs 23.7 ms average (max 43.7 vs 43.8), uneven frames 15.5 vs 15.1 per 5 s (Perfect Dark has ~240 in both: see the Perfect Dark judder entry). Done: no lag gain on the Duo, Portal or Odin 3, and the Flip fell to ~40 fps, so it stays a dev switch
- [x] Perfect Dark judders at 60 fps on 120 Hz screens (found 2026-11-05 in the swap2 runs, Joey: "Check why"): about 240 of every ~300 frames per 5 s count as uneven on the Duo, Portal and Odin 3 alike, against 10-60 for other games. Built to see it (app 45d00c9, in every stats line now): how many refreshes each frame stayed on screen (1/2/3/4+; 2 is even at 60 fps on 120 Hz) and the mean and spread of the time between our present calls. Duo (2026-11-05, `cadence.sh`, `duo-cadence`, 60 fps, 40 s x 2 rounds, steady windows): Perfect Dark by default shows about 44% of frames for 1 refresh and 40% for 3, only 12% for 2 (3% for 4 or more); our present calls come 17.0 ms apart on average but with a spread of 11.6 ms, so frames go out in bursts (roughly 5 ms then 28 ms apart), not every 16.7 ms. Smash Bros., which also draws a new picture every frame, gets 95% of frames shown for 2 refreshes, presents 16.67 ms apart with a 0.6 ms spread. So the judder is real and it starts in our frame loop, not in the screen or the compositor. Perfect Dark also runs 0.2-0.3% fast (60.2 emulated fps) because audio paces the frames on 120 Hz screens and it gives less audio per frame than the device plays; that small drift is not the main cause (the bursts are). Timed pacing (`N64_SWAP_INTERVAL=2`) fixes the cadence: 98% of frames shown for 2 refreshes, 0 uneven, presents 17.1 ms apart with a 2.2-2.4 ms spread, 59.9 fps; but it brings ~15 audio underruns per 5 s (crackles) in Perfect Dark and GoldenEye (Smash 1), because audio no longer paces. Input to display: the earlier 'default 54-83 ms, timed 84 ms' were wrong, means over every 5 s window, including the first two (state load) and the last (the run being stopped), each holding one poll->display sample near 490 ms. In steady windows Perfect Dark is 18-24 ms by default and 21-27 ms with timed pacing, Smash 16-18 ms (duo-cadence and duo-trace logs). Next (queued on the Duo after the scheduler check, `app-trace`, `N64_FRAME_TRACE=1`): log per frame the audio the core gives, the audio wait and the frame start gap, to confirm the bursts come from the audio wait (likely: Perfect Dark hands over audio unevenly between frames, so the wait for audio room swings from 0 to ~30 ms). The fix would then be pacing that keeps frames even (smooth the audio wait, or timed pacing with the rate control holding the audio), measured for judder, lag and underruns. Trace result (2026-11-05, Duo, `duo-trace`, app-trace, 300 frames each): confirmed. Perfect Dark gives its audio every other frame: 0 samples, then 1474 (two frames' worth), every pair, while Smash gives 733-736 every frame. Audio sync waits 0 ms after the empty frame and 18-28 ms after the double one, so frames start 5-8 ms then 25-30 ms apart (Smash: 15.3-18.3 ms, audio wait 12-15 ms every frame). Timed pacing in the same trace: no audio wait, frame start gaps 15-19 ms, but 18-52 underruns per 5 s, because the double frame arrives late compared with what the ring plays. Fix built (app c6f2a97, switch `N64_AUDIO_SMOOTH=1`, off until measured): audio sync compares the fill minus a fading sum of each frame's audio above its running average (decay 0.97), so a double frame raises the wait's limit by what it brought extra and an empty frame lowers it; games that give the same audio every frame are unchanged. Queued on the Duo after its compat and scanout-wait runs (`chain56_duo_smooth.sh`, `cadence3.sh`, app-smooth = app-trace + this, tag `duo-smooth`): Perfect Dark, Smash, GoldenEye, default vs smoothed, 2 rounds, frame trace on. Default only if Perfect Dark's cadence evens out with no new underruns and Smash/GoldenEye are unchanged. Duo result (2026-11-05, `duo-smooth`, 2 rounds, steady windows, default vs smoothed): it works. Perfect Dark: uneven frames 244 -> 39 per 5 s; frames shown for exactly 2 refreshes 12% -> 83%; spread of the time between presents 11.0 -> 2.5 ms; 0 audio underruns either way; input to display 20.2 -> 18.6 ms; 60.16 -> 59.97 fps (the small speed drift is gone too). Smash unchanged (spread 0.69 vs 0.70 ms, lag 16.7 vs 16.9 ms). GoldenEye uneven 40 -> 23 (better), lag the same (18.7 vs 18.8 ms). Odin 3 (2026-11-05, `odin3-smooth`, same test; steady windows = those with no input-to-display sample over 100 ms, the same rule as the Duo numbers): confirmed. Perfect Dark: uneven 246 -> 28 per 5 s; shown exactly 2 refreshes 10% -> 87%; present spread 12.8 -> 2.3 ms; 0 underruns either way; input to display 18.3 -> 15.7 ms (worst 53 -> 23 ms); 60.20 -> 59.92 fps. Smash (uneven 23/22, lag 15.3/15.2 ms) and GoldenEye (uneven 28/25, lag 15.9/15.8 ms) unchanged, the same underruns in both (all in the state-load windows). Compat with smoothing on by default: running on the Duo (`chain70_duo_smoothdef.sh`, `duo-smoothdef`, app-smoothdef = f6b99e6 + default on, `N64_AUDIO_SMOOTH=0` off). Flip 60 Hz (2026-11-05, `flip-smooth`): nothing to fix there. Vsync paces at 60 Hz, so Perfect Dark shows 97% of frames for exactly 1 refresh with or without smoothing, and steady windows are the same (lag 45-52 ms, 0-1 underruns). The one difference is the third 5 s window after loading, while the Flip compiles the game's code: 16 and 10 underruns smoothed vs 5 and 3 default. So smoothing goes on only where it helps: automatic, on when the panel refreshes faster than ~66 Hz (refresh period from display timing), off at 60 Hz or when the period is unknown; `N64_AUDIO_SMOOTH=1/0` still forces it (built uncommitted as app-smoothauto, patch in the scratchpad's tmp-pn/smoothauto.patch). Checks queued: Duo compat with smoothing always on (`duo-smoothdef`, covers the 120 Hz case), then `chain71_smoothauto.sh` (Duo and Flip, Perfect Dark auto vs forced on: the Duo must log it on and match, the Flip must stay off). Commit as the default when both pass. Duo compat with smoothing always on (2026-11-05, `duo-smoothdef`, app-smoothdef, bundled core): 82/82 start, 0 end black (blackcheck vs duo-gpu8c and odin3), screenshot differences timing-sized (largest 28). But underruns in steady windows were 31% higher than duo-gpu8c (6924 -> 9076, spread over many games: Majora's Mask 10 -> 107, Glover 1 -> 88, OoT 17 -> 97, Turok 38 -> 125), and that reference ran a different core on a different day, while the same-build A/B runs (Perfect Dark, Smash, GoldenEye) showed none. Not the default until a same-build A/B settles it: `chain72_duo_smoothab.sh` (after chain71), the 8 games that rose most, smoothing on vs `N64_AUDIO_SMOOTH=0`, alternating, 2 rounds (tags duo-sab-on/off-<round>). Auto check (`chain71`, app-smoothauto, Perfect Dark, 1 round): works on both. Duo logs 'smoothed audio wait on (panel refresh 8.34 ms)' and its steady windows match forced-on (uneven 42-66 vs 32-42 forced, ~244 off; lag 16-22 ms; 0 underruns); Flip logs no 'on' line, so it stays off at 60 Hz. Same-build A/B (2026-11-05, `chain72`, app-smoothdef, the 8 games whose underruns rose most in duo-smoothdef, on vs `N64_AUDIO_SMOOTH=0`, alternating, 2 rounds): steady-window underruns on 180 / 161, off 159 / 166, inside the round-to-round spread, so the 31% was the other core and day. One consistent spot: Majora's Mask's intro at ~30 s, 12-13 underruns on vs 5-6 off (a crackle either way, slightly longer). **Default since app 6de8bb8 (2026-11-05): smoothing automatic, on above ~66 Hz, off at 60 Hz; `N64_AUDIO_SMOOTH=1/0` forces it.**
- [ ] 60 Hz display mode on 120 Hz screens: no judder, a bit more lag; timed pacing `N64_SWAP_INTERVAL=2` is already a dev switch

Accuracy fallbacks for problem games
- [ ] LLE audio (`N64_HLE_AUDIO=0`): for games whose audio is wrong with HLE; costs CPU
- [ ] Plain sync instead of smart sync (`N64_RDP_SMART=0`)
- [ ] Page-level smart sync (`N64_RDP_SMART_PRECISE=0`): more waits, for games that break with the precise version
- [ ] Present every refresh (`N64_SKIP_DUPES=0`): for games where skipping repeated frames looks wrong
- [ ] GPU driver per game: faux123 Turnip / Turnip Gen8 / system driver (`--es driver`)
- [ ] faux123 Turnip v1.2.7 (2026-11-06; we bundle v1.2.5): v1.2.6 adds Adreno 710/720, v1.2.7 splits submits of more than 256 command buffers (they crashed or hung before); settings for the 722 unchanged, author measured no speed change. Parked, Joey 2026-11-06: "Not now". When picked up: download the 2.6 MB adpkg, check the SHA-256 from the release, Duo compat 82 + quick bench vs v1.2.5, and see whether the 4x geometry drops change
- [ ] Renderer per game: GLideN64 for widescreen hacks, texture packs or cheap upscaling on weak GPUs (`--es renderer gliden64`); kept, no work on it. Parked with it (no measurements planned): ROCKNIX's GLideN64 "performance" profile for weak GPUs, and an A/B of the `NEON_OPT`/`CRC_ARMV8` build flags, which are built into our core (f0a7d10) but were never measured.

Display (already switches)
- [ ] Sharp scaling (`--ez sharp true`), upscaling 2x/4x (native 1x is the default), upscaling cap override on the Duo (`--ez nocap true`)

Being measured now (move here if they help only in some cases): relaxed depth reads, spin waits, coherent RDRAM memory, early GPU kicks, thread priority (see the deep dive items)

Dropped for good (no case where they helped): pre-rotation, shared present mode, compiler flags/LTO/PGO, malisx2, the PIF copy-range patch, the command worker's spin-before-sleep, the game usage tag, sustained performance mode, presentMargin, synchronous/adaptive swapchain acquire (`N64_SYNC_ACQUIRE`: 10 ms less in Flip menus but broke matches), CPU wait before present (`N64_PRESENT_CPU_WAIT`, hakuX: no gain), 4 swapchain images (dip +5 fps for +21 ms), GPU spin after scanout on the Mali (`N64_RDP_GPU_SPIN`: holds the clock up, slow frames not repeatably fewer)
- [ ] Review of dropped items (2026-11-05, Joey: "have we been measuring the right things before dropping them, and do any have positives?"). Measured right, nothing found: compiler flags/LTO/PGO, PIF copy-range, spirv-opt (counters too), RSP chunk cache, status-register polling, Mali GPU spin (60 fps, slow frames, 3 rounds; the Flip's slow frames are dynarec compile bursts, which a GPU clock cannot fix), coherent memory (breaks the Mali), shared present mode, usage tags. Positives that were real but lost to a bigger cost: synchronous acquire -10 ms lag in Flip menus (broke matches); 4 swapchain images +5 fps in the Flip's dip (+21 ms lag); present thread lifted the Flip dip 41-50 -> ~55 fps (+8-13 ms; on today's build the dip is emulation time and it buys nothing); malisx2 ran OoT 44 vs 33 fps on the Flip at 60 fps (others slower; OoT now runs 139 fps uncapped on the stock driver, so moot); frame delay -4.5 ms in Flip menus (options list). Measured under the wrong conditions (recheck): (1) CPU wait/fence spins, early kicks, relaxed depth, SUBGROUP/SMALL_TYPES on Adreno, coherent memory on Adreno and thread priority were all judged uncapped at native 1x: uncapped keeps the clocks pinned, so ideas meant to stop clocks dropping between frames cannot show, and at 1x the Adrenos have 4-12x the GPU they need, so GPU-side ideas cannot show either; the queued upscaled retest covers only priority, clock pin, early acquire, minimum swapchain and 16x8 tiles. Recheck at 60 fps with 4x upscaling (Odin 3, Portal; Duo 2x), read slowest frames, frames over 33 ms and GPU ms, not fps. (2) Thread priority was measured on a quiet device; what it is for is a busy one (downloads, other apps): recheck worst frames with a background load. (3) Pre-rotation was tried only on the Portal and Duo (no lag change). All four panels are portrait-native (1080x1920, Flip 752x1336), so in landscape the display hardware or the GPU rotates every frame; on the Flip, the weakest GPU and never tested, check whether SurfaceFlinger composes our layer on the GPU (CLIENT composition) in a game, and if so test pre-rotation there for fps in the dip and lag. (4) Sustained performance mode was checked only on the Flip and Portal (unsupported); check the Duo and Odin 3. presentMargin likewise (garbage on the Flip and Portal). (5) Exclusive ubershaders were dropped on average uncapped fps (-57% on the Portal and Odin 3), but even at -57% those GPUs run far above 60 fps, and the point is no first-launch stutter: the Odin 3's queued first-launch run (`odin3-hitchfirst`, uber-cold variant) answers it; if it holds 60 with fewer slow frames, it is an option for strong Adrenos. (6) The 2-image swapchain on Adreno was never 2 images (driver minimum 3-4, see its entry) Queued 2026-11-05 16:0x (Joey: "Yes, queue them"): (1) `bench/recheck4x.txt` on the Odin 3 and Portal: def / wait spin 300 us / fence spin 300 us / kicks (2 passes, 250 us) / relaxed depth / SUBGROUP=0 / SMALL_TYPES=0 / coherent RDRAM, 60 fps with 4x upscaling, GoldenEye, Perfect Dark, Conker, OoT, MK64, MM x 2 rounds (`odin3-recheck4x`, `portal-recheck4x`). The switches lived only on the old speed branch, so they were carried onto the merged core (core branch `recheck`, c7e0eb6 = 023dd9e + 179a77e + 3d58f18; the lazy-waking calls in the old spin code dropped, gpu2 has no lazy ring) and built from clean objects into app-recheck.apk (core md5 79a51151ee8c, app fa2efba). (2) `bench/prioload.txt` with `benchload.sh` (benchhitch4 plus 3 shell busy loops at normal priority during each run, stopped before the next run's heat check): def / emu thread -19 / every thread -19, 60 fps native, same 6 games x 2 (`odin3-prioload`, `portal-prioload`). Both sit ahead of the 11-04 runs: Odin 3 after SoftRDP, first-launch hitch, GPU time and heat (`chain64_odin3_queue2.sh`), Portal after early acquire, NEON, GPU time, upscaled retest and the item 27 port bench (`chain11_portal_queue3.sh`). (3) Flip, after its queue (`chain65_flip_comp.sh`, `devcheck.sh`): GoldenEye and OoT at 60 fps, dumpsys SurfaceFlinger saved to `results/devcheck/flip-comp/` to read our layer's composition (DEVICE vs CLIENT) and transform. (4) Sustained performance mode and presentMargin: app fa2efba logs sustained-mode support at start and, with `N64_PRESENT_MARGIN=1`, presentMargin mean/min/max per 300 frames; the Duo after its queue (`chain66_duo_checks.sh`), the Odin 3 and Portal in their prioload runs. Smoke test on the Portal: sustained mode unsupported (as found 11-03). Results: (4) done 2026-11-05. Sustained performance mode is off on all four devices: Android's flag `config_sustainedPerformanceModeSupported` reads false on the Duo, Odin 3, Portal and Flip, also with vendor overlays applied (`cmd overlay lookup`), so asking for it does nothing anywhere; the Duo's own log line was lost because the Duo keeps only error-level lines. presentMargin on the Duo (`duo-checks`, GoldenEye and OoT at 60 fps): real numbers here, unlike the Flip and Portal: frames are ready on average 3.6-5.7 ms before the last moment they could be and still show on time, max 8.3 ms (one 120 Hz refresh), but the minimum in every 300-frame window is 0-1 ms. So there is no steady slack to start frames later (frame delay) on the Duo: the worst frames already arrive just in time. Composition on the Duo: our game layer is composed by the display hardware (DEVICE) with the 90 degree turn done there too, in both games, so pre-rotation could not save GPU work on the Duo. The only GPU-composed (CLIENT) layer is Android's rounded-corner overlay (ScreenDecorHwcOverlay), a system layer we do not control. (3) Flip result (2026-11-05, `flip-comp`, GoldenEye and OoT at 60 fps): the Flip composes on the GPU. SurfaceFlinger draws our game layer (ROT_90) and the FPS counter panel with the Mali every frame (CLIENT, `usesDeviceComposition=false` for the whole display), where the Duo's display hardware takes our layer. The cause is most likely not the rotation: the Unisoc composer's own table lists our layer as a DPU layer with transform 90, so its hardware can turn it. The display carries a colour matrix (diagonal 1.47/1.17/1.56) from `persist.sys.sf.color_saturation = 1.6`, a saturation boost on everything (the Duo's matrix is plain identity), and a colour matrix the composer cannot apply makes Android compose everything on the GPU. It is a saved device setting (not in the build files), so Joey's to change, not ours: test = set it to 1.0 (`adb shell service call SurfaceFlinger 1022 f 1.0` lasts until reboot; colours look less vivid), then repeat flip-comp plus a lag/fps run (poll->display, the Mario Tennis dip) to see what the GPU composition costs. Pre-rotation only if the layer stays CLIENT with the boost off. Result (2026-11-05 17:0x, Joey set the Flip's saturation to 1.0 in Retroid's settings, then rebooted: SurfaceFlinger reads it only at start; `flip-sat`, same build and games as `flip-comp`): confirmed. With the boost off the colour matrix is identity and the display hardware takes our layer and the FPS counter (DEVICE, `usesDeviceComposition=true`), 90 degree turn included, so pre-rotation is not needed. Steady windows (2 per game, 5 s each): input to display GoldenEye 35.7/44.9 -> 31.0/32.5 ms (worst 58.5-60.6 -> 42.4-47.4), OoT 42.2/38.8 -> 33.4/31.5 ms, so about 8 ms less (40 -> 32 ms); frame time GoldenEye 6.7 -> 5.8-6.2 ms, OoT the same 6.1-6.2. So on the Flip, Retroid's saturation boost costs ~8 ms of input lag in every game through GPU composition. It is a device setting we cannot change from the app; a note for Flip users (or a hint in the app when `persist.sys.sf.color_saturation` is not 1.0) is a design question for Joey. Flip lag figures measured before 2026-11-05 17:00 include the boost Also from this run: sustained mode false on the Flip (its log keeps warnings), swapchain min 2, asking 3. Result (1), recheck at 60 fps with 4x upscaling (`odin3-recheck4x` 2026-11-05 23:25, `portal-recheck4x` 20:28; 6 games x 2 rounds, slowest frame with the first 5 s window left out): nothing. In round 2 every switch matches the default within ~1 ms on both devices (Odin 3 GoldenEye 9.2-9.7 ms, OoT 5.1-5.4, Conker 14.1-15.8; Portal GoldenEye 13.9-14.6, OoT 9.6-11.7); no frames over 33 ms on the Odin 3, the same 4 on the Portal for every variant (Mario Kart 64, which runs at 30 fps there). Round 1 is slower only for the first variant run per game (the default) and for SMALL_TYPES=0 (Odin 3 OoT 18.5 vs 5.3 ms, Portal every game ~44 ms): shader variants compiling on first use, not the switches. So wait spins, fence spins, early kicks, relaxed depth, SUBGROUP=0 and coherent RDRAM stay dropped at 4x too, and SMALL_TYPES=0 has no gain to set against its first-run compiles. Result (2), thread priority with 3 busy loops running (`portal-prioload` 22:09, 60 fps native, 6 games x 2): Portal no difference (slowest frames equal; the default's two outliers, Perfect Dark 10.2 and Conker 22.1 ms, are single runs; no frames over 33 ms in any variant). Odin 3 (`odin3-prioload`, finished 2026-11-06 00:40): a real gain. With 3 busy loops running, the default's emulation thread needs 1.6-4.4x more time per frame than at nice -19 (mean run ms per frame: GoldenEye 4.75 vs 1.08, Conker 2.94 vs 1.25, Perfect Dark 3.04 vs 1.69, OoT 2.50 vs 1.59, MK64 2.27 vs 1.31, MM 3.33 vs 2.11), and its slowest frames are 12-28 ms against 4.5-7.6 ms in GoldenEye, OoT, MM and Perfect Dark, in both rounds (Conker 48 ms with 13 frames over 33 ms once). The emu thread alone at -19 does it; every thread at -19 adds nothing. Not heat: every Odin 3 run of every variant ended at thermal status 3 (fan off), every Portal run at 0. On the Portal only Conker gains (run 2.29 vs 1.49 ms). On a quiet device priority changed nothing on any of the four (2026-11-04/05 runs), so it costs nothing where it does not help. Next: a compat run with `N64_THREAD_PRIO=-19` on the Odin 3 (`odin3-priocompat`, chain99, after its texture pack run); if it passes, the emu thread at -19 is a candidate default (Joey decides). Duo (`duo-prioload`, 2026-11-06 01:15, same 6 games x 2): no effect; run ms per frame equal in every game (GoldenEye 2.73 / 2.73 / 2.79, Perfect Dark 3.64 / 3.73 / 3.87), slowest frames mixed both ways (Mario Kart 64 52 ms with 2-3 frames over 33 ms in every variant). So under load it helps the Odin 3 a lot, the Portal in Conker only, the Duo not at all, and costs nothing anywhere

### Later
- [ ] Future roadmap (Joey 2026-11-04): 16-bit storage for GPUs without it (Adreno 610/618/630) so parallel-RDP could run there: either Turnip lowers 16-bit storage-buffer access to 32-bit (stores as 32-bit atomics, since neighbouring 16-bit values are written by different threads; needs our own Mesa build) or parallel-RDP's shaders keep 16-bit values packed in 32-bit words (most RDP shaders). Days of work, and an Adreno 610 is likely too slow for parallel-RDP at full speed anyway
- [ ] Future roadmap (Joey 2026-11-04, 'add 5 to roadmap'; pitched 2026-11-03): N64-style octagonal stick gate with a per-game response curve; a Controller Pak manager; an on-screen measured input-to-display lag readout; profiles budgeted for power/heat; ahead-of-time compiled RSP microcode. GLideN64-based ones stay parked: auto-tuning GLideN64 options against parallel-RDP frames, RT64-style frame interpolation **AOT RSP microcode queued 2026-11-07 as work order item (22)** (Joey: "Queue all of these").
- [ ] RetroAchievements (reuse the mupen64plus-ae fork's work; note it's GPLv3)
- [ ] Display options: widescreen hacks, integer scaling, filters/CRT shaders, GLideN64 texture packs
- [x] "Sharp" display setting (Joey, 2026-11-04): native 1x on parallel-RDP looks blurry next to GLideN64; the accurate native look stays the default, Sharp is opt-in (Joey: "Keep it as an option"). Sharp turns the VI anti-aliasing and VI bilinear off and scales to the screen sharp-bilinear: nearest to the first whole multiple at least the screen size (OoT 640x480 to 1280x960), then one linear step down (VkContext::present, two vkCmdBlitImage). Rounding the multiple down left the Flip (752 lines) at 1x, a plain linear stretch. Checked in OoT on the Flip: Link, sash and shield clearly crisper, edges more jagged without the VI AA; textures stay soft (the game's own filtering). Dev switch `--ez sharp true` (NativeBridge.setSharp can switch it while running) until Joey designs the settings screen. Sharp2 (Joey 2026-11-05, 'Yes, build it'): `--ez sharp2 true` = Sharp plus the VI dither filter off, since the Flip screenshots put most of the native softness on that filter (edge contrast vs default: all VI filters off +38%, dither filter off alone +28%, VI AA off alone +9%; the price is the dither grain on flat surfaces). Built (app-sharp2 = merged core gpu8 + the switch); screenshots default / sharp / sharp2 of 6 games on the Flip (`chain61_flip_sharp.sh`, `sharpshot.sh`, results/sharpshot/flip; side-by-side sheet results/sharpshot/sharp-compare-flip.png, sent to Joey 2026-11-05). Result: edge contrast against the default look, OoT +121% / +212% (sharp / sharp2), SM64 +103% / +173%, GoldenEye +78% / +189%, Pokemon Snap +138% / +209%, Smash +171% / +224% (Perfect Dark not comparable: its shots caught different screens). Sharp makes outlines crisp (Mario, Link, Smash portraits) with jagged edges where the VI anti-aliasing used to smooth them; Sharp2 adds the N64's dither pattern as a fine grain on flat colours (SM64 grass and sand, GoldenEye walls, OoT walls), and part of its extra edge number is that grain, not detail. Both work; which look(s) the menu offers is Joey's call with the settings design.
- [ ] Sharp + dither filter off: crisper but shows the dither dots; show Joey once
- [x] Mario Party 2 text boxes show lines (old text strokes) on the Duo only (fixed by making faux123 the Duo's driver, 2026-11-04), in every Duo compat run back to the first; Flip and Portal screenshots are clean. Not caused by the 11-04/05 changes (still there with lazy waking, repeat skip and precise sync all off). Cause: the bundled Turnip (Gen8 V38) on the Adreno 722. Still there with plain sync (`N64_RDP_SMART=0`); the Qualcomm system driver gives a black screen at 6-11 fps; faux123 Turnip v1.2.5 (dev driver) draws the text right (Joey, live, 2026-11-04). The Odin 3 (Adreno 830, same bundled Turnip) is clean. Duo compat on faux123 done + lazy waking (`duo-faux-lazy`) (done: the Duo has faux123 by default, see the Duo driver item)
- [ ] SGSR upscaling (Snapdragon Game Super Resolution, hakuX) and FXAA for the final scale to the screen instead of bilinear: sharper for almost no cost, most useful where upscaling is capped (Duo 2x) or at 1x on the Flip; also during fast-forward, which drops parallel-RDP to 1x

Eden's model for the UI: a ReShade .fx catalog, a chain the user orders, per-value sliders and presets
- [ ] 60 fps hacks per game, an optional enhancement like widescreen, not a fix: community 60 fps codes/patches for 20-30 fps games (GoldenEye, Perfect Dark, Banjo, OoT, Star Fox 64), off by default and toggled per game from the library like hakuX's per-title 60/120 fps mods. Most need a CPU overclock to hold the higher rate, so the hack turns that on with it

Delay-based first (same servers as simple64/RMG/gopher64 if the protocol matches); rollback later, using flycast's trick of savestates that hold only the RAM pages a frame changed (no N64 emulator ships rollback yet)
  - [ ] In-game netplay screens, APPROVED as drawn (Joey 2026-11-10; room lost: OK only, no Rejoin). **Built 2026-11-10** (`netplay/NetplayInGame.kt`, PauseScreens `NetPaused` / `NetOutOfStep` / `NetLeft`, MainActivity `netWatch`, library `NetEndedPopup`; unit tests pass): (1)-(6) as drawn, the player-left popup, and the library's Couldn't reach the room / Your copy doesn't match the host's for codes 4 / 6. Names and pings: the engine has them now (main 638500a: `netplayPlayers()` "1|Odin3|5;2|Odin2 Portal|118", `netplayInfo()[6]` = own player, `N64_NETPLAY_NAME`); merge main and use them for (players chip, "Sam (Odin 3)", "Kim's connection is slow", "Alex's room", the host's copy in the mismatch text; until then "Player N", "the room", "the host"); code 5 (turned down) built as Joey picked (mockup B 2026-11-10, build_netplay4.py: "Couldn't join the room", Try again / OK); Joey on the player-left popup without its Wait row (rejoin is after the first testing build; text "You can carry on without Player N."). Not checked on devices: an in-game room needs two devices with the netplay core (Thor-only rule); the library popups can be shown on the Thor with `SHOW --es what net-lost|net-unreach|net-mismatch`. Was: to build after Share logs (mockup sent 2026-11-10, scratchpad build_netplay3.py / netplay3_mock.png). The engine (perf/compat session: joey64 main 1bb1a13, core `netplay` 82d790b) ran 1P, 2P and 3P Mario Kart in step; its bridge is `NativeBridge.netplayInfo()` and `coreShutDown() == 3`. Drawn: (1) player N paused, on every other screen; (2) the pauser's menu line; (3) waiting for player N (slow connection) pop-up; (4) the room is lost, back to the library (question: Rejoin or just OK?); (5) out of step (Keep playing / Leave; host: End for everyone); (6) a blocked hotkey pop-up, its pause menu rows greyed. Already approved in Play together v2: Your room, Couldn't reach the room, copy mismatch, player-left popup. Engine answers (2026-11-10, core `netplay` eb2781a, app 91c8013): a failed join stops the game, `coreShutDown()` 4 = room can't be reached (Couldn't reach the room), 5 = the room turned this player down (slot taken or full), 6 = this ROM isn't the host's (Your copy doesn't match), 3 = lost connection mid-game (room lost); hand the code to the library like GameStartFailed. Player-left popup: `pause(true)` on the host while it is up pauses everyone (their netplayInfo()[4] reads 1), `pause(false)` carries on; Carry on without them already works. Code 5 has no approved screen yet: ask Joey when building (Couldn't reach is the nearest). UPnP (app b1f541b): `NativeBridge.netplayOutside()` = "<state> <ip:port>", 0 off, 1 looking (~4-5 s), 2 open, 3 no UPnP router, 4 router refused, 5 opened behind another NAT. Your room (approved v2): 2 = the green line "Players anywhere can join with the code"; 3, 4 and 5 = the orange "Only players on this Wi-Fi can join" line (5 isn't drawn: same line, ask Joey if it needs its own words); 1 (looking) has no drawn state: ask Joey.

  - Open bugs to fix (2026-11-10 list, from gamedb and the open-bugs device queue): Cruis'n USA block-list black (Flip); Mario Artist Paint Studio 1999 beta draws nothing (64DD development disk, low priority); Majora's Mask white glow over the floor (Flip, one run of two); Majora's Mask first-launch stutter while shaders compile (Flip, 93-108 ms); audio underruns while half the frames repeat (T820 report, not reproduced on ours); Mario Kart 64 first-area hitch (= item 36). With Joey by hand or parked: Resident Evil 2 rooms in gameplay (Joey "I will do"), Banjo-Tooie music (no save). Frontend session's own: Tony Hawk's Pro Skater 2 and New Tetris with 4x run-ahead (T9). Bench-only, not a user bug: Cruis'n USA bench state stops on the Controller Pak notice.
  - Progress 2026-11-10: (3) Yoshi's Story: the aspect code (`8106BFCA 3FE3` ...) changes nothing visible at 16:9 on the Odin 2 Portal (plain and code pictures alike: the playfield is flat art, so its width doesn't come from guPerspective's aspect), so it goes to the widescreen sweep as not working; the demo states (Banjo-Kazooie, Mario Kart 64, Super Smash Bros., Diddy Kong Racing, at 1800/3600/5400 frames) were taken and mtxcmds found projection commands in each, for the sweep. Netplay: core branch `netplay` (joey64-core-net, f17aa73: the mupen64plus netplay client on POSIX sockets, no SDL; `N64_NETPLAY=host:port:player`) and the app's room server (`app/src/main/cpp/netplay_server.cpp`, the same protocol, in the host's own process; dev `N64_NETPLAY_SERVE=port:players:delay` opens a room and joins it as player 1). The server's loopback test (scratchpad nptest: registration, settings and save relay, input delay, gratuitous input, lag, desync flag, a player leaving, restart) passes on the Odin 2 Portal; two devices on Wi-Fi (Duo hosts, Odin 3 Portal joins, Mario Kart 64; scratchpad compat/np4.sh, app-net-5 = core bf3f080 0843c7): both at 60 fps in step from the title through a 2.5-minute race, every sync check matched (13, VI 0 to 7200), no lost connection, the two screens show the same race (timers 0.07-0.14 s apart, as the screenshots were taken; the minimap racers in the same places). Two fixes it took: the core asked for netplay API 0x010000 and the core has 0x010001, so the check failed without a word and both played offline (now logged); each input went out once over UDP, so one lost packet stopped both games for 10 s and then "lost connection" (np2: 25 s in), now each player's last 8 inputs are sent again (core bf3f080). `NativeBridge.netplayInfo()` and `coreShutDown() == 3` (connection lost) are for the Play together screens. Later the same day (Joey: "Yes we need to try 2p, 3p and 4p"; scratchpad compat/npgo.sh, npshot.sh, npstop.sh, npsim.sh): **2P** (Duo hosts, Odin 3 Portal) VS race Mario against Toad, each device steering its own kart, the split screens the same on both, 25 sync checks matched; **3P** (+ Odin 3) VS race Mario, Luigi, Wario, each picked from its own device, the same on all three. Found and fixed: a pause menu held more than 10 s dropped every other player ("lost connection"), so the core now waits as long as the room still answers and is lost only after 10 s of silence (core 82d790b); pause notices: each app tells the room when its player pauses, the others log "player N paused" / "player N is back after N ms" (host and joiner checked, 13-16 s pauses); connection numbers in the session log: `Netplay: net:` (waits for input, inputs, copies, requests, lag) every 5 s, `netplay: ping` (a real echo through the room) every 10 s, the room's per-player line every 10 s. After a resume the host waited 139 times in 5 s (2.6 s, worst 100 ms) and was back to 0 within 10 s. The Odin 3's Wi-Fi is slower (34 ms answers against 1-7 ms). Simulated internet (`N64_NETPLAY_SIM=delay:jitter:loss` on the room, 2P, 60 s each): 40:20:2 with input delay 2 held ~60 fps but waited ~1.9 s in 5 s; with delay 4: 0 waits, 60.0 fps, ping 52-72 ms; 100:30:5 with delay 6: 0 waits, 60.0 fps, ping 119-130 ms, 5% dropped, no desync. So input delay should follow the ping: about ping / 2 / 16.7 ms + 1-2 frames (2 on Wi-Fi). Still to do: 4P (Odin 2 Portal when its compat run ends), Mali (Flip) against Adreno, a real remote player (needs Joey: a hotspot, and port forwarding or UPnP), automatic input delay. Later still (2026-11-10): **failed joins stop the game** (core eb2781a, app 91c8013; Frontend request #2, approved Play together v2): `NativeBridge.coreShutDown` 4 = the room can't be reached (or the version check failed, or it isn't a Joey64 room), 5 = the room turned the player down (taken or full), 6 = the ROM isn't the host's; never offline play. **ROM check** (request #10): the joiner sends its ROM MD5 (Joey64 TCP message 0x40) before registering; the room compares it with the one from 127.0.0.1 (the host's own game). Duo host + Odin 3 Portal: same ROM joins, another ROM 6, no room 4, a taken player 5. That last case found a bug: the registration id came from an unseeded `rand()`, the same on every device, so the room took a second device asking for a taken player as a re-registration; now `arc4random()`. **4P** (np4p-2: Odin 3 Portal hosts, Duo, Odin 3, Odin 2 Portal): VS race on Luigi Raceway, Yoshi / Luigi / Wario / Bowser each picked on its own device, the same split screen on all four, 19 sync checks matched, no desync, ~59-60 fps everywhere; at input delay 2 the host waited 50-180 times per 5 s (0.5-3.5 s), the joiners little, so 4 players on Wi-Fi need more delay (automatic input delay item). Device keys: the Duo has a second screen that takes key focus back (tap display 0 first, `input -d 0 tap 5 5`); the Odin 2 Portal's and Odin 3's N64 A is keycode 97. Rejoin after a drop: back of the queue, not in the first testing build (Joey 2026-11-10). UPnP: Joey "Yes, add UPnP" (2026-11-10): miniupnpc + libnatpmp (BSD-3), map the room's TCP and UDP port while hosting, remove on stop, log the outside address; licences entries. Other emulators' netplay (Joey 2026-11-10, "Research other emulators netplay"): M64Plus AE (Android, GPL-3, the same mupen64plus protocol): the host runs the room on the device as we do, opens its ports with miniupnpc (UPnP) or libnatpmp (NAT-PMP), finds LAN rooms with Android NSD, and for the internet a small room-code server only tells joiners the host's address (`OnlineNetplayHandler`: register the room port, get a code, a joiner trades the code for IP:port; no relay). RMG (mupen64plus protocol): a lobby over WebSocket (request_create_room and so on) on servers listed in a server-list JSON the user gives (none by default); the room runs on that server, which relays everything. gopher64: WebRTC data channels (matchbox) with a WebSocket lobby (netplay.gopher64.com) and ICE (STUN, and TURN relays the server hands out), GGRS sessions used as lockstep with input delay (rollback states commented out), the host can change input delay mid-game, CP0 registers compared for desyncs. RetroArch: rollback by replay (late remote input predicted, rewound and replayed), TCP through the host, CRC per frame and a savestate sent to fix a desync, late join and spectators by sending a savestate (SYNC); libretro's lobby and relay servers (not in the docs read). Dolphin: lockstep, host-set pad buffer (1 frame per 15 ms of ping per client), Fair / Host Input Authority / Golf modes, a traversal server for NAT punching (no added lag; strict NAT needs a forwarded port), host's saves and codes synced, checksum of the game before start. souldew (Joey's link): not emulator netplay; it syncs a Pokémon tracker document over WebRTC with public MQTT relays as fallback, so only its connection ideas (WebRTC first, relay when a router blocks it, a 6-digit invite code) carry over. For us: UPnP + NAT-PMP (AE's way) first; a room-code lookup server (AE) or a relay room server (RMG, "server rooms") if UPnP fails often; Dolphin's ping rule for automatic input delay and gopher64's mid-game delay change; RetroArch's savestate-on-join is how rejoin would work. **UPnP built** (app b1f541b, miniupnpc 2.3.3 in `app/src/main/cpp/third_party/miniupnpc`, `upnp.cpp`): a hosted room asks the router to open its port (TCP and UDP, an hour's lease renewed every 30 min, removed when the room closes) on its own thread; `NativeBridge.netplayOutside()` gives the state (looking, open, no UPnP router, refused, behind a second NAT) and the outside address; `N64_NETPLAY_UPNP=0` skips it. Odin 2 Portal on Joey's router: open in 4.2-4.6 s, closed again on quit. Not done: NAT-PMP (libnatpmp, AE has it: Apple routers; needs Joey's go to download). Found and fixed on the way: quitting a room nobody had joined took ~20 s (the quit save waited for a game stuck in the room's registration wait, then the core waited up to 30 s): a state request now fails at once while the netplay core waits, and leaving closes the room before the core stops (~1 s now). A joiner leaving while its room still waits for others can still wait up to the room's 30 s. **First real internet room** (2026-11-10, npnet1/2): Odin 3 hosting on Joey's home Wi-Fi (UPnP open in 3.7-4.4 s), Odin 2 Portal on Joey's phone hotspot (cellular) joining at the router's outside address: ROM check, registration and sync all fine, no desync. Input delay 4: 48-58 fps, 0.4-1.4 s of waits per 5 s, cellular ping 84-146 ms with spikes to 370-458 ms. Input delay 8 (133 ms): 59.9 fps on both, 0-3 waits per 5 s, ping 63 ms (worst 208). So on cellular about 8 frames; Dolphin's rule (1 frame per 15 ms of ping) gives 4-10 here, which is where automatic input delay should start (it needs the worst ping, not the average). Run-ahead is off in a room (Frontend d434b36). **Input delay sweep on cellular** (npdelays, same two devices, 90 s each, Joey "Test it all"): delay 2: 44.7 fps, 1.6-1.9 s of waits per 5 s; 3: 50 fps, 1.0-1.2 s; 4: 53.3 fps, 0.75 s; 5: 58.4 fps, 0.22-0.25 s; 6: 53 fps, 0.7 s (a 1.7 s ping spike in that run); 7: 58.6 fps, 0.18-0.21 s; 8: 57.8-59.1 fps, 0.13-0.18 s. Ping averaged 88-165 ms with spikes of 436-1720 ms in every run, no desync in any. So on cellular: below 5 it stalls all the time, 5-8 holds ~58-59 fps with short stalls on the spikes, and no delay hides a 0.5-1.7 s spike (only rollback could). For home internet expect 2-4. Adaptive input delay (raise on waits, lower after a calm stretch; gopher64 changes delay mid-game) waits for Joey's go; rollback is a later item for Joey. **Players list** (app 638500a, Frontend request): NativeBridge.netplayPlayers() "1|Odin3|5;2|Odin2 Portal|118" (name from N64_NETPLAY_NAME or the device model, each player's own smoothed ping), netplayInfo()[6] = this game's player; the room collects them from the pause notices and sends the list once a second; checked over the internet room. Still to do for netplay: save states, rewind, run-ahead and fast-forward must be off in a room (they change the input count), and the Play together screens (Frontend session).

- [ ] 64DD (disk add-on, mostly Japan-only games): ares supports it, mupen64plus partly; check what the core does, disk saves **Built 2026-11-07 (Joey: "move up dd support", then "Yes, with core fix"):** core `dd` ddf3103 (from gliden64-update a8ab829 + the short-state check 05562df): a disk alone starts with no cartridge (upstream read past the end of its list), disk saves go to the save folder as `<disk file>.disk_save` (RetroArch's name; the whole disk, 64.9 MB, written as the game writes), the IPL is looked up by the disk's region (IPL_JPN/IPL_USA/IPL_DEV.n64, then IPL.n64), and a start that fails (no IPL for the region) no longer crashes retro_run. App `dd` (joey64-auditfix, from launcher/phase0 1f6338a): d47a78f (.ndd/.d64 plain or in zip/7z, `retro_load_game_special`, EXTRA_DD_CART for a cartridge, IPL check), 825e7a1 (disk saves in the data folder sync, streamed; SrmSyncTest), 9a73296; unit tests 272/272. Flip (flip-dd1, flip-dd2, Japan v1.2 IPL): SimCity 64, Mario Artist Paint Studio (writes its disk save), Kyojin no Doshin, Super Mario 64 Disk Version (proto), Nihon Pro Golf Tour 64 boot at full speed; Dezaemon DD (a development disk) needs the Tool IPL as IPL_DEV.n64 and then asks for its Dezaemon 3D cartridge. Not run: F-Zero X Expansion Kit and Dezaemon DD with their cartridges (no Japanese F-Zero X or Dezaemon 3D dump in the test set), Mario Artist's other disks, Randnet (needs the online service). Open: Paint Studio's menu dipped to 44-57 fps on the Flip on its second start; per-start save backups skip disk saves (64.9 MB a copy: Joey's call); the launcher side (scan, list, box art, IPL import by md5, cartridge pairing) is the Frontend session's; the N64 Mouse is in the accessories item below. BIOS location (Joey via the Frontend session): the data folder's `bios/`, so a picked folder, moves and backups carry it; app 5eeece8 (BiosSync: files/system/Mupen64plus follows bios/ before a disk starts; flip-dd3 checked both ways). **Open: disk games run slow inside the emulated machine:** Kyojin no Doshin and Pro Golf draw ~15 fps, SM64 Disk Version 1-8 fps, with a quarter of their audio (AI DMAs every 4th VI, ~780 underruns per 5 s; SM64 cartridge at the same 32006 Hz rate gets full audio), while the Flip runs 61 VI/s at 1-4 ms each, so the games wait on something emulated (first suspect: the core's 64DD seek/read timing); Paint Studio's menu runs 50-60. **Compared 2026-11-07** (Joey: "Did you research how other emulators handle dd"): ares times seeks and sectors as our core does (5.3 ms a seek + 0.1 ms a track, ~0.2 ms a sector; only its 1 s spin-up is longer than our 10 ms) and Project64 answers seeks at once by default, so disk timing is not the cause. Project64's database gives the Doshin cartridge conversions Counter Factor 1; disks get no database settings in our core (CountPerOp 2). Flip flip-dd4, `mupen64plus-CountPerOp=1`: Doshin 13 -> 20 fps, SM64 Disk Version 7 -> 15, Pro Golf's menu 60 (15 in play either way), underruns only 780 -> 620-730, so it is part of it, not all: the audio is still mostly missing. IPLs: Project64, RMG and ares keep Japan/USA/development IPLs and pick by the disk's region, as we do (ares checks each by sha256). Disk saves: ares and we keep the whole disk; Project64 offers Full Disk Copy or Save Area Only; upstream mupen64plus defaults to the RAM area only (`.ram`, 1.9-38.5 MB by disk type; our core has that code, switched off). Mouse: ares has a Mouse device per port. **Fixed 2026-11-07 (Joey: "Yes, dig in"):** not a 64DD problem. Debug counters (env N64_DD_STATS, kept as scratchpad/dd-debug-counters.patch) showed SM64 Disk Version taking one VI interrupt in four with no disk traffic at all, the CPU 60% in its idle loop, and `two events of type 0x1` in the log: our VI handler (since the display-lag change 65f2d76) removed the queue head assuming it was the VI event, but with the IPL's VI registers (V_START <= V_INTR) the new scanout event has no delay and sorts first, so the scanout was dropped, the VI kept and a second one added, for good. Core `dd` 7a8c4ba removes the VI event by type (also on drc-tier e3cbb3f and gliden64-update 6e3e944; any cartridge game whose VI registers do the same was hit too). Flip flip-dd11: SM64 Disk Version 30 fps 0 underruns (was 1-8 fps, ~780), Pro Golf 58-60, Paint Studio 60, Communication Kit 60, Talent and Polygon Studio 30, Chibikko 60, SimCity 60 then 48-51 in one scene where the Flip itself is busy (11-12 ms a frame). Kyojin no Doshin then crashed while loading after Start (reads through a bad pointer, also on the cached interpreter): CountPerOp 1, as Project64 gives it, loads it (core 0ac9d17: both Doshin 1 disks in mupen64plus.ini by md5; flip-dd16 retail and kiosk demo 60 fps, 0 underruns). Cartridge compat with the fix: Flip `chain148` (`flip-dd6-compat`, app-dd6 = core ff8136) 82/82 ok, no `two events of type 0x1` in any log, no crash. Disk saves in the per-start save backups (Joey 2026-11-07: "Zipped whole disk"; app dd 8e33d85): `<save name> <stamp>.ndd.disk_save.zip`, unzip into saves/ to restore; Flip `dd12` (SimCity 64): 2.1 s at start when the save changed, 19.9 MB a copy, unchanged save skipped, unzipped bytes identical. Core for the app: cores/core-dd3.so (ff8136 = dd 0ac9d17), APK app-dd6

Also Game Boy Camera through the Transfer Pak. Low priority: a handful of games **N64 Mouse for Mario Artist (2026-11-07, Joey via the Frontend session: "add it to the accessories item"), after 64DD disks boot:** checked in the core (dd branch): mupen64plus has the device (`g_mouse_controller_flavor`, game_controller.c) but nothing picks it: main.c hard-codes the standard controller for every port ("FIXME ... mouse"), and the libretro layer passes no mouse in. Core change (needs Joey's go): let a port be an N64 Mouse and take relative X/Y and two buttons from the host. Host: a stick as the mouse (relative motion, speed by tilt; two buttons as left/right click), per game for Mario Artist or as a player's controller choice; touch as the mouse is worth trying too (a paint program; on the Thor the bottom screen could be the pad). Any Controls options for it are a menu for Joey to design

- [x] RK3566 handheld report (2026-11-09, Joey sent `Z:\joey64-report-AOSP_TV_on_ARM64-20261109-0919.tgz`; kit 2026-11-08 17:28, apk c7fa94db, core fa53f5fc; unpacked in the scratchpad report-aosptv/): Rockchip RK3566, 4x Cortex-A55 at 1.99 GHz, Mali-G52 r1p0 with 1 core at 800 MHz (the Flip, a Unisoc T618, has 2 cores and big A75s), 2 GB RAM, 640x480 60 Hz screen, LineageOS 20 TV GSI with Daijishou (GammaOS-style), Mali driver g15p0 (Vulkan 1.3). The kit ran every step this time, screenshots work, no crash, no error lines. Uncapped from the bench states, emu fps: SM64 parallel-RDP 44-47 (capped 47, 80% speed), GLideN64 84 (131%); OoT 57 / 65; Conker 27 / 44 (cold-cache stall 6.9 s on parallel-RDP); parallel-RDP alone: DK64 39, Cruis'n USA 47, Smash 51, MK64 54, Paper Mario 56, Perfect Dark 38, Yoshi's Story 38; 2x upscaling 14-20; malisx2 0.4.0 33-34; PanVK (fristonerr 370) 13-17 with 20 s stalls. Every screenshot draws the game right on both renderers. Work per frame, SM64: parallel-RDP 21 ms, GLideN64 12 ms; the profile (prof/2-profile) has 39% of the CPU in libGLES_mali.so (the Vulkan driver's own CPU side) and 27% in the core: on four A55s the driver's per-submit cost is what sinks parallel-RDP. Underruns (~556 a window in every run, GLideN64 too) are the bench dropping audio, not a fault. So on this device no game holds full speed on the default (stock Mali -> parallel-RDP) and GLideN64 does in SM64 and OoT. Proposed: a GPU rule picking GLideN64 on the RK3566 (how the app tells it apart is not settled: ro.board.platform rk356x, RAM under 2.5 GB, or the Mali sysfs gpuinfo "1 cores" if the app can read it; the RG DS Plus below is RK3566 too). **Built 2026-11-09 (Joey: "Yes, add the rule"):** `GpuRules.autoRenderer` picks GLideN64 on stock Mali when every core in /proc/cpuinfo is a little one (CPU part A32/A35/A53/A55: `GpuRules.littleCoresOnly`), logged as "stock Mali, only little CPU cores (A53/A55): GLideN64"; keyed on the CPU because this build reports neither ro.soc.model nor a usable board name. A dev driver still gets parallel-RDP, and Settings › Video › Renderer still wins. Our devices' cpuinfo (read 2026-11-09): Flip 6x A55 + 2x A75 (0xd0a), so it keeps parallel-RDP; Duo, Portal and Odin 3 are Adreno, which the rule does not touch. GpuRulesTest pins the RK3566 and Flip cases; Duo check (`duo-rkrule`, app-main-rk): game ok, rule line unchanged (Adreno 722, custom driver: parallel-RDP). Not checked on an RK3566 itself: the next report from that tester shows the rule line in its app.log

- [ ] Sleep test (2026-11-08, Joey: "Add proper sleep as a test to our queue, so games don't crash if left open when android sleeps"): scratchpad compat `sleeptest.sh` + `devsleep.sh`. Start a game, let it run 20 s, KEYCODE_SLEEP with the game open, stay asleep, wake + dismiss keyguard, twice. Pass = same process (not restarted), Joey64 in front, stats lines resume, last stats >= 45 emu fps, no crash in logcat; shots a (before) / b / c (after each wake) checked by eye. Three runs per device, queued after each device's chain (`next3b-sleep-2026-11-08.sh dev=<id>`: a waiting chain must not look like `*.sh <device>`, or devclean.sh stops it): `<dev>-sleep1` 6 games (SM64, OoT, GoldenEye, Banjo, Smash, Conker) default renderer 60 s; `<dev>-sleep2` SM64 + OoT 10 min with deep Doze forced (`dumpsys deviceidle force-idle`); `<dev>-sleep3` SM64 + OoT on GLideN64 60 s. Duo, Flip, Portal, Odin 3 (not the Thor). Results: **Duo first run (`duo-sleep1..3`, app-spfix, 2026-11-08 22:30-23:20):** every game read "nofocus" at its first wake, a test bug: the check grepped ".MainActivity" (Android prints the full class name) and the Duo lists one focus line per display; the second line was Joey64 each time. What it did show: all 10 runs (6 games 60 s, SM64/OoT 10 min with Doze forced, SM64/OoT GLideN64) kept the same process through the sleep, Joey64 in front after the wake, stats paused while asleep and resumed after (58.5-60.1 emu fps), no crash or tombstone in any log. The second cycle never ran, so devsleep.sh is fixed (full class name) and the Duo runs it again as `duo-sleepb1..3` after its S&P play run; the other devices get the fixed script. **Did it sleep?** (Joey: "how do we know it went to sleep?", then "Only add the checks"): not proven. Read 2026-11-08: Doze is off on the Duo, Portal and Odin 3 (mDeepEnabled=false, the vendor's setting), so "force-idle" did nothing there (its error was hidden); kernel suspend count 0 since boot on the Duo and Odin 3 (on AC with adb, never suspended), 428 on the Portal; Doze on only on the Flip. So the Duo run proved screen off + app paused + clean resume, not deep sleep. devsleep.sh now writes `<game>.sleep` per cycle (screen state, Doze deep/light, suspend count, AC/USB power, before / screen-off / end-of-sleep / awake, the force-idle reply, stats lines logged while asleep) and a pass is "ok" only if the kernel suspended or Doze went IDLE in both cycles, else "ok-awake(slept n/2)". Not added (Joey chose checks only): longer sleeps, an unplugged overnight run, a simulated unplug for Doze. **With the checks (overnight 2026-11-08/09):** Duo `duo-sleepb1` 6/6 and Odin 3 `odin3-sleep1` 6/6 "ok-awake(slept 0/2)" at 59.9-60.2 fps: the proof shows screen off (mWakefulness Dozing), 0 stats lines while asleep (paused), Doze ACTIVE throughout and 0 kernel suspends, on AC: clean pause and resume, no real sleep on these two, as expected. **Duo complete (`duo-sleepb1..3`, app-spfix, done 2026-11-09 ~00:00): 10/10 ok-awake**, both cycles each: 6 games 60 s, SM64/OoT 10 min ("force-idle: Unable to go deep idle; not enabled", Doze off on the Duo), SM64/OoT on GLideN64 60 s; 59.3-60.1 emu fps after the wake, same process, Joey64 in front, 0 stats lines while asleep, no crash/tombstone in any log; shots after each wake drawn right with the game moved on (SM64 title to its demo, OoT title). Not a deep-sleep result: the Duo on AC never suspended. **Portal `portal-sleep1` (6 games, 60 s, app-ship2, done 2026-11-09 ~01:20): 6/6 ok-awake (slept 0/2)**: 59.9-60.0 emu fps after wake, every shot a live game, 0 stats lines while asleep, mWakefulness Dozing, Doze not enabled, on USB power the suspend count stayed at 428 (no suspend), no crash. **Flip `flip-sleep1` (6 games, 60 s, app-ship3, done 2026-11-09 ~01:00 device time): 6/6 ok-awake (slept 0/2)**: 59.9-60.0 emu fps after wake, 0 stats lines while asleep, mWakefulness Asleep; Doze stayed ACTIVE within 60 s and `suspend_stats/success` reads 0 on the Flip (no counter there), so the 600 s runs with forced idle decide whether it slept; every shot drawn except Conker's first (bug (a)). **Flip complete (`flip-sleep2` 600 s + forced idle, `flip-sleep3` GLideN64, app-ship3, done 2026-11-09 03:04): the Flip really slept: `flip-sleep2` 4/4 cycles "Now forced in to deep idle mode" and Doze IDLE at the end of each 10 min sleep (status ok, not ok-awake), SM64 and OoT 60.0 / 60.1 emu fps after every wake, 0 stats lines while asleep, every shot a live game, no crash; `flip-sleep3` 4/4 ok-awake. So a game left open survives real deep sleep (Doze) on the Flip; the other three have Doze turned off.** **Portal complete (`portal-sleep2` 600 s idle, `portal-sleep3` GLideN64, done 2026-11-09 02:19): 10/10 ok-awake**, SM64 and OoT at 60.0 emu fps after every wake, every shot a live game (attract demos running), 0 stats lines while asleep in all 8 cycles, suspends still 428. Duo, Odin 3 and Portal all 10/10; none of them suspended (on power, Doze disabled); the Flip (the one with Doze) runs now. **Odin 3 complete (`odin3-sleep1..3`, app-ship2, done 2026-11-09 ~00:30): 10/10 ok-awake**, the same picture: 59.9-60.2 emu fps after every wake, 0 stats lines while asleep in all 20 cycles, force-idle "not enabled", 0 suspends on AC, no crash in any log.
- [ ] Open bugs never looked into: device queue (2026-11-08, Joey asked "Is there any other open bugs we have not looked into yet or missed?", then "All of them"). Each device takes its next one as its current job ends, after its ship-vk12-pc compat (core 5cb74cb1, app-ship2). **Duo ship2 compat done 2026-11-08 22:12 (`duo-ship2-compat`): 82/82 ok**, no game ends black where gpur1 drew except War Zone 75; dark last shots 23, 36, 58, 65 as in every run; last shots match duo-vk12-compat (largest difference 6.9, game 70). War Zone on the Duo: its last shot has been black in about half the Duo's compat runs since 11-03 (duo-smart, dup, jit2, gpu8c, gpu2pn, faux127compat, drcthread3-compat2, vk12, ship2) on every build, and the menu in the rest, while emulation keeps running (60 emu fps, 30 presented): timing, not a new break; item (e) War Zone rerun with later shots decides it. **Odin 3 done 2026-11-08 23:17:** War Zone with later shots (`odin3-warzone`, compat64w) ok, menu drawn in all five shots (brightness 24-30), as on every Odin 3 run; ship2 compat (`odin3-ship2-compat`) **82/82 ok**, none ends black where a reference draws, dark 23/36/58/65 as always, last shots match odin3-merge1b (largest difference 5.6, game 16). Left: Flip and Portal ship2 compat, Portal War Zone. **Flip: ship2 started no game (found 2026-11-08 23:28, overnight tick):** every Flip run on app-ship2 (`flip-conk-def/def81/sync/sync81`, `flip-ship2-compat` 1-16) read nofocus, the log ending "the emulator stopped (11): the game could not start" after Granite's "Vulkan loader does not support required Vulkan version": the vk12 change asks for a 1.2 instance, the Flip's loader is 1.1 (stock Mali-G52, API 1.1.149); a 1.1 loader accepts the 1.2 apiVersion, but Granite compares the loader version with the app info and refused, so the core's create_device failed. The Flip was the one device without a vk12 compat run. Fix: core d0f72a4 on ship-vk12-pc caps the version given to Granite at the loader's (also on sp-dynarec as 2ef8b55); core 045d8475 = app-ship3 (main + that core; jniLibs back to 16298e1d), S&P core 8882eadb = app-spfix2. **Flip ship3 compat 82/82 ok (`flip-ship3-compat`, done 2026-11-09 ~01:38): the regression is fixed**; blackcheck flags none; the biggest shotcmp difference, Mario Tennis (32, 42.1), is timing only (court fly-in and Player Select vs the Odin 3's Play Mode), every other game draws its normal screens except Conker (81, bug (a), dark intro as before the fix). Flip check (`flip-ship3-check`, SM64): ok, Granite on Mali-G52 API 1.1.149, plays. The void Flip runs were stopped; the Flip list runs again on app-ship3 (`next9-flip-2026-11-09.sh`: `flip-ship3-compat`, `flip-conk3-*`, `flip-drvmalisx2041b`, then its sleep test). Duo/Odin 3/Portal ship2 results stand (loaders 1.2+, where the cap changes nothing). The bundle candidate is now ship3 (+ spfix2 once S&P is settled). **Odin 3 Portal (7b49726d, Adreno 830, new 2026-11-09) first compat 82/82 ok** (`o3p-main-compat`, next52, main d3ac371 + bundled jcache-sp core c0a3963 md5 71494897, Auto renderer and driver, 1x); blackcheck: 0 of 82 end black where the Portal and Odin 3 ship2 runs draw. **Portal done 2026-11-08 23:42:** ship2 compat (`portal-ship2-compat`) **82/82 ok**, none ends black where a reference draws; the largest last-shot differences from portal-vk12-compat (32 Mario Tennis, 5 Body Harvest, 73 WCW Nitro) are other moments, all drawn right. **(e) War Zone settled** (`portal-warzone`, compat64w): shots a/b menu, c black (0.0), d and e menu again (24.9, 25.5): the black last shot is a black moment between attract screens that compat64b's shot c sometimes lands on, not a hang; same build drew it on the Odin 3 and Flip. Nothing to fix. **Flip:** (a) Conker garbage, default vs synchronous (compat64b ENVX/AMX, games 7 and 81) **[settled 2026-11-09, not a bug: nothing to fix]** Time series on the Flip (scratchpad compat `runseq.sh` + `devseq.sh`, a shot every 3 s for 45-75 s, with and without compat64b's keys; `flip-conkq1-def`, `flip-conkq1-s85`, `flip-conkq3-defk` x2): at full speed with the default renderer the whole intro draws right (mature-audiences card, Dolby, Nintendo logo, N64 logo, Conker, Rareware squirrel, the town), with short black gaps between scenes. compat64b's shots a and b land on those gaps at full speed (a at about 20 s = the gap after the Nintendo logo; b after five key presses whose focus checks push it to about 48 s = the gap after the squirrel). Anything slower (N64_SLOW_PCT, ubershaders, GPU spin, sync-every) moves the scenes a few seconds later, so the same shots hit a drawn scene: that is why every slowdown looked like a fix.
