With the cxd4 RSP instead of the parallel RSP (`duo-lis-cxd4`, `odin3-lis-cxd4`, `--es opts mupen64plus-rsp-plugin=cxd4`) it cuts the same. **So every LLE setup cuts it** (two LLE RSPs, parallel-RDP on Turnip, Mali and the Qualcomm driver, and GLideN64 in LLE mode) **and only HLE draws it whole.** The far half of the pad is under the floor where the floor bulges up to it (its far yellow edge still shows at Mario's waist), and the sign text likewise: near-equal depths that the hack's own microcode, run as written, resolves the other way from HLE's float maths. parallel-RDP draws what the RDP commands say, as GLideN64's LLE path does, so there is nothing to fix in parallel-RDP: the cut is most likely how the hack looks on a console (Last Impact was made on HLE emulators; a tasvideos post says it was never designed for console and accurate plugins garble it, not checked on a console here) **parallel-n64's HLE geometry, 2026-11-09 09:01-09:08 (Joey: "can last impact not be fixed on parallel?", then "Yes, try it"; Portal, `next41`/`next42`):** the libretro/parallel-n64 core (app-pn64core, master list item 9) into parallel-RDP, with its HLE RSP (graphics tasks go to its F3DEX2/S2DEX display-list walker, `rdp_emit_hle.c`, which turns them into RDP commands for parallel-RDP: the code item 9 would port) and with its LLE RSP. On the stock parallel-n64 both were black from boot (`portal-li-pn-hle/-lle`: it aligns PI DMA, the Last Impact crash every upstream build has); with our bit-0 rule patched into its pi_controller.c (scratchpad app-pn64pi, test build only) both reach the file-select screen at 60 fps and **both cut the pad and sign exactly as our LLE runs do** (`portal-li-pnpi-hle`, `-lle`: the purple/yellow line at Mario's waist, the far half under the floor). So an HLE that models the microcode's vertex rounding cuts it too; only GLideN64's HLE (float maths) draws it whole. **Porting item 9 would not fix Last Impact**, and nothing on the parallel-RDP side can: the whole picture needs GLideN64 + HLE for this game, or it stays as the microcode draws it. Joey's choice. **Joey 2026-11-09: "GLideN64 for it". Built:** `GpuRules.gameRenderer` (by ROM MD5, 70059d4d...) gives Auto GLideN64, and with it the HLE RSP, for SM64 Last Impact wherever the GPU rule gave parallel-RDP; Settings › Video › Renderer and `--es renderer` still win; MainActivity's `earlyMd5` (the launcher's EXTRA_MD5, else a handed-over or dev ROM hashed) and the log's `GPU rules: ...; this game: SM64 Last Impact: ...`. Portal (app-lirule, `portal-lirule-43/-53`, defaults): Last Impact on GLideN64 + HLE at 60 fps with the pad and SELECT A FILE sign whole; SM64 stays on parallel-RDP. Left for Joey: per-game GLideN64 + HLE for Last Impact (as FZ, whole), or leave parallel-RDP (accurate, cut)]; **Duo:** (l) Banjo-Tooie music problem (reproduce first: what is wrong) [**what it is, 2026-11-09 03:27 (no device time):** the M6 list took it from mupen64plus-core issue 1069: in Jolly Roger Lagoon, warping from Big Fish Cavern or Locker's Cavern back to Town Center leaves the music silent until the next loading zone, with parallel-RDP in RetroArch and not with GLideN64 (a similar report: DK64 bonus-stage music starting 10-15 s late). In RetroArch parallel-RDP runs every RSP task, audio included, on the LLE parallel RSP, while GLideN64 runs audio HLE; Joey64 already runs audio HLE with parallel-RDP by default (core bd3cd23, `N64_HLE_AUDIO`), the setup the reporter says plays fine. So we are likely not affected, but it cannot be checked without a save in Jolly Roger Lagoon with those warp pads (none in the bench set or on Z:; scripting there from boot is not practical). Needed a Banjo-Tooie save or state in Jolly Roger Lagoon (**Joey has none, 2026-11-10: parked**), or a try by hand (warp Big Fish Cavern to Town Center, default settings, then with `N64_HLE_AUDIO=0` to see the RetroArch fault). **Joey 2026-11-09: "I will do" (by hand).**]; (m) [**read from the ship2/ship3 compat logs 2026-11-09, no device time:** leaving out the first two 5 s windows of each game and Super Mario 64 Elise 54 (frozen title: no audio, ~290 underruns a window, item j), underruns per window with no repeated frames / with half repeated: Duo 0.21 / 1.60 (in 1% / 5% of windows), Odin 3 0.02 / 1.15, Portal 0.08 / 0.89, Flip 2.33 / 1.43; next worst Pokemon Puzzle League 39 (~250 in its run). Repeated frames go with a little more underrun, but nothing like the T820's 150-310 a window: not reproduced on our four; the T820 side stays a report-kit question] audio underruns while half the frames repeat (T820 helper report 19:24-19:44: Bust-A-Move '99 838 underruns in 20 s, Army Men Sarge's Heroes 2 65 and a 143 ms frame; the T820 windows with ~30 of 60 frames repeated have 150-310 underruns, the ones without 0-9; on the Flip compat logs both kinds of window have them, so not settled) on the Flip and Duo. **Core, any device:** (n) Sin and Punishment: find the dynarec commit that broke it (bisect upstream's 3.0 update), fix, then drop its interpreter setting.
- [ ] Play Store release (2026-11-09, Joey asked what is needed; parked, Joey: "Not now"). Code blockers found: targetSdk 34 (Play needs 36 since 2026-08-31); the core, libjoey64host and the adrenotools hooks are 4 KB-aligned (Play needs 16 KB: -Wl,-z,max-page-size=16384, then retest; bundled Turnips are already 16 KB); the in-app updater and REQUEST_INSTALL_PACKAGES need a Play flavour without them; AAB. Never fetch drivers from the app. Joey's side: developer account (personal: closed test, 12 testers, 14 days), Play App Signing with our own key so Play and sideloaded builds share a signature, privacy policy + Data safety (reports, RA sign-in, ScreenScraper hashes), content rating, public GPL source, no Nintendo marks or art in the listing; developer verification also affects sideloading
- [ ] Our own offline RetroAchievements, like SeedlessDS and WatermelonDS have (Joey 2026-11-08: "add ... to the queue at the back"): earn achievements with no connection and send the unlocks to RetroAchievements later. First look at how those two do it (and Joey's RAOfflineProxy), then build on the Frontend session's RetroAchievements work on launcher/phase0, which already has an offline unlock queue (a real unlock was queued and sent, Thor 2026-11-08, c2d11f6 notes): the gap is starting and playing a game's set with no connection at all
- [ ] Raphnet N64/GC-to-USB adapters (Joey 2026-11-08: "add raphnet adapter to back of queue"): button maps are already in docs/n64-controllers.md (v3 289b:0063 family, v2 289b:0004) and Settings › Controls lists "Raphnet: test first" (docs/settings-gap-audit.md item 10); needs the adapter in hand first. Then look at raw mode like mupen64plus's raphnetraw input plugin, which sends real N64 controller commands through the adapter (the real Controller Pak, Rumble Pak and Transfer Pak), over Android USB host
- [ ] Analogue 3D features to look into (Joey 2026-11-08: "Add analogue 3d features to look into it, back of the queue"). From their firmware notes and press (read 2026-11-08, up to firmware 1.5.1, 2026-09); for each: what it does, what Joey64 has, worth copying? Menus stay Joey's design. (1) **Original Display Modes**: CRT recreations (PVM, BVM, consumer TV...) with beam convergence (consumer / professional / off), edge overshoot (can be turned off), edge hardness, size/fit; ours: Picture with librashader and the user's own RetroArch presets, so the question is a small set of tuned presets with those few knobs. (2) **Overclock tiers**: Enhanced (RAM), Enhanced+ (RAM + GPU), Unleashed (+ CPU), aimed at CPU-heavy slowdown (GoldenEye, Perfect Dark; 1.1.9 reworked it), switchable in-game since 1.5.1; ours: Emulation tab Overclock Auto/Normal/2x/4x (CPU) and the smoother CountPerOp list; look at an RDRAM/RDP-timing tier and an in-game switch. (3) **Memories** (save states, 1.3.0): 20 per game, instant quick-load (1.5.0), start the console straight into the most recent Memory; ours: states + undo; the resume-on-launch idea is the new part. (4) **Internal Graphical Upscaling** (1.5.0): doubles horizontal detail in the RDP/VI while keeping the original timing; compare with parallel-RDP upscaling, a 2x-horizontal-only mode could be a cheaper step for weak GPUs (Flip, Duo at 2x). (5) **Force progressive output** (2026-02: interlaced games shown progressive). (6) **Force Original Hardware** switch, in-game since 1.5.1: one toggle that turns every enhancement off. (7) **Library**: header detection for variant carts (1.2.4), customise unknown carts (1.5.1), VRU support (Hey You, Pikachu! / Densha de Go!, 1.5.1). (8) **Controllers**: Switch Online N64 pad (2026-02), 8BitDo 64 and the 8BitDo N64 Modkit (C buttons, USB rumble). Sources: analogue.co/support/3d/firmware/1.5.0, timeextension.com (1.1.9, 2026-02, 2026-05, 1.5.0, 1.5.1 articles), theshortcut.com (save states).
